*The builder's readings, Michael's to overrule:* the labels read "Main hand (right)" and "Off-hand (left)", swapped for the left-handed; the doll's left column is the character's right hand (the doll faces you), so a left-handed character's main-hand slot stands in its right column; the figure turns the arms round in the rig (`pose`), not the sprite, so the face, the hair's parting, and the garments are never mirrored, and the walk's free arm keeps swinging with the opposite leg; the dead and villagers are right-handed; the identity screen's turning figure is empty-handed, so it looks the same either way.

Docs: 12, 21, 32, `src/CLAUDE.md`, testing.

### 2026-10-25 — The belt's slots have roles: a main hand, an off hand, and one utility slot; an empty slot's tap does nothing; the key order a setting; the slots stamped (Michael)

The belt's own discussion (pinned earlier the same evening, The evening's rulings), in session:

> "You get 3 slots to start, differentiated by handedness or no. Slot 1: main hand, slot 2: off hand, slot 3: utility … A hotkey tapped on a utility slot consumes if possible, else does nothing. This is a freebie and the one and only you ever get. It can hold utility items like a torch, extra weapons you don't want to put in your bag (understanding that tapping this key does not equip them) as pure storage expansion, consumable items like a potion or a food. Any waterskin put in a slot will auto drink as if from bag … The one belt upgrade remains and adds one more slot for each hand"

> "Any two handed item can go in the utility slot as a 1x1 that doesn't equip. Any two handed item can go in a main hand slot and tapping it will equip in both, a two handed item will be refused in every offhand slot. Applies to the bow as well."

> "every expected slot action that fails should give you a log line"

> "don't let a tap on an empty hand slot move the hand item from your actual hand to the belt, because it means a tap on an empty hand belt slot in combat disarms you"

> "I would not let you manually drink from a waterskin on tap"

On the key order: "your 1/2/3 never changes version should be the default". On the look: "keep the look we currently have, but stamp the empty gray background of each slot with a hand icon or utility icon and when empty and hovered it should tell you 'This is a main hand slot. It can ...' etc."

- **Every belt slot has a role.** The rope belt gives three: a main-hand slot, an off-hand slot, and the utility slot. The leather belt gives five, one more main-hand and one more off-hand slot. **The utility slot never expands:** nothing in scope adds a second (an idea for a legendary drop someday, `05`).
- **A main-hand slot's key** draws its item into the main hand if it can be held there, swapping what the main hand held onto that slot; else eats it if it is food; else does nothing, with a line saying why ("The wooden shield can't be held in your main hand."). **An off-hand slot's key** is the same for the off hand.
- **A tap on an empty hand slot does nothing at all, and says nothing:** it never moves what is in your hand to the belt, so a stray key never disarms you.
- **The utility slot's key** eats a food, else does nothing with a line ("You can't equip a weapon from the utility slot."). It holds anything that can go on the belt: a torch, food, a potion, or extra weapons as pure storage, **any held item in one cell there, whatever its bag shape**.
- **Two-handed items** (the halberd, the bow, anything held in both hands): in the utility slot a single cell of storage, never equipped; in a main-hand slot its key draws it into both hands, the off hand's thing going to an empty off-hand slot, else the bag, else the feet; **refused at placement in every off-hand slot**, with a line. The halberd may go on the belt again.
- **A wrong-hand item** (a shield in a main-hand slot) may be placed there as storage; its key does nothing and says why. **Every expected slot action that fails gives a line.**
- **A waterskin** goes in any slot and sips by itself as from the bag; **a tap never drinks**, and says "Your waterskin drinks by itself from your belt." (the item's own name).
- **A light** (the torch, a lantern later) lights from the belt in any slot, as before; a hand slot's key draws it into that hand, the utility slot's does nothing, with a line.
- **No hand chosen per slot:** the slot's menu loses Equip to main hand and Equip to off hand and their mark, and the player keeps no choice (#467's `beltHands`, gone from the save, the wire, and the sync check; an old world's is dropped, since worlds are expendable).
- **The key order is a setting of each player's own, "Belt slot order":** client display only, mapping the number keys and the places on screen to the slots' fixed roles; the client turns the key into the slot's role before it goes into the snapshot, so the host, the other players, and the save never see it. **Sequential is the default:** the rope belt 1 main, 2 off, 3 utility, the leather belt appending 4 main and 5 off, so the first three keys never change. **Hands grouped:** 1 and 2 main, 3 and 4 off, 5 utility; on the rope belt the keys are 1, 3, and 5, with 2 and 4 drawn as locked places.
- **The look as it is,** each empty slot's grey stamped faint with its role: a main-hand icon, an off-hand icon (the same hand mirrored), and a utility icon (a pouch). Hovering an empty slot shows a card saying what it is for ("This is a main-hand slot. Its key draws what's here into your main hand, or eats it if it is food."), two-handed things where they may go included; a filled slot's card leads with what its key does there ("From this main-hand slot: drawn into your main hand.").
- Overturns, struck where they sit: the hand chosen per slot in its menu (2026-10-19, A belt slot's key does one thing, into the hand chosen for that slot; no left-click parity), and the halberd never on the belt (2026-10-19, The belt restated and added to). No left-click parity stands.
- *Primary's readings (the builder's, where the ruling was silent; Michael's to overrule):* an empty utility slot's tap does nothing and says nothing, as an empty hand slot's; a number with no slot under the order sends nothing, and a locked place's key (a grouped rope belt's 2 and 4) says "A leather belt adds a slot here." and any belt key with no belt worn "You have no belt.", each on that player's own screen alone, never sent, while a number past a Sequential rope belt's three, with no place drawn, stays silent; the slot's right-click menu keeps Drink for a skin (a deliberate choice, never a tap), and a double-click is the key's path (Equip or Eat, else the key's line); the slot's menu offers a held thing the key does not draw (anything in the utility slot, a wrong-hand thing in a hand slot) an Equip into its own hand, as from the bag, since storage in the bag keeps its Equip: only the key never equips from the utility slot; what a draw pushes out of a hand goes to an empty slot of its own role, else the bag, else the empty utility slot, else the feet (the off hand's thing under a two-hander's draw, and the two-hander under a draw into the off hand), so a free utility slot comes before the floor; a one-handed weapon put away with the bag full tries the main-hand slots, then the utility slot, never an off-hand slot, so a swap never sets up a dual wield; Take All fills any empty belt slot whose role takes the thing; a weapon in the utility slot is a blow, a bow, a throw, a shield, or a staff, and a torch, a book, or a tool there is named in the line ("You can't equip the torch from the utility slot."); the role icons are one hand drawn right and mirrored left, a main-hand slot showing the hand a character favours (`player.handed`, right when unset; the handedness choice is its own build).

### 2026-10-25 — The evening's rulings: the belt pinned, Mageroot, potions to a sitting of their own, no plant only at night, pemmican as it is, eggs one a cell (Michael)

Answering what the evening's builds still left open, Michael ruled:

- **The belt waits for its own discussion** (held the same evening, 2026-10-25, The belt's slots have roles): "Let's hold on the belt situation and make that its own discussion after we wrap up the rest of this stuff. So pin in the belt, and we come back to it before the sit downs." The belt's open defaults (#467: the hand choice kept per kind of item in a slot, Equip to a hand also drawing, an empty off-hand slot taking the off hand's item, the two Equip spellings) stay as built and open, and get a discussion of their own before the sittings ahead.
- **The names:** "Mana herb is Mageroot for now, keep grave moss." The item `mana_herb` is named Mageroot (its id, its node `herb_mana_herb`, and its map kind kept); grave moss is its name, no longer a placeholder.
- **Potions get a sitting of their own:** "we have not yet explicitely talked about which herbs and in what quantities go into potions, how we handle making them (do you need vials, water?). This may need its own sit, I want to make sure this system is built thoughtfully." Open: which herbs and how many go into each potion, and what making one takes (vials, water); it joins the sittings ahead.
- **No plant is night's alone:** "I don't want any 'only at night' plants, that is an old idea. If those skill lines are stale, strike them and we can talk about potential replacements." Grave moss is gathered and grows back at any hour, the `night` field gone from the data and the code (the validator refuses it). Struck in `docs/39`: Herbalism 7 ("The graveyard's herbs and fungi, planted only at night") and Herbalism 6 ("Cuttings and seeds from herbs you can pick, for the herb bed", stale since herbs grow wild, never in patches, 2026-10-25, Natural resources ruled). Open: their replacements, and what the herb bed (Farming 7) grows without Herbalism 6. Grave moss stays recorded at Herbalism 7 until then.
- **Pemmican as it is:** "The current pemican health and hunger seems just right" It heals 3 and fills 35 hunger, stacking to twenty.
- **Eggs do not stack:** "Let's make eggs not stack, but I would be open to changing that after playtesting." An egg is one a cell, so a coop's grid of six (3 by 2) holds six eggs and feathers between them, and a full coop takes no more until it is emptied.

### 2026-10-25 — The evening's defaults ruled: a birth may appear in its pen, the rocks rise with a shudder, the rest as built (Michael)

Shown the defaults the evening's builds left for him (`TODO.md`'s Status, each named in its pull request's Risks), Michael answered two and accepted the rest. The rulings:

- **A newborn animal may appear in view in its pen** (Michael: "Definitely!"), confirming #460's default: a young one beside a parent, in view or not. The rule that nothing materialises in view was for the dead (2026-10-25, Natural resources ruled).
- **The rocks' rise: the frozen-zombie reading stands, with a shudder:** "a little shudder as they rise from the ground, as if the ground had shifted and forced them to the surface. Doesn't need a lore explanation, it's just give them a cinematic entrance." So a new rock, ore rock, or salt rock rises whole through the ground line over `riseSeconds`, cut at the ground and never scaled, and is jolted sideways in whole pixels a few times as it comes, easing to still. *Primary's numbers* (`resources.shudderOf`): eight beats over the rise, about one in four still, the sides alternating; 2 px in the first sixteenth, then 1 px, none from about seven tenths of the rise on; the dust at its foot puffs up and out at the start and slows. From the sim clock and the prop's id, so every client draws the same shake from the host's `risenAt` and nothing new crosses the wire.
- **The rest stand as built:** "Anything not commented on is fine as-is." They are: a guest's own shot drawn about 67 ms later than before, on the bodies' clock (#463); the market's workbench, now too full round it to make a counter there (#466); every spell passing tame stock by, aimed at it or not (#456); a weaker weapon's yield by expected value, the whole part and one more at the chance of the rest (#456); milk as a jug, not the bucket's fill (#460); the smoking rack, the tanning vat, and the spinning wheel not carried (#462); the resources' counts (stone 40, ore 6, salt 4, clay 8, herbs 120 by kind, mushrooms 774); ~~grave moss gathered and grown back only at night~~ (overturned the same evening: The evening's rulings, above); mushrooms as forage, not food; an apple tree's sapling growing an apple tree (#461); and the millstones named "a pair of millstones" (#470).
- **Still open with him:** the belt's hand choice (#467), the two placeholder herb names ("Mana herb", "Grave moss"), and pemmican's heal of 3 (the names and pemmican ruled later the same evening, and the belt pinned for its own discussion: The evening's rulings, above).

### 2026-10-25 — Natural resources ruled: random spawns within defined areas, a steady count, rocks rising from the ground, no herb patches, trees from saplings (Michael; #288)

The seventh sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass. Michael reshaped Primary's spawn rules and adopted the rest; the spec is `docs/46`. The rulings:

- **One comeback rule for ore, stone, clay, herbs, and mushrooms:** "I want them to be random spawns but within defined areas. Stone everywhere, ore in certain areas, clay by the river, herbs all over in the ground. All the same in that once exhausted, a replacement will spawn somewhere eventually so unless you go on a resource binge all day the amount in the valley should stay roughly the same." Ore no longer comes back in place.
- **No herb patches:** "they are like weeds that grow randomly."
- **Appearing:** "As far as things popping into existence, that was mostly a zombie rule so they don't bum rush you unfairly. For rocks and ore, do the frozen zombie plan for that region - they pop up out of the ground with an animation. Mushrooms and herbs can definitely just appear." *Primary's reading:* a rock or ore rock grows up out of the earth over a second or so, in view or not.
- **Why:** "the main point I think is to make exploration more or less required for getting these types of resources vs. farming and animal keeping where you can put it in a defined spot."
- **Adopted from Primary's draft:** trees never regrow where felled (saplings at Woodcutting 4, planted, growing in about five game days); sticks gathered under a standing tree once a day ("Sticks under trees is great"); fish never run out ("Fish never run out is great"); a new node only on natural ground, never in a room, a fenced yard, a built floor, tilled soil, or under a prop.

It overturns the ore rock's comeback in place (2026-10-18, Mining, struck there) and narrows `src/CLAUDE.md`'s "nothing materialises in view" to everything but a resource's comeback.

### 2026-10-25 — Animals ruled: game mostly in the woods but free to roam, livestock from the farms, safe pens, generous feathers, raw meat in recipes, the companion last (Michael; #289)

The sixth sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass. Primary's defaults stood but for four of Michael's changes; the spec is `docs/45`. The rulings:

- **Seven kinds:** hens, rabbits, deer, cows, sheep, pigs and boar, with your own beehives.
- **Game mostly in the woods, never fenced in by a rule:** "let's be careful about restricting game to certain areas. It would be a problem if most of the deer were clumped in the city center ... but that also does not mean they can never leave the forest." Game comes back slowly to a cap per wood (*default*). Rabbits eating unfenced crops is an idea, not ruled ("Not sure if that is worth building").
- **Livestock comes from the farms,** led home on a lead at its Beastlore level; a pen is any fenced yard with its gates shut.
- **Deer flee, boar fight back when attacked; the weapon decides the yield; snares and the bow both hunt; skinning and butchering with a dirk.**
- **Penned animals never starve or die of neglect; fed animals produce, unfed ones stop;** breeding at Beastlore 6; **livestock is safe** (the dead ignore animals; no predators).
- **Feathers generous, so arrows can be farmed:** "If bow and arrow is supposed to be usable as a staple item, we should probably be a little more generous with feathers." *Defaults:* a fed hen a feather most days, a butchered hen three to five, one feather fletching five arrows.
- **Raw meat goes into recipes, and onto a fire as it is:** "I don't want a deer to yield only 'Cooked Deer Meat' to cooking, although throwing it directly in to cook should still be possible for the desperate or impatient."
- **The lead moves animals; the companion's command item waits:** "a lead does not make sense for sending an animal out to attack, unless it's REALLY long lol. Let's lead now for moving animals and wait on the beastlore attack item until we get there."
- **The companion is in 1.0, built last, after the demo,** with Michael's shape of 2026-10-19 (#289), logged here for the first time: three commands (follow, stay, attack a target and come back), the command item held in the main hand, a left click sending it, and his rule, "if any non-boss enemy is targeting you and a pet of any kind intercepts before they reach you they swap targets to the pet." *Primary's defaults:* bosses ignore interception; a companion that "dies" goes home hurt and is back after a game day. Open for when it is built: whether a companion counts as a target for the siege, and how the death school's thrall differs.
- **The art: drawn sprite sheets, not a second rig** (Primary's approach; Michael: "I like this overall"): the loader plays a sheet's named animations; four facings, two drawn and mirrored; dev art first; the brief points at the chicken coop and the beehive as guides, with the earlier briefs' detail ("your detailed descriptions for those briefs produced better work than I would have, so keep that up"); the style question (#426) settled before real art.
- **Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule** (`docs/45` marks each): herds of two to four; the farm stocking as `docs/39` places the buildings (hens, cows, pigs at Hollow Farm, sheep at the croft); a boar's charge; snares, the lead at Crafting 1, shears at Blacksmithing 1; hay, grain, or vegetables in a feeding trough; hens laying into their coop, whose placeholder table is emptied; the meatless pottage kept with a meat pottage beside it; livestock hurt only on purpose, shared in a shared world, wandering near when its pen opens; tracking as faint tracks; pens that give more as one more yield some days.

Docs: 45 (new), 39, 30, 42, 05, README.

### 2026-10-25 — Cheery at home, spooky beyond: Tirisfal is the outer regions' register, region by region; Habbo no longer the reference (Michael, clarifying)

Asked whether the valley should be brighter to match "start as Stardew" (#426), Michael: "Tirisfal was always for the outer regions. The idea was always the further you go from home the spookier it gets, we changed the interpretation to be more of a region than gradient change." So the valley is cheery and cosy, start-as-Stardew; the regions beyond are spooky, Tirisfal Glades for the darkest; the mood changes region by region, not as a gradient. It corrects how `docs/00`, `docs/06`, and 2026-10-09's "Dev art gets the game's visual identity now" read; how bright the valley is drawn, and Plants vs Zombies as a style reference, stay open on #426.

**Habbo is no longer the visual reference either.** Michael: "I think we have also evolved past Habbo as a visual reference. That and Tirisfal are ANCIENT ideas in the lifespan of this game ... Habbo doesn't have action combat with skillshots." The game is a cute isometric pixel-art village; the identity lines (`CLAUDE.md`, the README, `docs/00`, the style bible, the art briefs) drop the name. What stays are the drawing techniques it once named, the figure's flat side shading and the stepped shadows, which describe how the pixels are drawn today (`src/figure/raster.js`, `src/ground.js`, `docs/12`, `docs/15`), and the records under `docs/history`, never edited.

### 2026-10-25 — Water ruled: no fixed source is clean, boil with Use or either menu, a clean starting skin, Sick (Michael; #279)

The fifth sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass. Primary's defaults stood, with Michael's shape for boiling. The spec is `docs/44`. The rulings:

- **No fixed source is clean:** wells, the river, ponds, the trough, and the chapel's font. **Mixing taints** a clean skin topped up at a tainted source.
- **Boiling at a lit cooking fire (Cooking 1), three ways to one verb.** Michael: "Purifying water is a perfect spot for 'use' to shine. Click use on skin or bucket, click on fireplace or campfire, little loading bar like when you fill from well, and it boils the water to clean it. Can also be done from right click on either the waterskin/bucket (grey if not near fire) or fire (grey if no waterskin or bucket with tainted water in inventory)? Are we mixing too many interaction methods?" Primary's answer: no; right-click is the complete interface and Use its shortcut, all three one verb. About 30 seconds a skin (*default*); *Primary's default:* skins only until fluid transfer comes with the boiling pot, since a clean bucket has no use yet. Sanctify stays Faith 10, carried water only.
- **The card says which it is,** "(tainted)" stays on Drink, a brown drop marks a tainted icon; **the starting skin is full of clean water.**
- **The sickness is a debuff named Sick, and the infection's third stage is renamed Queasy** (Michael, reversing Primary's naming: "Sick is natural, queasy is pre-zombie!"): tiredness builds 50% faster, food heals 25% less, about half a game day; 20% a tainted drink, stacking to three (*Primary's default:* each stack adds half a day, the effects not growing); **1% a tainted drink of the infection**, which with no cure in the valley makes a tainted drink a desperate choice; the infection's chance joins the difficulty dial.
- **Filed for later:** the boiling pot, for large stews that restore water too and for boiling four skins at once, perhaps built into a fireplace to raise its cost ("I think this might be an eventual goal because I think it's a massive flavor win").

### 2026-10-25 — The dead ends ruled: fires and lights, Pick up and Take apart, made things, stations behind a switch, repairs that run out, a body burned, a wave, the cowering, the dye vat (Michael; #273)

The fourth sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass, over four rounds in chat. Michael overruled or reshaped much of Primary's draft; what he did not comment on stood as Primary's default, marked so below and in `docs/43`. The spec is `docs/43`. The rulings:

- **A lantern goes out.** Michael: "never goes out is an ANCIENT ruling, if light attracts zombies, they need to be snuffable." A lantern on a post or in a bracket can be put out and lit again with a tinderbox or a lit torch. It overturns 2026-10-17 (Torches burn down), struck where it sits.
- **"Put out" everywhere**, never "douse" (Primary's answer to "which is more universal?"): a lantern, a torch, a campfire, a fireplace.
- **Fixed home lights are switches; portable lights cost fuel.** Michael: lighting a house "is one of the least fun tasks I have done so far ... I had contemplated at more than one point just making this a permanent on/off switch." A fireplace, a hearth, and a lantern turn on and off and burn no fuel (turning one on still needs a flame, #168); a torch burns down and a campfire takes logs. *Primary's default:* the stations' fires (the oven, the forge, the clamp) keep their fuel, a station's fire being work, not comfort. It narrows `docs/30` law 4 and overturns 2026-10-09's open question 17 ("the hearth burns wood and goes out") and the hearth's line of 2026-10-09's Fires are data, struck where they sit.
- **The campfire:** built from the palette (three stone, two logs, outdoors, no skill), its two logs its starting fuel; six logs at most, three game hours each ("they take up SO MUCH inventory space"); put out; picked up only when out and empty ("to stop people from using them to do log inventory shenanigans"), as a Campfire item of 5 by 5 cells. It overturns 2026-10-11's "The campfire is fixed until Michael says otherwise".
- **Pick up and Take apart, for everything** (Michael: "I think it should, but there are a lot of candidates"): Pick up keeps a thing whole, into the bag or carried overhead (a container carried overhead keeps what it holds, as today); Take apart destroys it for half its materials, with its recipe's tool; Pick up into the bag and Take apart refused while it holds anything. *Primary's defaults:* only what a player made can be taken apart (keeping 2026-10-15, "X removes only what players made"); the build palette's Remove becomes Take apart, half back.
- **Made by default** (the loot sitting's rule; the recipes and numbers Primary's defaults): the hearth, wall brackets, torch posts, the cupboard, desk, bookcase, counter, chair, cart, stall, strongbox, and barrel, and the jack-o'-lantern carved from a pumpkin (a switch, as the home lights). **Place as decoration**, small in scope: an item naming a decoration prop may become it, food one way only; the pumpkin first; ground only.
- **The village's stations stay one of a kind, behind a switch:** "we need to BUILD it in such a way as to make this change more like flipping a switch than rebuilding anything ... Once built, I accept your defaults as the starting situation." A world rule, off by default, makes them movable and craftable.
- **Repairs run out:** "it would give you infinite repairs, meaning the item would truly never break ... That definitely can't happen." One repair count per item; the grindstone sharpens for free up to 60%, then 40%, then 20%, then never; the anvil repairs up to 100%, 80%, 60% on the same count; skills raise the ceilings, every item still decays to breaking.
- **Burn corpse** (Michael's): greyed out without a tinderbox and a log; the body burns about a game hour, giving light, then it and everything in it are gone, with a clear warning on its hover card. "Zomboid's disappear on a timer but I like this better."
- **Wave**, for the demo: "if I should be so lucky that someone mentions my game in a youtube video or something, that is the one they would want." A new pose, approved.
- **Immune villagers cower:** "a fig leaf of having them cower or run away scared before pathing back ... a cowering animation with their arms up in a defensive post would be pretty funny." A new pose, approved.
- **The dye vat:** all three usable, unlocked at Crafting 3 as a reward; a picker like a stripped-down character creation screen; each item declares its dyeable regions and allowed colours or schemes, so a two-colour piece can gain a new scheme as data; artists paint one pair of key tones per region (Michael: "I am trying to think where this might go when I hire real artists").
- **The oven cooks** the fire's recipes too (ruled before; a bug). **What the demo includes** of all this is open. **Filed for later:** things set on a table; villagers waving back.

Docs: 43 (new), 39, 42, 05, README.

### 2026-10-24 — The loot tables and the bags ruled: a table per container, gear made by default, one thing a cell, bags made never found (Michael; #268, #260)

The third sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass. Primary's defaults stood but for one: Michael made the rucksack crafted rather than found, "and I prefer that in general as a default. Just make it cost a fair bit, and require some skill levels so you really do have to work for it." On the tables: "Just having these fixed loot tables as a starting point will let me browse the wiki, see the drop rates for everything, and tell you to adjust them. Use OSRS drop tables as a guide." The rulings:

- **Gear is made by default.** Found gear is the exception, placed where it makes sense; a thing that matters costs a fair bit and asks for skill levels.
- **A table per kind of container**, holding what it would really hold: a chest a little food, cloth, a rag; a barrel one or two apples or a waterskin; shelves and cupboards a pantry; a woodpile its wood; carts and stalls the market's goods; bookcases and desks nothing until books; a strongbox nothing until gems and trinkets; a coffin nothing, a rag, or rarely a dirk; the inn's counter bread, cheese, apples. Weights are out of 32, so a weight reads as a chance in 32 a roll, as OSRS's tables are read.
- **No weapon, tool, bag, hat, or belt in any rolled table,** but for a coffin's rare dirk, as grave goods (sticks and planks count as materials here). Placed once: the hatchets in your home chest and the joiner's; a sword and a mace in the toll house's chest until the castle's armoury is built; a leather belt and work gloves on Hollow Farm's barn rack; the Onion-head Hat, one of a kind, on the pumpkin field's scarecrow. No rucksack anywhere.
- **Rolled once,** when a container is first searched, and never refilled: the fields, the orchard, the fish, and the regions are what comes back.
- **Boss drops are personal and protected:** each player rolls their own; a capstone starts at about 1 in 8 and rises with each kill that misses, certain by the twentieth, the count on the player and in the save (with the bosses).
- **The dead's pockets stay poor.**
- **Measured, not guessed:** the wiki shows every table's rates, generated from the data.
- **One thing a cell:** only arrows and fire arrows (20), throwing knives (20), throwing axes (10), bandages (5), every seed (20), and pemmican when it comes stack; food and materials take a cell each, which also keeps rot simple (a stack would merge food of different ages). Shapes stay; nothing becomes 1 by 1. The wheelbarrow is the hauling answer.
- **Bags are made, never found:** the sack (6 by 4) in the kit; a linen satchel (7 by 5) at the loom at Crafting 3, from flax, so it can be made before the animals; the rucksack (8 by 6) at Crafting 6 and Beastlore 4, a fair cost (*Primary's default, Michael's to overrule:* four leather, four woollen cloth, two spruce planks, and the satchel it replaces); the valley's best (10 by 6) at Crafting 10; a region's two from bosses, named by Michael. A bag taken off keeps its contents and stands as a container. The Developer Bag stays a dev world's.

### 2026-10-24 — No boards on a fence, a gate included (Michael)

The review of #397 found that the dead's strikes on a fence gate ignored its boards, so a boarded gate was no stronger. Primary asked whether boards on a gate should count as they do on a door. Michael: "I would not allow boards on fences period." A fence and its gate take no boards: Board up is not offered on one, `B` at a gate says "A gate takes no boards.", and `map.addBoard` refuses a fence for the host and a guest alike. A fence is a yard's edge that a crowd brings down in time, not a seal; the house's doors and shutters are what a player boards.

### 2026-10-24 — The shove pushes farther: 0.65 tiles, a tired one 0.45 (Michael)

After playing the halved push with the closing burst: "I think my cut in half of the push was too far, especially with the lunge mechanic. When you are low on stamina it seems like it pushes them like a foot or two back and only staggers them for a split second. I think it can be like 0.2-0.3 tiles more generous on both full stamina and empty." **Ruled:** the push is 0.65 tiles (`shove.knockback`, was 0.4), and a tired shove's push has its own share, `shove.tiredPush` 0.7 of it, 0.45 tiles (was 0.2, at the tired blow's half). *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* the tired shove's stagger stays at the tired blow's half, 0.4 s, under the shove's 0.5 s recovery, so an empty bar still cannot hold a crowd in a stagger (the lock found building #389); its knockdown chance stays halved too. ### 2026-10-24 — The first real melee test: the dead strike a fence while they wait, the shove's push halved, the downed made readable, a closing burst to try, the aim guide on, the sounds widened, a mute key, a list of the levers (Michael; #385)

Michael played the whole melee pass twice (`docs/41`; his notes, in Primary's words, are #385): legible "for the first time ever", much more Zomboid-like. Primary laid out eleven decisions with recommendations; Michael: "Otherwise if I have not commented on it yet, adopted as written", with four answers of his own. The rulings:

- **The dead that wait for a bite token strike the fence or gate between them and their target** instead of standing. Michael saw the dead "get near the fence and stop before getting within arms reach, and just stand there": since bite tokens (`41` step 5), only two bite over a fence at once and the rest waited; with the tokens lifted, as before, all bit and none struck it. A siege must keep its pressure on a fence (2026-10-17).
- **The shove's push is halved**, 0.8 tiles to 0.4 (`shove.knockback`): "comically far ... they just slide backwards, pause briefly, and then resume movement." **A placeholder pose of the dead reeling back** plays through a stagger, in `data/poses.json` (the rig's poses, approved here for it). *Primary's reading in building it, Michael's to overrule:* **a shove the bar cannot pay for pushes, staggers, and floors at half** (`blow.tiredDamage`), as the tired blow does: with the push halved the shoved stay in reach, the stagger (0.8 s) outlasts the shove's recovery (0.5 s), and a shove worked in full on an empty bar, so a crowd could be held in a stagger for ever (the instrument: no bite and no kill).
- **The downed dead are made readable:** their health bar stays up while they are down; Object Highlight outlines them as anything else you can act on; new art and a fall animation are an art ask; a falling thud joins the sounds. Michael: they "look cut in half so they all mix together ... It makes it very hard to tell downed from dead."
- **A closing burst, as a trial:** within about three tiles of a target it sees, one of the dead walks faster (Primary's first numbers: 1.5 times, three tiles), two numbers in `data/tuning.json` and a lever to switch it off, measured by the instrument's lone-zombie row. Michael's question: whether the game needs Zomboid's speed-up near the player "to help 1 or 2 zombies close the deal and have a chance of taking down a player not paying attention", and the kiting it allows. Within the clock law: it is keyed to distance, never time.
- **Left as they are for now:** the spear's and the halberd's main attack ("maybe not since halberd is a thing you level to"), the bow ("it will make leveling up and getting fire arrows and whatnot feel like the upgrade they should"), and the dead's 150 health ("about right, give or take a bit"; and later, "PLENTY of room to go up": the bullet-sponge instinct stands, and his earlier thought of bringing melee's top end down is withdrawn). They come back with the skills and the difficulty pass (#133).
- **Corners stay as dangerous for sight as in Zomboid:** "players should learn not to have fights there as you do in zomboid". Not to be perfected.
- **The aim guide is on by default**, off in Settings for the challenge: "I personally think it's just way more fun to play with it on, because it somehow represents the FEEL of drawing a bow or casting a spell really well. The little marker zipping down the line from you to the enemy is like a loading bar." It overturns 2026-10-16, The aim guide is off by default (struck where it sits). A browser that saved its own choice keeps it.
- **The sounds:** the grass footstep is deleted and a player on grass plays the dead's shuffle: "the dead's shuffle is also my footsteps, hence they sound alike", which is "inherently scary". Step 2 of `docs/40` widens to the shove, the bow's draw, release, and landing, a spell's cast, the dead's death and a downed one's fall, and doors and shutters opening and closing ("it does not have to be fancy and both can be the exact same sound"); #386. **`docs/40`'s six questions answered:** sound on by default, no panning, the whoosh loudest at the click, the generated ambience with no music until Michael picks a track, and the three deliberate gaps (a door out of sight, the dead's steps in the dark, a groan behind a wall) stand as written; **a mute key exists after all, on F8** ("easy to build and nice to have"; F1 opens the browser's help in Chrome), with "Sound off." or "Sound on." as its twin.
- **One list of the levers:** `docs/42-levers.md`, every tuning lever Michael means to keep turning and every world setting he has named, each with where it lives, its value, the ruling, and its kind; a rule in `CLAUDE.md` keeps it current; and a wiki page made from it (#387). Michael: "I do keep saying it and I am not sure if it ever gets written down."

Docs: 40, 41, 42, CLAUDE.md, README.

### 2026-10-24 — The spear breaks one throw in four, hit or miss, and says so; a spear that stays in a body keeps its wear (Michael)

Asked whether a thrown spear can hit, kill, and not be in the body's loot: yes, half the time, since the break was rolled on every throw, hits included, and a broken spear lodged nothing and said nothing. Michael asked what a spear costs before ruling the rate. A spear is one iron bar and two sticks at the anvil, and an ore rock gives exactly the two ore of one bar, then stands spent three game days: at 50% a throw cost half a bar, about two kills a bar against a sword's or a mace's twenty. **Ruled:** "That rate for that effort sounds perfect":

- **The break is 25%** (`thrown.breakChance` on the spear, was 0.5): about four throws a spear.
- **The same whether it hits a body or lands on the ground** ("equally applied across both in and out of inventory"): a broken spear neither lies where it fell nor lodges, as before.
- **The thrower is told:** "The throwing spear breaks." on screen and in the log, the host's own on the host, a guest's on that guest (`host.js`), from the landing's `broken`.
- **A spear that stays in a body comes out of the search as itself, its wear kept** (`lodgedItems` on the character; the wiki sync's finding, cmz-wiki#20): it came back new before. Arrows lodge by count, made fresh, as they always have.

Michael's reading of the whole, from his play: "often but not always a one hit kill" (a full draw does the whole 150; a partial one 40 to 100 percent of it), and "for its power the 'you have to go get it' thing is a real drawback. So I think this plan in total feels about right. High risk, high reward, renewable but not infinite, requires you to return home." *For the throwing knives and axes (#51) when they throw:* a lodged thrown thing is now an entry each, so a hundred knives in one body need stacking on the search.

### 2026-10-23 — The melee feel pass: the bite's wind-up, the flinch, the shove, knockdown, stamina, bite tokens (Michael, with the Secondary (combat); `docs/41`, #89)

Michael, after a session: melee feels "very wishy washy", "a lot of random swinging and hoping I am close enough to hit", the dead "walk into and attack you as you are attacking them". He proposed a stagger on every hit, a Zomboid shove on Space, knockdown restricted to some weapons, and reopened the turn-rate question, and asked for research first: Zomboid's melee, and how the twin-stick isometric games handle close combat. The code reading found the three things the research then put first: the bite lands 83 ms after the attack begins, with nothing to read or cancel; a hit that does not stagger shows no change of pose; and every zombie in reach bites at once. The research and the comparison are `docs/41`, section 2.

**Ruled, "I accept your suggestions", the recommendation adopted across the board, in six steps, each measured by a scripted fight before the next (`docs/41`):** (1) the dead's bite gets a 0.4 s wind-up (six frames at 10 fps, the bite on frame 4), any landed hit during it cancels the bite, every hit shows a flinch (the stagger still by weight with its immunity), hit stop 0.07, the Swing targets outline on by default, and the commitment is a slowdown to 0.6 and a held facing through the swing's animation; (2) the shove on Space is the crowd tool: three targets whatever the Multi-hit setting, a push of 0.8 tiles, a stagger of 0.8 seconds, a knockdown one time in ten, 15 stamina, and a 0.5 s recovery in which no swing starts, with its own pose; (3) knockdown 0 by default, the mace 0.35, the halberd's thrust 0.5, the shove 0.1, the floor 0.8 s, and a rising zombie takes plain damage with no finisher; (4) stamina regeneration 10 a second (was 22) with swing costs raised (sword 10, mace 14, halberd 16 and 20), the tired blow kept; (5) bite tokens: a pool of 2 plus one for every three more of the dead in reach of a target, a holder keeping its token through its cooldown and a stagger and losing it when floored, killed, or out of reach, the nearest waiter taking a freed token, per target; (6) the dead's health set from hit counts after the rest, 150 as the first trial.

**Ruled with it:** **no endurance tiers and no muscle strain, ever:** "I really, really do not want those. I am hoping we can balance around not having them." Stamina regeneration has no law ("there is no law about stamina regen"); a cut to about 10 a second "is on the table" if it does work, and step 4 is that. His first thought of doubling the dead's health is held until the feel changes land, then set from hit counts. The turn rate is not capped: the research found no top-down game that caps it; the swing's direction is already settled at the click. Bite tokens are per target, which answers the multiplayer question cleanly, and holds whether or not villagers can be hurt. **Melee combat testing is not finished until hit sounds are in:** "HEARING a hit land would be an important signal"; the pass is built and measured without them and its acceptance is reopened with sound on (`docs/41` section 9, #310).

**The cancel is a lever** (Michael, 2026-10-23, after Primary's review found that with the unconditional cancel a lone zombie never bites a player who keeps clicking, measured by the instrument at 0 against `main`'s 0.4 a fight): "Let's let the unconditional cancel stand for now, but is it worth noting somewhere that this is a level we can tune after some real combat testing?" So it stands, and the two settings are named where the number is (`docs/41` step 1; the `stagger` note in `data/combat.json`): what a cancelled bite costs the dead one (the bite's whole cooldown today, half of it the softer setting) and whether every landed hit cancels or only a staggering one; read by the instrument's lone-zombie row after real combat testing.

**Michael's leaning, not ruled:** NPCs invincible by default ("I think we are going to rule that"). It would meet a standing ruling: villagers are killable and carry no weapons (2026-10-08), the guide and every villager who teaches a skill are immune by a flag, and "some villagers are always left at risk" (2026-10-18, The valley first); when he rules it he rules against that. Nothing in the pass depends on it: a villager's crowd has its own token pool either way.

**Struck by this entry:** the 2026-10-16 hold "Combat stays paused until Michael's play session"; the 2026-10-16 "knockdown chance 0.3 stands until the combat pass"; the 2026-10-12 hold on the spin-click cap (question 29b, closed: no cap); and, of the 2026-10-12 stagger ruling, the part that only an `interrupts` weapon cancels a bite under way (`interrupts` is retired; the wind-up is what is cancelled).

### 2026-10-23 — The food economy ruled: everything rots, planted food slowly; a leaner start to about twelve days; the starving fade; no auto-eat (Michael; #264)

The second sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass. Michael overruled one default and sharpened two; the rest stood. The rulings:

- **Everything rots, including food nobody has found.** Primary's default was that only food a player had taken rots. Michael: "any food PLANTED, i.e. it still has roots in the ground or is still hanging from a tree, rots much, much more slowly. Generously slowly, but does still rot. Food that spawns in containers should rot I think, else opening container but never looting food becomes a viable strategy since it would preserve it forever if you left that single bread in the barrel until you needed it. We also don't want to punish people for wanting to loot all of the food they find and centralize its location to a base." So: food in a container rots from the world's start; food in the ground or on a tree rots slowly (*Primary's reading:* five times the item's life); and it sits within the clock law because the world's clock runs only while someone is in the world (`31/09`), so nothing rots while a friend is away.
- **The rates** (game days to rotten, a number per item in the data, Primary's defaults accepted): raw fish 1; cooked dishes 2; bread 3; apples 6; potatoes, cabbages, garlic, pumpkins 8; cheese 10; salted or smoked 20; grain, flour, dried herbs, pemmican, honey never. **Rotten food** is a Rotten item, not edible, for the composter and the pigs later. **On the card:** "Spoils in N days" (Michael: an American phrasing, not "goes off"), a mark on the icon on its last day; fresh and rotten only.
- **Preserving:** salting and smoking 20 days, drying and pemmican forever, **a barrel doubles the life of food in it**, the cellar cut for 1.0.
- **A leaner start, to a target:** day one holds about twelve game days of food for one player with tools and about six without, a tenth of today, read on the wiki's Day-one food page; one ripe row per farm and one at your cottage, the rest at the first two stages; bread and cheese at a third of their weight in the chest's table and barrels one or two apples; the kit and the orchard unchanged. The pantry table for shelves and cupboards is #268's.
- **The starving fade:** from empty, health drains to dead over half a game day instead of about a minute. **The hunger drain** stays a day and a half until rot is in, then both are retuned together.
- **No auto-eat from the belt:** food heals exactly its number, so an automatic bite would spend a heal; the Hungry moodle and a stomach growl are the prompt.
- **Untended crops:** a growing crop that goes dry pauses, as before; a ripe crop left in the ground rots on its slow clock, which answers Michael's worry that "there is never a reason to harvest until you are sure you're going to eat it soon" without a death timer. Michael is open to discussion here.

Docs: 39 (every #264 line), TODO.

### 2026-10-23 — The skills' open lines ruled: death takes all, teachers and Learn, the station panel, every weapon craftable, the staff and the book, the testing map (Michael; #275)

The first sitting of the Valley scope's defaults pass (`CLAUDE.md`'s way since 2026-10-23: Primary proposes an answer for every open line of `39`, Michael overrules in prose, what stands is the default). Every line of `39` tagged #275 is rewritten to these. Michael's rulings:

- **Death takes all skills.** "With no real stakes beyond a fig leaf of one level, dying is just another thing"; it is what makes a life a run. A sandbox world rule to soften it may come later; playtests decide. Nothing softens it in co-op.
- **Combat experience:** Melee, Ranged, and Magic by damage dealt with that kind; Faith by its acts (a prayer, a ward, a cure) and by a smite's own damage, never the swing's that caused it. **A skill touches combat exactly as far as the four combat trees say** and no further; the other trees only through what they make.
- **Recipes:** a new character knows every level-1 recipe and nothing else. A level's perks come by themselves; its recipes and unlocks are **learned once from the skill's teacher** after reaching it, free, which is the reason to go back to the valley ("we WANT people going back to the valley"). Learn is the teacher's own screen: what you can learn now, what the next level gives, a button to learn each. Reserved for later, Michael's: **recipe drops** from bosses, a scrap of paper that teaches a thing and is consumed.
- **The station panel lists every recipe of the station** but boss-drop ones, the unlearned greyed with what they need: "an immediate goal to work towards if you were struggling to figure out what to do".
- **Who teaches what is punted into the final valley design**, because **the map as it stands is a testing map, not the final valley**: "It's all a smidge too close, and most of the buildings you can barely fit into. So I consider the current valley map to be just an updated testing map, ala the clearing early on." (#92.)
- **Every melee weapon is craftable**, the halberd, flail, and greatsword included: weapons degrade and repair only delays it (2026-10-19), so a weapon found once would be lost for good. Throwing knives and axes at Blacksmithing 3 and from the bandits' and the woodcutters' camps; bolts with the crossbow at Crafting 8, their tips Blacksmithing 3. *Primary's readings:* the halberd forged at Blacksmithing 5, the flail and greatsword at 7; the castle's armoury still holds them.
- **The staff and the book, settled:** ambidextrous, a staff in either hand and a book in the other; a staff is one-handed and degrades; **a book never degrades**, "a physical representation of having learned that skill", and owning it grants its spell; a hold of right does nothing with a book; no spell without both, so no spell bar. Names for now: "Book of [Spell]" and "Staff of [School]", cooler ones later. Staves are made at the workbench at Crafting 4 from the school's wood and a gem (Mining 4). The fireball becomes **Fire Bolt**, the flare with some damage of 2026-10-17 under the new name, and it still sets alight what is made to burn. **Life Ward** is a Faith 3 prayer book. The cure has two ways, both beyond the valley: Cure Undeath and a Divine Potion (Potions 10, the Abbey's). *Primary's reading, from Potions 5 and 7:* a throwable poison flask at Potions 7, a splash that poisons the dead it hits.
- **Fishing 10:** no unique fishing reward before a playtest; the feast (Cooking 10) is the shared reward. *Primary's reading:* a perk instead, fish traps hold twice as much and nothing slips the hook.
- **Bandages:** one a cloth at Crafting 1, two at Crafting 2 [change: two today]; a bandage also heals modestly under its stop-bleed, more than an apple, tuned in the food pass.
- **The stations and things no tree named:** millstones Cooking 1; the charcoal clamp Blacksmithing 1; cider at the press Cooking 3; ale cut with hops; tanning at a tanning vat (Crafting 3) that comes in with Beastlore 1; no kiln: clay (Mining 3) shaped at the workbench (Crafting 1) and fired in any oven, a bottle a jug, a mug wood; berries Herbalism 1; the common herbs sage, nettle, and feverfew, yarrow the healing herb, the mana herb's name Michael's; hay cut with the scythe at Farming 5 for penned animals (Beastlore 4), straw and thread cut; the fishing rod Crafting 1; the barns' watering can, pitchfork, sickle, and threshing flail are props (the pitchfork "someday"); the halberd aside, the tunic and breeches Crafting 1, gloves, boots, the jerkin, and the waterskin Crafting 3, the bandits' leather armour found only; the carried lantern Blacksmithing 4 on fish oil (Fishing 5); the stamina herb tea Cooking 1 at a fire; the stew split into a pottage (Cooking 1) and the hearty stew (Cooking 4), cooked meats Cooking 1, cheese Cooking 3, eggs and bacon Cooking 4; the plan's other crops (turnip, wheat, beans, hops, onion, carrot) cut from 1.0 ("Plenty for now"); today's broadleaf is oak and today's conifer spruce, mahogany new and needing a design; a log and a plank per wood; your own workstations (Woodcutting 7, Crafting 5): the workbench, loom, drying rack, composter, cauldron, smoking rack, and hive, never the forge, anvil, millstones, or oven.
- **Deposits:** each resource from the ground gets a prop of its own to mine, signposted by its look ("design a small prop for each that can be mined. Also: gems!"); the rock gives stone and the ore rock iron today. *Primary's reading:* a clay bank at the river's edge, a salt rock in the mine, gems by chance from the ore rock from Mining 4 and a crystal cluster for the finer ones.
- **Buffs and debuffs are not moodles:** a moodle describes a general state and is read-only; a buff or a debuff is one specific effect, often directly interactable (a bandage removes a bleed). Buffs are shown in the debuff row, in gold, well fed among them.
- **The infection's direction** (its numbers at #123's sitting): about a day and a half (Michael: "24 or 48 hours") to find the cure, then death, the moodles worsening along the way, Zomboid's way; "your moodles probably won't be green across the board when you finally turn".

Michael, naming a new Primary with a planning-and-infrastructure focus and setting three goals (a final Valley scope, #271; a limited demo scope; then building both, demo-first, after a plan is talked through): "Make that change to the demo rule, there is no reason it must wait for final art and is the much simpler overall playtest because it's not meant for long term survival or maxxing skills or whatever, it's there to give you a bounded experience of the game." **Ruled:** the demo (`36`) is built now, on dev art, as the game's bounded playtest; 2026-10-13's "not built yet" and "nothing goes into the demo without its final art" are struck. What stands from 2026-10-13: it is built, tested, and given real art before it goes to the public as a demo or beside a Steam page. *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* the demo's scope is a cut list from the Valley's (`39`), each line naming the valley issue it depends on, so the two scopes close together, and the build order is demo-first with the shared systems (combat feel, sounds, the scenario layer) first. ### 2026-10-20 — Doors and windows in another wood, built: the palette, the leaf, and the costs (#334, PR 2; the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

Built as the ruling says (a door or a window may be of a different wood from its wall; only Winter Pine and Bloodbark are restricted; nothing is painted). **The leaf:** a door or a window gains `leaf`, the wall sprite of the wood of its leaves and shutters, stored only when it differs from the wall's own (a wall's own are its wood's, daub's are oak's: `wall.leaf` in its definition), so every wall made before reads as it did; it rides the chunk, the save, and the sync hash with the wall. An open leaf is drawn from the leaf wood's parts sheet; the shut door and the closed shutters, painted into the wall's `door_shut` and `window_shut` frames, are recoloured on first use (`src/woods.js`): the oak wall's frames are the base, every other wood's wall being the oak one with its table of `data/wood_tones.json` applied to every pixel, so a variant is the wall's table on the pixels outside the shut leaf and the leaf wood's table on the pixels inside it (the pixels that differ from the open frame and carry a leaf role's tone); it is registered as a generated sheet keyed by wall and leaf. `check("woods")` proves it against the delivered files: the oak frames with a wood's table equal that wood's delivered shut door and shutters, pixel for pixel, for all six and four frames each, and every wall with every other leaf wood is the wall's delivered pixels outside the leaf and the leaf wood's inside it (25 pairs). **The palette (the builder's reading):** one row per wall type (Log wall, Oak, Spruce, Mahogany, Daub, with Winter Pine and Bloodbark only while Free building is on), one row for a door and one for a window, and while a door or a window is the mode **R cycles the wood of its leaves**, shown the same way, "leaves: Spruce · R", under its row and in the prompt at the ghost, the cycle being the wall's own, oak, spruce, mahogany, then the regions' two with Free building on; the ghost shows the door in the chosen wood. A guest's click sends the same `build` command with the chosen `leaf`, and the host applies the rules its own click meets. **The rules:** a leaf choice is only for a wall of a wood (a log or stone wall has no table to recolour its shut door from, so it takes only its own: "Only a wood wall takes another wood's door"); a region's wood is refused unless Free building is on ("Winter Pine is found beyond the valley"); a gate in a fence has no leaf. **Costs, until planks per species (#275):** a wall of any wood or daub is one plank, three seconds, the hammer (the log wall stays a log, the hatchet), a door three planks and a window two whatever its wood. **Over a broken door or window** a new one is still never a repair, but its default wood is the old one's (a broken Winter Pine door rebuilt with no choice comes back Winter Pine, and with Free building off is refused as a chosen Winter Pine one is), shown as "leaves: as it was, Winter Pine · R". A refusal names the thing and the way out ("Only a wood wall takes a spruce window · R to change"). A plank's card says the walls of every wood once ("wood wall (1)") beside the door, window, fence, and gate, and never lists a region's wood, whatever Free building says.

### 2026-10-20 — The woods as wall types, built: the valley's mix (#334, PR 1; the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

Built as the ruling above says (every wood and daub a wall type of its own, nothing painted): `wall_spruce`, `wall_mahogany`, `wall_winterpine`, `wall_bloodbark`, and `wall_daub` are sprite definitions beside `wall_wood` (oak), wired from the delivered files in `data/art/village` with the anchors of `anchors_woods.json` (the oak set's), each with its post (`post_<wood>`) and its leaves' sheet (`wall_parts_<wood>`); daub has no leaves of its own and names `wall_parts_wood`. All are `material: "wood"`, so where two woods meet the post is the heavier material's and, the ranks being equal, the first wall's; a straight run changes wood at a post. **Which building gets which wood** is the builder's reading, Michael adjusts it by eye: **oak** your cottage, the joiner's, the smithy (both rooms), the mill and its granary, the miners' hut, the burners' hut, the cottage by the baker's, the second cottage on the lane; **spruce** the weaver, the toll house, Hollow Farm's great barn and granary, Long Acre's barn, the quarryman's hut, the cooper's long house, the shut cottage; **mahogany** the inn (both wings), the merchant's storehouse, the reeve's house's south and east faces (stone at the back as before), the chandler's house; **daub** the baker, the priest's house, Hollow Farm's farmhouse, Long Acre's farmhouse, the cottage by the merchant's, the first cottage on the lane. The cottage plans take the wood as a parameter. Winter Pine and Bloodbark stay out of the valley (the regions' woods). **Bloodbark, at night:** the art README warns it is the darkest thing in the set, and it is: in the torch's pool its planks read, but the lit face falls to near-black a couple of tiles from the flame (`docs/images/woods/`); flagged, not tuned. The lever, if Michael wants it lifted, is `chroma_kept` or a touch more lightness on the base in `wood_tones.json`, and the art is the design agent's.

### 2026-10-20 — The woods are wall types, mixed with any wood's doors and windows; only Winter Pine and Bloodbark are restricted (Michael)

Asked #334's five questions (mixing, finishes, doors per wood, where the woods come from, daub), Michael:

> "1. Yep
> 2. I don't really think we need to paint the walls? Or is that how you interpreted the daub version and other colors of wood? I assumed they would not be painted but would in fact be whole new walls
> 3. By default yes,  but I think we should be able to do different windows and doors on different woods.
> 4. The only ones that need restricting are the white and deep red ones, I think the rest come from trees in the valley? So they should be mixed and matched in the default buildings
> 5. I think you are thinking of this material differently than I was. I assumed it was simply a new wall type, not plastering "over" anything?"

His first question (2026-10-20) was: "do the wood recolorings mean we can mix and match? So like? Standard wood but with white doors and shutters?"

- **Every wood is a wall type of its own, and so is daub.** Oak, spruce, mahogany, Winter Pine, Bloodbark, and daub are each a whole wall, built as itself; nothing is painted. There is no paint and no "finish" table: Primary's proposal of paints is withdrawn. The "white" of the first question is Winter Pine, the pale wood, and the "deep red" is Bloodbark.
- **Mix and match: a door or a window may be of a different wood from its wall.** By default a wall's doors and shutters are its own wood (a mahogany house has mahogany doors); building one, the player may choose any other wood for it (oak walls with Winter Pine doors and shutters). Daub, which has no leaves of its own, takes oak's by default (Primary's reading, agreed below).
- **Only Winter Pine and Bloodbark are restricted.** They are the regions' woods (the Pale Reach, the Crimson Abbey; docs/39), so building with them means going there. Oak, spruce, and mahogany grow in the valley, and the valley's own buildings are built in all of them and in daub, mixed, instead of all in oak.
- *Primary's readings, agreed by Michael ("Agree on all of your assumptions", 2026-10-20):* daub's doors and shutters are oak's by default; until there are planks per species (#275), a wall, door, or window of any valley wood or of daub costs what today's do, in plain planks; Winter Pine and Bloodbark are offered only while Free building is on, until the regions and their planks exist. Which valley buildings get which wood is the map's to choose, place by place, in `tools/mapgen/valley.py`.

### 2026-10-20 — Paintings, built: hung from the bag onto a wall's face (#326; the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

Built as the 2026-10-20 ruling says (paintings are items, placed from the bag, picked up again): bag sizes **2 by 2, 2 by 3, 3 by 2**, the card says what Place does, and Object Highlight outlines a hung one (it has Pick up). **The builder's readings:** what hangs is **state on the wall segment** (`wall.hangings`: `{ item, slot, span }` on the segment that holds the painting, `{ cover, slot }` on the second segment of a wide one), so it is in the chunk's record, the save, a guest's chunk, and the sync hash with the wall, and no new world list exists. **Which walls take one:** a plain solid wall of a drawn material only; not a door, a window, a fence, bars, a broken piece, or a segment with a bracket on its face (a torch is lit from there). **Which face:** the camera-facing face only (the one the bracket's `side` +1 and the boards' `near` are on, the only face the art is drawn for, none for the far face): the inside of a building's north and west walls, the outside of its south and east walls. **How many:** one to a segment, whatever its size (a small and a wide painting both take the top slot, a tall one both; the second slot stays free in the data for a low piece later); a wide painting needs two segments of the same material with no wall meeting the joint (the post would stand in front of it), never at a corner (a wall meeting either end of the run on the camera's side, a west wall going down from a north run's end or a north wall going out from a west run's, stands in front of the painting's end and hides much of it: the refusal reads "A corner is in the way"; a corner behind the run hides nothing), and aimed at the last section of a wall it hangs from the one before. **Reach** is the furniture's, 2.6 tiles to the nearest floor tile under it with a clear line, so a painting is hung and taken down from the face's own side; a click aimed through a translucent near wall at the one behind it hangs on the one behind. **When its wall goes** (Remove, or a door or a window built over it, which stamps the wall) **the painting drops to the floor as the item, whole, once**, on the face's floor tile: a painting is never lost with its wall (that tile may be under a solid prop; it stays there and can still be picked up, so nothing is lost). **A wide painting is drawn in two halves,** each with its own wall segment, cut at the joint like a cut wall, so neither half sorts in front of furniture that stands before its own wall. **Pick up** is the menu's (a right-click on the picture); E does not take one down, a wall being a place E is pressed often. **Place stays on the screen that chose it**, as the rug's does, and a guest hangs and takes down through the same command path (`performPlacement` and `performByLabel`); a hanging is a pixel-hit rectangle on a wall, an address `{ kind: "painting", tx, ty, edge }` of the segment that holds it. The Developer Chest's grid grew from 10 by 8 to 10 by 14 to hold the eight.

### 2026-10-20 — Fences and gates are buildable now; only the gate swings, doors later (Michael)

Asked, wiring the drawn fence (#332), whether fences should be buildable now and whether house doors should swing like the gate, Michael: "Buildable now and only fence for now, we can animate doors later".

- **A fence and a gate are in the build palette now.** *Primary's reading of the costs*, Michael's to change: a fence segment is a plank, a gate two planks, built with the hammer like the other building; Free building waives both as it waives all building. *Also Primary's reading:* a gate goes on an empty edge or into a fence, never into another wall ("Only in a fence"); a house Door built into a fence still makes a gate, as it has since #147.
- **Only the fence's gate swings** (its two drawn in-between leaves, played on every screen from the gate's open state; *the length, about a quarter of a second, is Primary's reading*). House doors keep snapping open until they are animated later.

### 2026-10-20 — Rugs, built: laid from the bag into a slot of their own (#327; Michael's agreed readings, the builder's the rest, Michael's to overrule)

Built as the 2026-10-20 ruling says (rugs are items, placed from the bag, picked up again): bag size **1 by 4** (rolled), **Pick up refused while furniture stands on it** ("Move the chest off it first."), **no baked outline**, and Object Highlight outlines a laid one (it has Pick up). **The builder's readings:** a laid rug is a prop in **a slot of its own on the tile** (`rug`, beside `prop`), not in `prop`, because a tile holds one prop and furniture must be able to stand on a rug and a rug lie under furniture; it is `solid: false`, never blocks a step or a sight, and carries the item it came from; **Place stays on the screen that chose it** (the ghost, R, and Esc are the client's; the click sends the command to the host as any placement's), and the reach is the furniture's, 2.6 tiles to the nearest tile with a clear line; **E picks a rug up only when nothing else is in reach** (a rug is under the player's feet as often as not, and E would otherwise take it before a chest); the Developer Chest's grid grew from 10 by 6 to 10 by 8 to hold the two rugs. **A rug is laid only under what can be lifted away** (a table, a chest, a bed, a chair, a barrel: a sprite with `carry: true`), never under a tree, an anvil, a crop, or a cart, which would leave it unable to come up; **a wall is not built through a laid rug** (both tiles of the edge under one rug); and in the menu a rug's Pick up follows the characters' entries, a villager on the rug being the thing pointed at.

Michael: "I think for the belt I would probably prefer recolored closer to today's belt". Done by swapping its four rope tones for four browns built from today's two (`#8a7a60`, `#73583a`, `#5e4028`, `#432e1c`), the outline and the drawing untouched. *Primary's reading:* no new art was needed, as a straight swap keeps the drawn ramp.

The art of art round 3 is wired (the places and the hashes are in the pull request, #338). **The beehive** is `interactable` with no `clickAnim`, so it sways when clicked as Michael asked; the bee frames stay spare (a `clickAnim` would stop the sway) and it has no action yet, there being no honey item. **The chicken coop** is a container with a placeholder table of its own (`coop`: rags, sticks, a little grain, mostly nothing; no egg item), and **it is not `interactable`: a container does not sway** (the chest does not either; the outline is in the art), though the delivery's JSON said it was.

### 2026-10-20 — Faith: Smite is a share of the hit; prayer grows by level; Consecrate Ground gives light (Michael)

Michael, reading the specs late on 2026-10-20 (first recorded on #275):

> "For the faith skill, if the spec still has does L extra damage, I would change it to does 150% of the hit that proc'd it's damage as holy spell damage (which will mater someday). Then 200%, then 250%? For the tiers? If we are going to scale bosses to the thousands I think this is still conservative. 1-10 extra damage is nothing. This also makes it scale with holy might which is quite a good thing."

> "Also, praying at altars (faith level 1) it also restores stamina and health to full and removes bleeds. Or maybe start with mana and energy, then add bleeds, then add health? We can space these out OSRS style."

> "For consecrate ground, it should emit some light over the whole area, because it's cool."

Then, after a break the same day: "Go ahead and record those 3 faith rulings in the right place".

- **Smite is a share of the hit, not a flat bonus.** A smite deals 150% of the damage of the hit that triggered it, as holy spell damage, at Faith 2; 200% at Faith 5; 250% at Faith 9. Its chances are unchanged (1, 2, then 3 in 10). It scales with whatever raises the hit, Holy Might among them. "Holy spell damage" is a damage type that will matter later (resistances, the dead that fear it). docs/39's "the level's worth" is replaced.
- **Praying at an altar grows by level, OSRS style:** from Faith 1 it restores mana and stamina. Later it also removes bleeds, and later still it restores health. *Primary's reading of the levels,* Michael's to change: bleeds at Faith 4 (beside more mana), health at Faith 6 (beside more health). His first thought, everything at Faith 1, is set aside for his second.
- **Consecrate Ground lights its whole area** while it lasts, as well as its small damage and utility.

### 2026-10-20 — Paintings and rugs are items: placed from the bag, picked up again (Michael)

Michael (2026-10-20), minutes after ruling Decor into the build palette: "Actually wait, instead of building can these be items? Put them, in your bag, then right click "Place" with hover outline? Right click pick up painting" . Same with rug? They would need inventory art. But I like this more than building specifically because you can find this stuff later and bring it home"

- **A painting or a rug is an item.** It is carried in the bag, and its right-click menu offers **Place**: a ghost outlined where it would go (a painting on a wall's face, in its slot; a rug on the floor, `R` turning it), green where it fits and red where not, and a click puts it there. A placed one offers **Pick up** on its own right-click, and it goes back into the bag. You can find them in the world and bring them home. This overturns the same day's Decor-in-the-build-palette ruling, struck below.
- **They need inventory art,** as every item does ("Item art comes as a pair"): a bag icon each, beside the placed picture, which is the item's world picture.
- *Primary's three readings, agreed by Michael ("Agreed on all 3!"):*
  - **Bag sizes:** a small painting 2x2, a tall one 2x3, a landscape 3x2, a rug rolled up 1x4.
  - **A rug under furniture doesn't come up** ("Move the table off it first"), so nothing is left standing on nothing (#327).
  - **Outline:** Object Highlight outlines a placed one, since it now has an action; the art keeps no baked dark outline, the way trees are excused.
- *Still Primary's readings, Michael's to overrule:*
  - **Where they come from now:** the Developer Chest, and the world's Extra starting items. Loot tables (the manor, the castle) and Crafting (docs/39, Crafting 5) come later.
  - **Who can pick up:** anyone can pick up a placed one, as anyone can take a torch from a bracket.
