The wall hangings (#326) and the rugs (#327) are placed from a new Decor group in the build palette, from the art commissioned the same day. Asked whether Decor is offered only while the world's Free building rule is on, or always at no cost, Michael: "Offer Decor only while Free building is on, until Crafting gives it costs."

- ~~**Decor appears in the build palette only while Free building is on;** once Crafting gives each piece its cost (docs/39: Crafting 5 makes rugs and wall pieces), it is offered as other building is, paid for.~~ (superseded 2026-10-20, Paintings and rugs are items: placed from the bag, picked up again (Michael): they are items, not building)

### 2026-10-20 — Every digit in the clear font, at any size (Michael)

Small text (#322) put everything at or under 15 UI px in the system's sans, since the pixel font's small 3 and 8 run together ("our main font has a small 3 and that looks very close toa small 8"). Asked whether the numbers above that size should follow, Michael: "Yes, make every digit use the clear font at any size, including world names and the character screen heading."

- **Every digit, at any size, is in the system's sans;** every letter keeps its face (the pixel font, or Pirata One in the titles). Built as a digits-only face (`unicode-range` U+0030-0039 over `local()` system fonts, nothing downloaded) put first in the interface's and the titles' font stacks.
- *Primary's reading:* Small text's **Pixel** gives back the old look exactly, digits included, since the setting exists to compare.

### 2026-10-20 — Very small text in a clear sans-serif, a Display setting (#321; Michael's ask, Primary's and the builder's readings Michael's to overrule)

Michael: "Is it possible to have a fallback very readable sans-serif font for very small text? Like both numbers in the bleed, some of the very small settings text, basically anything under like 13px +/- a couple just renders in that font for extra clarity at the sake of a smidgeon of flavor?" **Built:** `--font-small`, the system's own hinted sans-serif (`system-ui, "Segoe UI", Roboto, "Helvetica Neue", Arial, sans-serif`: no download, and what an Electron build carries), on every text at or under **15 UI px** (one number, `SMALL_TEXT_MAX_PX` in `ui/small-text.js`; it catches the bleed's 15 and 14 and everything smaller: help lines, the log, the chat box, name tags, the hover card, the skills panel, the floating numbers, the map's place names). Pixelify Sans stays above it, the Pirata One titles stay Pirata One, and monospace stays monospace. **A Display setting, "Small text", Clear (the default) or Pixel**, so he can flip and compare: Pixel points the variable back at the pixel font, so it is exactly the old look. **The readings:** the class is on the page's root, not `#ui` (the screens and the loading line sit outside it); a rule above the threshold names its pixel font so a big heading inside a small container (the Controls screen is one) does not inherit the small face; `em` sizes under 1 count as small. A check scans `style.css` and `src/ui/*.js` for any new small rule without the font, and another reads the page's computed fonts.

### 2026-10-20 — A change to an item checks its card (Michael)

After playing with the hover cards (#313):

> "Hover cards confirmed working. If we spot errors we can fix them, but I am happy to have the pattern started. Make sure this is something to check whenever anything that could have one is edited. Edit how an apple works? Check the card too"

- **A change to what an item does checks its card in the same change:** a number in its data (an apple's heal, a weapon's damage, a bag's size), a new use (a verb, an interaction, a recipe it goes into or comes from, a building cost), or code that changes what it does. The card is read in the pane for each item touched, and a card that says something untrue, or leaves out what changed, is fixed in the same pull request. Errors found later are fixed as they are found; the pattern stands.
- *Primary's reading of "anything that could have one":* every item, since every item drawn in a grid or slot has a card; the same holds for a debuff's card (#315) when a debuff's numbers change.
- Where it lives: the law in `CLAUDE.md`, a line in `github.md` 8.4 ("Prove it"), and a line in the pull request template.

### 2026-10-19 — Bleeding is a debuff: the row at the top right (#308; Michael's ask, the builder's readings Michael's to overrule)

Michael: "fast track bleed moodle -> Bleed debuff, row running along the top of the screen, stuck top right ... a bleed icon, dealer's choice of dev art, number of minutes left (10m) unless < 1m and then (34s)." **Built:** `src/debuffs.js` and `ui/debuff-row.js`: a row at the top right growing leftward, each debuff a square icon with its time under it, its stacks in a corner when more than one, and a hover card in the item cards' style (`ui/hover-card.js`); the bleed icon is a red drop, dev art drawn at first use; **bleeding left the moodles**, the moodle and its "Not bleeding" line both. **The readings:** the time is whole seconds rounded up under a minute ("12s" at a fresh wound, never "0s" while it lasts) and **minutes rounded up** from a minute up, as WoW's are (`600` reads "10m" for its whole first minute, `61` to `120` read "2m"); **the bag's home moved down 64 px** (`top: 76px`, first 52 px and `64px` until the row's numbers grew, 2026-10-20) so it opens under the row, not over it (a dragged panel keeps its own place); the player's record carries `bleed.left` to two decimals (it carried the whole float), and the sync check compares each player's wound; the card says "Losing N health a second", "A bandage stops it", and that another bite makes it worse and starts the time again. Only bleeding is a debuff so far (burning on a player is not a thing; poison and the sickness from unsafe water are open).

### 2026-10-19 — The item hover card: WoW's tooltip, built (#262; Michael's look, the builder's readings Michael's to overrule)

Michael (after a playtest): "some of the dev items have long names that are unreadable and you really just have no idea what it is. Style this like item tooltips in wow when you hover. Little mostly black box, rounded, item name and basic details." The law stands (2026-10-18, "Every item in a grid has a hover card"); this is its look and its content. **Built as:** a small, mostly black box with rounded corners and a thin light border (`ui/hover-card.js`), the full name first, then the kind, then one short line each for what the data says it does (`item-info.js`: food heals and fills, a drink's fill, a weapon's damage, the hand or slot it is equipped to, a tool's verbs, what it can be **Use**d on from `data/interactions.json`, what a seed grows, what it is made at and goes into), the count, fill and wear, and a dim "Right-click for actions."; the word **Use** in `--gold`, the other menu words (Eat, Equip, Drink, Apply) bold white; it shows after `hoverCard.delayMs` (60 ms), follows the pointer, flips to stay on screen, and replaces the browser's title tooltip on every grid and slot (the bag, the belt, the hands, the worn slots, a chest, a body, a station's output); an empty slot shows its name. **The readings:** ~~at most seven lines; a clothing, bag, or weapon item does not list what it goes into (every cloth item goes into a torch);~~ (narrowed 2026-10-19, #313's review: at most eight lines, and every item lists what it goes into and what it builds, weapons too, since the plank and the stick hid their real uses) an item's `note` is a dev note and is not shown. **Out of this build:** the build palette, a station's recipe rows, a prop in the world.

### 2026-10-19 — Object Highlight (#301; Michael's setting, the builder's readings Michael's to overrule)

Michael: a dev setting, on by default, **Object Highlight** (first "Item Highlight", renamed because Items are a special category): hover a game object with a built-in interaction and it is outlined; a thing that is only art (the leaf pile and sticks under a tree) is not. **The readings:** it is the one thing a right-click would act on first, found with the menu's own hit test (`interact.actionsAt`, and the pixel hit `interactableAt` for a tall tree's crown), so a prop with no action gets none, and the bare ground or leaf litter inside a tree's sprite rectangle does not count as the tree (the menu's prop counts only on the tile under the cursor; a pixel on the sprite counts anywhere); an **interactable** thing counts even with no action (a mushroom sways on a click: the gatherables law); a character comes before an item before a prop; **the living dead are left to the cursor's zombie hand and the swing outline**, a corpse (a container) is outlined; **a door, a shutter, and a wall bracket are edges, not sprites, and are not outlined yet**; nothing in placement, removal, or Use mode, with a panel under the pointer, with something carried, or out of sight; **a slice of a prop that is the see-through occluder this frame is not outlined** (eight stacked copies would show far more than the faded tree and fill the hole); the colour is a soft near-white, `#f4f0e6`, not the swing outline's green nor the Inspect tile mark's gold (`data/tuning.json` `objectHighlight`). **Doors and shutters are left out on purpose** (#304): Michael's to rule.

### 2026-10-19 — Unsafe water says so; a crop can be inspected; Object Highlight, a dev setting (Michael)

From Michael's playtest:

> "We need to make sure every water source that is not safe to drink says to besides the drink option. Right click -> "Drink (tainted)" ala zomboid"

> "Give each crop patch a right click -> Inspect that drops a tile highlight so you can see exactly which crop you are inspecting and a little info about it. Type. Time till mature. Is it watered? Is it fertilized?"

> "Can we add a new dev setting, on by default, called "Item Highlight" and it works by highlighting whatever you hover over that is a game item with some kind of built in interaction and not just game art. So a tree would but the pile of leaves and stick beneath it would not since at the moment they are just art." Then: "I meant to call that "Object Highlight" because Items are a special category. Object meaning game object not art."

- **A Drink from unsafe water says "(tainted)"**, beside the option: "Drink from the well (tainted)", "Drink from the water (tainted)", a waterskin's "Drink (tainted, 40%)". Every source is tainted until purifying exists (2026-10-19, Natural water is unsafe until purified). *Primary's readings, Michael's to overrule:* the trough and the font are tainted too (whether they count as natural water was open, question 34; ruled so 2026-10-25, Water ruled); a skin's water is tainted unless its entry is marked clean, and nothing marks one yet; `tuning.water.clean` lists the sources that are safe, none yet. The sickness itself and the skin's clean-only sip are not built (#279).
- **A crop has Inspect**: a line naming the crop, ready or how long until it is, watered or dry, fertilized or not, and the tool that harvests it, with the patch's tile marked on the ground while the line shows. *Primary's readings:* it only reads the world, so it runs on the inspecting player's own screen, a guest's too, and is never sent to the host; it works from wherever the patch is in sight within a dozen tiles and never walks there; "not fertilized" until something fertilizes (the composter, #275's Farming).
- **The Harvest labels name the crop** ("Harvest potatoes", "Harvest cabbage (need a dirk)"). *Primary's reading of the playtest:* potatoes and garlic dig with the shovel from your cottage's chest and cabbages and pumpkins cut with the dirk (2026-10-18, the tool rule, #215); with the potatoes beside the cabbages, E's bare "Harvest" took the potatoes, so they seemed to need no tool.
- **Object Highlight**, a dev setting, on by default: whatever game object the pointer is over, one with an action of its own and not decoration, is outlined. Built as its own pull request (#303); its readings are the entry above, "Object Highlight (#301)".

### 2026-10-19 — Floating numbers over heads, and an icon for each skill (#294; Michael's spec, the builder's readings Michael's to overrule)

Michael: a red "-X" over whoever is hit (damage taken and dealt), a green "+X" for healing, a profession symbol with the number for a gather in white or black, and a tiny icon for every skill. **Built as events, not health diffs (the builder's reading, Primary's, Michael's to overrule):** the authority records a hit, a heal, and a gather where they happen and they ride the frame (`net.PROTOCOL` 8), so natural regeneration (a trickle) shows nothing and each number knows which hit made it. **The builder's readings:** a gather's number is the items gained until skills give experience (#275), by a table in `data/tuning.json` (`floaters.gathers`: chop, mine, fish, pick, and harvest); its text is **black where the light at the character is above `lightAbove` and white where it is below**, with an outline of the other, so it is never taken for the red or the green; **a volley over one body in one frame starts each number a line higher** (`staggerLines`) on top of each one's own sideways drift; **a number over a body I cannot see is hidden** (never a number through a wall or the dark); the fourteen icons are 16 by 16 pixels drawn by shapes at first use, placeholder art like the rest of the kit (`ui/skill-icons.js`).

### 2026-10-19 — Debuffs apart from moodles; only pemmican stacks; hearty food carries buffs; the belt knife is the Dirk (Michael)

Quick hits on the Valley 1.0 scope (`docs/39`):

> "Belt knife -> Knife or Dirk"

> "We need to be careful with what is a moodle and what is not. We are going to need to separate the concept of debuffs out, more WoW style. Bleeds and poisons should go here. Reserving moodles for anything that is a general ongoing need rather than a specific negative effect, excepting zombie infection which should stay a moodle"

> "Remove "minor" from healing potions unless we are making major ones a separate item"

> "I also like these more explicit heartier foods as the ones that carry buffs. Like maybe Bacon and Eggs gives you a 10% greater natural healing for 5 real minutes? Or it gives you cold resistance or something? I do think we should have foods that do stuff like this, so you have to balance when it will go bad, how much it heals for, how full it makes you, AND whether it provides a useful buff."

> "I wouldn't mind making Pemmican the only food that can stack. That gives a small-to-mid food reason to make it besides the no going bad thing. And I like other food not stacking because it pushes you to convert it into cooked foods because it's worth more per bag slot. Otherwise you can just bring a million stacks of apples and just eat constantly during any encounter for infinite healing."

- **The belt knife is renamed the Dirk** (its id stays `knife`). *Primary's pick* of Michael's two: "Knife" is already the Throwing Knife's short name.
- **Debuffs are apart from moodles,** WoW-style. A moodle is a general ongoing need (hunger, thirst, tiredness), and the infection stays a moodle; a specific negative effect is a debuff: bleeding and poison. *Primary's reading, not ruled:* the sickness from unsafe water is a debuff too, which would settle its name clash with the infection's "Sick" stage if Michael agrees (open question 34; #279). (Ruled 2026-10-25, Water ruled: a debuff named Sick, the infection's stage renamed Queasy.)
- **A healing potion is not "minor"** unless a major one becomes a separate item.
- **Hearty cooked dishes carry buffs** (for example bacon and eggs: more natural healing for a while, or resistance to the cold). Every food is weighed four ways: how long it keeps, how much it heals, how full it makes you, and whether its buff is useful. *Primary's assignment:* the dishes and their buffs are the skills scope's (Cooking, #275).
- **One buff dish at a time, for now** (Michael, later the same day, on Primary's suggestion on #275): "Adopted for now, although I will point out that eating food JUST for the buff means even more food production is required so it would push us in the right direction, but I don't want to make food buffs feel mandatory and if you can stack a bunch of them they might."
- **Pemmican is the only food that stacks.** Every other food takes a cell of its own, so a cooked dish is worth more per bag slot than its raw parts and nobody carries "a million stacks of apples" into a fight (#260).
- **Open:** Life Ward, a spell or a potion (#275). Michael:

> "I think this was meant to be a spell when I first mentioned it, but I could be wrong. We can debate."

### 2026-10-19 — The valley has no cure; the wizard lives; the witch teaches Potions; gates stop a harvest; every loot table provisional; two old limits struck (Michael)

Answering the disagreements found while compiling the Valley 1.0 scope (`docs/39`; #271):

> "1. I think this is fine and it gives people more incentives to explore beyond the valley
> 2. I have no idea where dead came from, he should be an alive teacher npc
> 3. Let's give potions to the witch since it's a full spec now
> 4. They should stop you harvesting food yes, this is another way to gate day one food without needing all of the fields to be empty. As for chests, consider every single container loot list in the game provisional until we say otherwise. In fact this might be the last thing we should do for The Valley scope since getting loot tables right means know what all the items are that can go in them
> 5. Yep strike both, they are both older rulings"

The numbers are Michael's, from his chat, not #271's list: his 1 answers #271's 5, his 2 answers 4, his 3 answers 3, his 4 answers 7, and his 5 answers 1 and 2.

- **The valley holds no cure for the infection** (1): the cure lies beyond the valley, an incentive to explore (with 2026-10-19, The cure for the infection is mid to late game, gated by skill).
- **The wizard is alive,** a teacher (2): `docs/29-world/03`'s dead wizard in the castle study is corrected.
- **The witch teaches Potions** (3), the cauldron hers as `docs/30` had it; the wizard teaches Magic (2026-10-18's "magic and potions" narrowed where it sits).
- **A skill's level gate stops a player harvesting the food it gates, ripe fields included** (4): "another way to gate day one food without needing all of the fields to be empty." *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* a gate stops any other harvest it names (a herb, a tree, an ore) the same way.
- **Every container's loot table is provisional** until ruled otherwise (4; #268), and the loot tables are likely the Valley scope's last step, once every item is known (Michael: "this might be the last thing").
- **Two older limits are struck** (5): "one per station family at most" (2026-10-13, What this game is) for the fourteen skills (#275), and "two animals, last" (2026-10-12, The big pass answered) for Beastlore (#289).

### 2026-10-19 — A production skill's best is never the best of its kind; repair is a stop-gap (Michael)

On Primary's Blacksmithing and Crafting drafts (#275):

> "On that capstone I want to make a general note: the best item you can make from a PRODUCTION skill should not be the best item of that type in the game. Those should come from boss drop tables. So the special weapons we discussed, the best available armor, etc. Bigger bags than the crafting capstone are one of those things."

On repairing at the anvil (Blacksmithing 4 in Primary's draft):

> "As long as this doesn't completely negate the need to ever make another one. Otherwise the profession dies pretty fast. It should be a stop-gap like in Zomboid where it makes the item last longer (and gives xp) but isn't a long term solution."

- **A production skill's best is never the best of its kind:** the best weapons, the best armour, and bags bigger than Crafting's capstone come from bosses' drop tables (beside the capstone-drop policy, 2026-10-19, Rare capstone drops worth farming a boss for, a policy on its condition).
- **Repairing is a stop-gap,** as in Zomboid: it makes an item last longer and gives experience, but never removes the need to make a new one. It overturns in part 2026-10-12's "no repair, no mending" (Weapon durability: permanent, hits only, evaporates; struck where it sits); one-way wear by landed hits, and evaporating at zero, stand. *Primary's readings:* each repair gives back less, so an item wears out in the end; repair is for made weapons and tools, while "everything is spent, nothing is repaired" for boards, doors, and shutters (2026-10-10, Openings; 2026-10-17, the siege) stands.
- *Primary's note:* how strong the best armour gets is still open (2026-10-18, Health is a thousand; drops by chance; armour modest; weapons differ by situation).

### 2026-10-19 — The cure for the infection is mid to late game, gated by skill (Michael)

On Primary's note that 2026-10-13 ruled the zombie cure "as soon as the ingredients are gathered, not gated by skill", against Faith's Cure Undeath at level 10 (#275):

> "I want to overrule that explicitly, if you can do this on day one zombie bites are just a minor inconvenience. Avoiding zombification once bit should be a mid to late game option."

- **The cure is gated, mid to late game.** It overturns 2026-10-13's "not gated by skill" (struck where it sits) and narrows 2026-10-12's craftable cure at the cauldron (noted where it sits). A bite is more than "a minor inconvenience" on day one: avoiding zombification once bitten is "a mid to late game option".
- *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* "gated by skill" reads the overruling of "not gated by skill"; Michael's own words set the timing. The routes are the skills scope's (#275): Faith's Cure Undeath (level 10, learned in a region) today, and any curing potion the Potions tree may add; how high each sits is for that scope.
- *Primary's note:* the infection and combat difficulty stay one dial (2026-10-12, Combat difficulty and infection are one dial); with the cure late, the dial is where an early bite's cost is tuned.

### 2026-10-19 — A belt slot's key does one thing, into the hand chosen for that slot; no left-click parity (Michael)

On Primary's catch that a press on a belt slot starts a drag, so a left click that also eats or swaps must be told from it:

> "Maybe we remove the left click parity for that reason. That might have gone too far. The other thing is we have to be sure that left clicking a belt slot with an item in it reliably does exactly one thing, so there are no heat of combat surprises. If an item could go to say the left or right hand, we can fix this with a right click or shift right click on the belt slot to choose what it does. So you'd get options:
>
> * Equip to main hand
> * Equip to off hand
>
> And you pick, and until you change it, tapping that item from that slot keeps the choice? Default to main hand with visible refusal ("That can't be held in your main hand") or something like that?"

On Primary's tweaks (the choice in the slot's right-click menu, which already exists; the default the item's own hand rather than always the main hand, so a book is not refused until set; a refusal only when the chosen hand cannot take it): "Agreed on your tweaks".

- **No left-click parity:** a left press on a belt slot stays a drag; it withdraws the left click of the entry below (struck where it sits). A double-click still draws or eats, as before.
- ~~**A belt slot does exactly one thing when used:** a consumable is used; a held item goes to **the hand chosen for that slot**; a carried light swaps into a hand, as before.~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The belt's slots have roles: a slot's role decides) *Primary's reading:* Michael's "left clicking" and "tapping" mean using the slot, by its key or a double-click, since the left press stays a drag; a double-click draws into the chosen hand too (#285 to confirm).
- ~~**The hand is chosen in the slot's right-click menu** ("Equip to main hand", "Equip to off hand", Michael's words; the slot's menu already offers "Equip (main hand)" and "Equip (off hand)", `src/interact.js`; *Primary's note:* the current choice is marked), and the choice stays with the slot until changed. **The default is the item's own hand** (a book or a shield the off hand, a sword the main hand, either-handed the main hand); **a visible refusal comes only when the chosen hand cannot take it.** *Primary's reading:* the choice is per player, kept on the player's record and in the save, and a guest changes it through the same menu command. The build is #285.~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The belt's slots have roles: no hand is chosen; a slot's role decides)

### 2026-10-19 — Rare capstone drops worth farming a boss for, a policy on its condition (Michael)

> "Make sure my thought about rare very powerful capstone items dropping from bosses as incentive to farm them gets written down somewhere. This is something I am prepared to adopt as policy so long as I can find good items that are worth it. A spell shield that acts as a magic book would be a cool idea. A bow that makes its own arrows when you pull the string back I had bandied about. Maybe a sword with some kind of spinning knockback proc chance or something? But you get the idea: power and especially mechanical novelty."

And earlier the same day: "I do want to argue for low drop chance items that make farming bosses worth it. Not the 1/2,000 like OSRS but if the best item drops on every kill, you finish the zone and never go back."

- **A policy, on its condition:** a boss may drop a rare, very powerful capstone item, the reason to farm it, "so long as I can find good items that are worth it". What makes one worth it: "power and especially mechanical novelty". His examples: a spell shield that works as a magic book (the "someday" shield of the entry below); a bow that makes its own arrows as the string is drawn; a sword with a chance of a spinning knockback.
- **Rare, never certain:** far kinder than OSRS's odds, but the best item never drops on every kill. *Primary's note:* it restates 2026-10-18's drops by chance (Health is a thousand; drops by chance; armour modest; weapons differ by situation); #268, #123.

### 2026-10-19 — The belt restated and added to; a book in the off hand costs the shield; the Holy Symbol is Faith's staff, within the proposal (Michael)

On Primary's points about the staff and the book (#275, where the whole shape is a proposal: the staff the school's permission, each book one spell, one book in the off hand at a time):

> "#3 [Primary's third point: a book on the belt, swapped into the hand by its key] answers a question I had had for a while: if belt us a utility spot, why does not not hold and do anything with weapons, which is what people often put on their belts. Answer: you can put them there and tapping the key now swaps it into the appropriate hand.
>
> So for the belt it's:
>
> * Strict utility, no action: carrying a torch there for less light, carrying a lantern there for more light
> * Single action utility, anything consumable: tap key to eat food on belt, or drink potion. Left click should also do whenever hitting the right number key would do if people prefer it
> * Held item quickswap, sword or book or whatever. Certain weapons should strictly not allow this like the halberd, perhaps the greatsword."

> "Your bringing holy symbol into the fold here as a staff stand in is great, accepted. I also like the idea that the casting animation for the holy school keeps the same raise up animation which already makes sense. Like something out of exorcist - holy symbol raised high, reading from the holy book
>
> Utility books fighting for their place with spell books is great IMO, and it forces you to plan for what you are going to do which is what we want.
>
> Go ahead and rule off hand book fine to cost the shield. Maybe someday we will add a special shield that acts as a book for a specific spell. It's probably not 1.0 unless it's some kind of a zone capstone drop, although I do want to argue for low drop chance items that make farming bosses worth it. Not the 1/2,000 like OSRS but if the best item drops on every kill, you finish the zone and never go back."

- **The belt, restated and added to.** As 2026-10-10 ruled (noted where it sits), its key uses up a consumable and swaps a held item "into the appropriate hand" (a sword to the main hand, a book to the off hand; today the code puts an item in its own hand, though with both hands empty a book goes to the main hand). Added: **carried with no action**, a torch there for less light and **a lantern for more**; ~~**a left click on a belt slot does what its key does**, "if people prefer it";~~ (superseded (2026-10-19, A belt slot's key does one thing, into the hand chosen for that slot; no left-click parity): withdrawn, a left press stays a drag) and ~~**the halberd cannot go on the belt, and perhaps the greatsword.**~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The belt's slots have roles: a two-hander goes in a main-hand slot or the utility slot, never an off-hand slot) *Primary's notes for the build:* a press on a belt slot starts a drag today, and a double-click on the belt draws or eats (2026-10-10, night, later still, Double-click, zoom; #179), so the click must be told from both; today the halberd can go on the belt, so the rule belongs in the slot check, which also refuses a guest's slot command (#226).
- **A book in the off hand costs the shield** (ruled). Someday a special shield may work as a book for one spell, probably not 1.0 unless a region's capstone drop (the entry above). *Primary's note:* `docs/24`'s Q2 asked whether a two-handed staff leaves no shield; a book costs the shield either way, so that half no longer decides it.
- **Within the staff-and-book proposal (#275), ruled as part of it:** the Holy Symbol is Faith's permission, as a staff is Magic's ("accepted"), cast with the raise-up pose: "holy symbol raised high, reading from the holy book"; and utility books compete with spell books for room, so a player plans what they will do.
- **Proposed, not ruled:** the staff as the school's permission and each book one spell, one book in the off hand at a time (#275). Open with it: what a hold of right (`docs/21`, the off hand's stance) does with a book in the off hand.

### 2026-10-19 — Unsafe water: a sickness that may come and passes; the skin sips only clean water; crops take any water (Michael)

Answering open question 33 (unsafe water, #279):

> "Maybe we need to add a sickness moodle and effect for just general "I drank stream water and am now sick" type of effect. Makes you more tired, slower to heal, etc. It should be a small overall effect that resolves itself. I would also attach a small but real chance of getting infected with the virus. I also want to point out that a desperation drink of bad water should never GUARANTEE a penalty and it should always still satiate your thirst. So it becomes a real risk/reward judgement."

> "Auto sip only clean water, but don't forbid filling a waterskin with bad water"

> "Crops do not need clean water"

- **Unsafe water is a gamble, never a sure penalty:** it always quenches thirst; it may make you sick, a small effect that passes by itself (more tired, slower to heal), perhaps with a moodle of its own ("Maybe we need to add a sickness moodle", a leaning); and a drink carries a small but real chance of the infection.
- **A second way to catch the infection,** beside a landed bite (2026-10-12, The virus comes back as a marker, which counted bites alone). *Primary's note:* whether water's chance follows the one dial that sets combat difficulty and infection together (2026-10-12) is open, question 34.
- **The waterskin sips by itself only clean water** (2026-10-16's automatic sip, narrowed); a skin may still be filled with unsafe water, and (*Primary's reading*) drunk from by choice, the "desperation drink".
- **Crops take any water;** watering needs no purifying.
- **Still open** (question 34): how water is purified, whether the trough and the font count as natural water, how a skin shows which it holds, and the numbers.

### 2026-10-19 — Natural water is unsafe until purified; mana; drain heals never replace food; a region gates its spells (Michael)

On Primary's four points about the combat trees (#275): (1) Blood Bolt and Siphon Life heal you renewably, against food and potions as the costly heals; (2) Sanctify Food and Water implies water that needs purifying, which would also answer open question 31; (3) Blizzard, Magic's capstone, comes from a region, so a valley-only mage has no capstone; (4) mana doesn't exist yet, though several unlocks assume it, and `docs/24`'s Q2 asks what a spell costs:

> "1. I am fine with this, it will have some kind of a cooldown or a high mana cost. It's meant to be a utility heal, but it should never ever replace food and potions"

> "2. Yes! This is a proposal somewhere and it needs adopting. Water is currently way too easy, I would say all natural water (water tiles, wells) is not safe until purified"

> "3. This is fine for now! I will also add that I don't intend these to be things you just learn automatically when you get there, you can be level 10 magic and still a ways from getting blizzard because you are putting off the snowy region"

> "4. Definitely will need to exist. Slow natural regen, potions to top up, top up free at an altar, maybe special foods later that even restore a trickle of mana"

> "Also, blizzard (like consecrate) should do small aoe damage, but the main reason to use it is utility. Worth noting as well that some of these might be spells you just press rather than coming from a staff and book (maybe) in which case there is no UI for this right now. The belt slots look like the place for it, which makes sense for food and other consumable, but a spell on your belt is a little weird."

- **All natural water is unsafe until purified:** water tiles and wells alike. It answers open question 31 (struck where it sits). *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* purified means boiled at a fire, or sanctified by Faith's Sanctify Food and Water (#275), which his tree says is "not usable on a well", so it purifies carried water. The rest is open question 33 and the build, #279: what unsafe water does to you, whether the waterskin's sip by itself (2026-10-16) drinks only safe water, whether watering crops needs clean water, and whether the trough and the font count as natural water. (Answered in part (2026-10-19, Unsafe water: a sickness that may come and passes; the skin sips only clean water; crops take any water): what it does, the sip, and crops; the rest is question 34.)
- **Mana exists:** slow natural regeneration, potions to top it up, a free top-up at an altar, and perhaps special foods later that restore a trickle. It answers the cost half of `docs/24`'s Q2 (a spell costs mana). *Primary's note:* the altar's top-up gives the valley's altar, a dead end today, a use (#273).
- **A spell that drains health** (Blood Bolt, Siphon Life) is a utility heal, on a cooldown or a high mana cost, and "should never ever replace food and potions".
- **A region gates its spells, for now** ("This is fine for now!"): never by level alone, so a level 10 mage may still be far from Blizzard, "because you are putting off the snowy region". *Primary's reading:* how the region gives the spell (a teacher, a find, a reward) is for each region.
- **Blizzard and Consecrate** do small damage around you, but their point is utility.
- **Open:** how a spell is cast when it comes from no staff or book. The belt fits food and consumables, "but a spell on your belt is a little weird"; there is no place for it yet (#275).

### 2026-10-19 — A player's health stays a hundred, for now; the dead scale freely (Michael)

On the skills trees (#275), when Primary suggested scaling their damage numbers to a thousand health:

> "I actually don't want to do this right now because I am worried it's way too aggressive, I am wondering if instead players shouldn't start with 100 health and it can be improved through leveling and/or gear. Enemies we can scale as much as we want, thousands for bosses, etc. What if certain gear gave you flat max health increases like + 10 or +15 (on a 100 scale)."

- **The ×10 health scale is withdrawn, for now** ("I actually don't want to do this right now"): a player's health stays a hundred. It overturns 2026-10-18's "Health is a thousand" and Primary's reading of it (struck where they sit); that entry's other rulings (drops by chance, weapons by situation, regions returned to) stand. Its armour ruling stands too, but its reason for flat numbers ("1000 lets us play with more literal numbers") is gone: whether flat armour still works on a hundred is open for Michael (#133).
- **The dead and bosses scale as far as they need,** "thousands for bosses".
- **A player's maximum health can grow,** through levels (Faith's "More overall health", #275) and gear, Michael's leaning, not yet ruled: "What if certain gear gave you flat max health increases like + 10 or +15 (on a 100 scale)." *Primary's reading:* flat amounts by default, sized on the hundred.
- *Primary's note:* the reason given for the scale on 2026-10-18 (five foods heal 1, natural healing is 1 a minute, the low end is cramped) still stands, so fine steps at the low end are for #133 and #275 to solve another way, a fractional heal or rate, if they need them.

### 2026-10-19 — Skills may touch combat, open for discussion; death takes them, the one real cost of dying (Michael)

On Primary's skills draft (now #275), which listed "off the battlefield: no skill makes you hit harder" as already ruled:

> "Revoked right off the bad as a law, making these changes at a minimum allowable for discussion."

> "On death, yes it should take skills. As of now this is the only true punishment for dying and we need one. As is you can just walk to your corpse and get your stuff back."

- **"No skill makes you hit harder" is revoked as a law** (2026-10-13, What this game is; struck where it sits, with its "only those that touch non-combat things"; `00`'s two lines rewritten). It is not ruled the other way: whether a skill may touch combat, and how far, is open for discussion in the skills scope (#275).
- **Death takes skills**, as 2026-10-13 said: it is reaffirmed, with its reason. A body's things can be walked back to, so losing what the character had learned is the one real cost of dying. *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* how much death takes (all of it, or a share) is for #275.

### 2026-10-18 — The Valley's final scope is the first priority; finished means sounds (Michael)

Michael, at the end of his first playtest session, for the next morning:

> "When I come back in the morning, remind me that our #1 priority need to be making a final scope for The Valley. We've neem bouncing around between should I work on story and setting, or skilling, or fighting. When we ordered things overall we gave ourselves, the answer, The Valley is proof it works, and it gives us way more than we need to construct The Demo then from the final valley pieces. I think if The Valley, all dev stuff flipped to real values, works as a game the rest will. And finished means sounds if not quite art yet, because demo sounds are easier. In fact this could be hurting my interest in playtesting. Also makes it hard to balance survival in any sense if I unintentionally give myself the "deaf" trait."

- **The first priority is a final scope for The Valley** (#271): one list of what the valley needs to be a finished game, which ends the switching between story and setting, skilling, and fighting. It restates the roadmap's order, the valley first (2026-10-17, The roadmap, and sound: step 1, the valley and skilling; 2026-10-18, The valley first: "the first step in the overall plan"). The list adds to step 1's finish line, which is a feel and not a feature list (2026-10-17), rather than replacing it.
- **Finished means every dev value flipped to real and sounds in,** "if not quite art yet". *Primary's reading:* "demo sounds" means placeholder sounds, not sounds made for The Demo; what they are is open on #271. It narrows 2026-10-17's "free placeholder sounds come in step 2" (noted where it sits): they are part of the Valley's finish, and #271's scope sets when. Michael's reasons: silence "could be hurting my interest in playtesting", and survival is "hard to balance" while he is in effect deaf.
- **The demo is cut from the finished valley's pieces.**

### 2026-10-18 — Health is a thousand; drops by chance; armour modest; weapons differ by situation (Michael)

Michael, on Primary's case for a thousand health (100 is cramped: five foods tie at a heal of 1; healing is 1 a minute; #133's resistances need room):

> "Inflated numbers are good and fun! It feels like doing more damage even if we've adjusted friend and foe by the same amount. We could also allow people to swap some bars to % which makes the scale irrelevant. I think the 1000 is an easy win, you are right 100 is super restrictive. And that allows us to scale bosses too, 10,000 health even, with good gear and weapons, enough mana, maybe the final boss has phases some of which give you time for natural regen. Bigger numbers are a playground, and we can always move the decimal back down if we need. So yes, basically."

> "And drop tables important, one way to make gear harder to get: drop % which is never a thing we have touched before. Again, I don't want it to JUST be zombie runescape, but imagine if the bow dropped the first time you killed a boss every time guaranteed, would you ever do it again? It's a balance to be found somewhere in there. I am hoping regions are also not a thing you do in one sitting, step just inside later to get the one special ingredient you need, and otherwise never return."

> "We also talked at some point about making our armor not extreme. The 'green dragonscale' in OSRS, least powerful just better than nothing gear. Wellll part of that was also simply a necessity of 100 health. 1000 lets us play with more literal numbers and the very occasional %."

> "I know we said we didn't want to do a ton of 'stricly better' type stuff because it's boring but that does not also mean that all weapons need to do the same amount of damage and in every situation. That is part of what resistances are for."

- ~~**Health is a thousand** ("1000 is an easy win"): health numbers ten times today's, a player 1,000.~~ (superseded 2026-10-19, A player's health stays a hundred, for now; the dead scale freely: a player starts at a hundred) **Big numbers are welcome** ("bigger numbers are a playground"), and a boss may have 10,000 health. ~~The decimal can move back if it must.~~ (superseded 2026-10-19, A player's health stays a hundred, for now; the dead scale freely: moot)
  ~~*Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* every health number scales together so the game plays exactly as before, as step one of #133, before the difficulty pass tunes in the finer steps.~~ ~~That means the player's, the dead's, and the villagers' health; weapon damage, bites, bleeding, burning, and starving; food's heals and every healing rate, the villagers' included; and what walls, doors, shutters, boards, and fences can take, with the dead's strikes on them, so a fence's timing (2026-10-17) stays as it is.~~ (superseded 2026-10-19, A player's health stays a hundred, for now; the dead scale freely: the scale is withdrawn)
  **Two things he raised that stay open:** "maybe the final boss has phases some of which give you time for natural regen" meets "no end boss, no win screen" (2026-10-13, The map's choices, and the long arc; `00`), which stands; Primary reads it as a region's last boss, for Michael to say (#123). And "we could also allow people to swap some bars to %": a possibility, perhaps a setting, not ruled.
- **Gear can drop by chance** (#268): a drop chance is a new lever. A boss's best item doesn't drop on every kill, or nobody would fight it twice, but the balance is to be found ("I don't want it to JUST be zombie runescape").
- **Regions are returned to:** "I am hoping regions are also not a thing you do in one sitting, step just inside later to get the one special ingredient you need, and otherwise never return." *Primary's reading:* the hope is that a region keeps drawing players back, not one visit and then a dash in for one ingredient (beside "would you ever do it again?"). A hope, to be designed for (#123).
- **The least armour is just better than nothing,** like OSRS's green dragonhide, and armour works mostly in flat numbers, a percent only now and then, ~~which a thousand health makes possible~~ (superseded 2026-10-19, A player's health stays a hundred, for now; the dead scale freely: whether flat armour still works on a hundred is open, #133). Michael adds that keeping armour weak "was also simply a necessity of 100 health", so how strong the best armour gets is open.
- **Weapons differ by situation:** few strictly-better items, but weapons don't all do the same damage everywhere. Resistances are what makes one weapon strong in one place and weak in another (#133).

### 2026-10-18 — Food rots: a pace, not world decay (Michael)

Michael, on #264's question whether rot meets the clock law's "no world decay": "Rot isn't a world decay problem, it fits like torches that get used up are - it's a design tool to push users into doing upkeep tasks. If I didn't have to cook in Zomboid, I would just eat everything I found on the spot and never stop raiding new houses because it's my favorite thing. We have to pace people a bit."

- **Food rots,** "like torches that get used up", "a design tool to push users into doing upkeep tasks", and not world decay, so it sits within the clock law. It overturns 2026-10-12's "No spoilage." (The big pass answered; struck where it sits). *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* the upkeep it pushes toward is cooking, preserving, and going home.
- **Open, on #264:** whether food nobody has found yet rots (in the valley's chests and fields, which changes the day-one food count and sits nearest the clock law) or only food a player has taken; how fast each food rots, what preserves it (cooking, salting, drying, a cellar), whether a rotten food is still edible at a cost or gone, and a leaner day-one start beside it. The day-one food statistic (the entry below) is how its effect is read.
- *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* "a dry crop pauses" stands; crops dying untended is still only an idea.

### 2026-10-18 — Day-one food is a tracked statistic; what scripting means (Michael)

From Michael's playtest, after starving in the starting room ("this is how many minutes it takes you to die if you spawn in and never leave or open the starting room"):

> "Let's make a rule and wiki statistic somewhere: how long it would take you to starve if all you had is the food that spawned in the valley on day one? This will be instructive for how much exploration it allows, and how much time it takes for you to get farming working and start production chains. We could also tweak this by starting crops in staggered ages so they are not all harvestable on day one and some could die if not upkept by you. Also on hunger remember cooking is supposed to improve the raw food and new regions could give better or stronger foods as an incentive, so starting foods being weak is good."

- **The statistic** (its definition Primary's reading of Michael's words): the hunger in all the food the valley holds on day one (the starting kit, every container's fixed loot and its rolled loot as an average, the fruit on the trees, and the crops ripe at the start), and how long it keeps one player, and two or four sharing a world, from starving, at hunger's drain. The wiki is to keep it on a page generated from the game's data at every build, so it can't fall behind (cmz-wiki, a Day-one food page, its pull request under way); without tools and the kit alone are its two narrower lines. It is a design target: it says how long a new player can explore before farming must work.
- **The count** (the wiki's Day-one food page, from the data at 63ea3be): about 9,400 hunger points, about 94 full stomachs, about 28 real hours awake or 142 game days for one player; about 81 game days without tools. The ripe crops hold about 4,000 and need a shovel or a knife; the 144 containers about 3,950 (every prop that is a container rolls its sprite's table, so shelves, woodpiles, cupboards, carts and the like roll the chest's); the apple trees about 1,400; the kit 70, about 13 minutes. Primary's first count, about 7,800 and 117 game days, missed the containers that are not chests; Michael's words below answer that first number. **The valley is far more generous than this ruling intends.** Michael, on the number: "'roughly 117 game days for one player' empty fields or we introduce rotting lol. I am glad I had this thought, that is wildly imbalanced with no rotting." **Two ways, open:** a leaner start (fewer crops ripe or planted, fewer food rolls in the containers), or **food that rots**. Rot would overturn "no spoilage" (2026-10-12, The big pass answered: "No spoilage.") and is a clock on what you carry and store, so it has to be squared with the clock law's "no world decay" (`00`, Never harder on a clock); Primary's reading is that it can fit, as a property of the food a player chose to gather and store (the way a torch burns down), not a decay of the world, with preserving (cooking, salting, a cellar) as the answer and a production chain of its own. For Michael to rule.
- **The starting foods are weak on purpose:** cooking improves raw food (it fills more and heals more: `02`, 2026-10-18, The valley first), and the regions could offer better and stronger foods as a reason to go (Michael's "could", #123).
- **Crops in staggered ages are already so in part:** the valley's crops are placed at all three stages, not all ripe. **Crops dying if not kept up is an idea, not ruled:** today a dry crop pauses and never dies (2026-10-12, The big pass answered: "a dry plot pauses, never dies"; `data/crops.json`), and a crop that dies on a timer meets the clock law's "no world decay" (`00`, Never harder on a clock). A crop the player planted and let go dry is arguably the player's choice; Primary's flag, for Michael.

**What scripting means** (Michael, the same day): "when I asked for scripting and an issue got made a while back, I didn't just mean lines of dialogue, it meant NPCs who can move around and bark in response to things, as a set routine, and possibly even at your command." *Primary's reading:* the scripted sequences of 2026-10-13 (a beat list in the host's step, never a cutscene) and the villagers' routines of the next NPC pass (#103) are one want: villagers who move on a set routine, react to what happens around them with movement and a bark, and possibly do as a player asks. The NPC pass is scoped with it (#103). **And more** (Michael, later the same day): "Add to what I meant by scripting: controlling camera and player character. So basically in-world cut scenes, is what I am gesturing at. ... Sparsely used but important for conveying any sense of story, which OSRS also does." That meets 2026-10-13's "never a cutscene" (The map and talk scoped), which stands until Michael rules; an in-world scene keeps the one continuous map (no cut to another place), but taking a player's camera and character in a live, shared world is open (#106).

### 2026-10-18 — Every item in a grid has a hover card (Michael)

From Michael's playtest: "A lot of our buttons are super cramped, even showing unreadable gibberish as the short name for a 2-3 word title. They also tell you literally nothing about what they are. I propose as a universal rule, anything in an inventory (you, zombie, npc, output of stations, et al), really anything sized as a puzzle piece in a grid, should get a hover bubble explaining the basics. The full name and direct uses at least. 'Use' on an object to light it, etc." And: "Give the word Use in these caps and the same yellow color as actually using an item." Asked whether to log it as a standing rule beside "Item art comes as a pair": yes.

- **The rule:** every item drawn as a piece in a grid or slot (your bag, belt, hands, and worn slots; a chest; a body, of the dead or a villager; a station's output; anything else drawn so) shows a hover card with at least its **full name** and its **direct uses**. It is a law in `CLAUDE.md`, beside the item art law, so every new item is checked against it.
- **Use** is written so, capital U as in the menu, and drawn in the Use mode's gold (`--gold`, the outline on the item being used).
- *Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule:* the uses are read from the data (a food's heal and hunger, a tool's actions, a weapon's numbers, what a material is made into and where), never written by hand per item, so the card cannot fall behind the game. The card is drawn from what a guest's replica already holds, so a guest sees what the host sees. The build, the card's look, and its reach beyond grids (the build palette, recipe rows, props in the world) are #262.

### 2026-10-18 — Food heals what it says on the tin; a full belly heals faster (Michael)

From Michael's playtest: "food still heals you but cannot fill your fullness past capacity. It feels unfair to stop you from using it to heal AND since production chains are part of the game, if you are scarfing a barrel of apples a day they will not last long. So in other words, hunger hurts you, but you can eat as much as you want." And on Primary's bounded-by-appetite scaling: "I don't like total caps or scaling nerfs, I think it should heal what is says on the tin, like in OSRS."

- **Food is eaten whenever you like and heals exactly its number;** hunger stops at the top. "You are full." is gone. It overturns #186's refusal and Primary's scaling (struck where they sit, under "The valley first"). Food still heals a little and potions big: that ruling stands.
- **The cost is the food:** the same supply must feed hunger and heal, "two systems fighting one another for the same resource". That is why healing potions matter, why the start must never hand out a bagful of either, and why food must take work to get (the skills).
- **Natural healing is faster well fed:** "your natural healing should heal faster when full than when starved." *Primary's reading, agreed by Michael:* a bonus for being well fed, never a new penalty for hunger. At `player.fullAt` (90 of 100) or more, health comes back `player.wellFedRegenMult` times as fast (2, Primary's default), awake or asleep; fed below that, the rate is unchanged; past "hungry" or "thirsty" it stops, as before. With the Survival setting off the needs stay full, so a body there is always well fed.
- **An idea, not ruled:** a food that heals and gives a small bonus besides.

### 2026-10-18 — Remote bodies on a guest are drawn at a fixed delay behind the host's frames (#248; the builder's reading, Michael's to tune)

On a guest the host's player stopped for a step every sixth frame (#244's review): a body walked to each frame's position in a fixed three steps, and frames reach a guest irregularly, so a late frame left it standing and an early one hurried it. Measured (`tools/netsmooth.js`, and two real tabs on the loopback): a body now moves evenly whatever the arrival. Built as `src/netsmooth.js`: each remote body keeps the last positions the host sent with the host's sim time and is drawn **`feel.netDelaySteps` (4) steps behind the newest frame**, interpolated between the two either side (its speed is the host's), the guest's draw clock nudged toward the frames' clock (`feel.netSlew`), a jump over `feel.netJump` tiles put where it landed. **The cost is about one step (17 ms) more delay on remote bodies** (by the review's measure the drawn body trails the newest frame by about 1.9 steps against about 1.0 for the old walk, so the added delay is about one step; the tool's 3.0 to 3.5 counts the wire's four steps of latency too): 3 steps stalls on a late frame, more only adds delay. My own player is predicted and unchanged.

### 2026-10-18 — The feel numbers are data, and the flying things are blended too (#161's follow-ups; the builder's, Michael's to tune)

**`data/tuning.json` `feel`:** `cameraFollow` (10 a second: how fast the camera closes on the player; it trails a walking player 0.3 tiles, doubling it halves that), `blend` (true: the draw between steps; false draws the last step as it stood, no added delay and the unevenness on a 75, 120, or 144 Hz display), and `blendJump` (1.5 tiles: a move farther than this in a step is drawn where it landed). **There is no input acceleration to tune:** a held key is instant velocity, the arcade feel, by design (`check("feel")`). **Arrows, thrown items, and the fireball** (`archery.arrowsInFlight`, which carry a height `z`) are marked in `interp` with the characters, their position and height blended together, so on a smooth display nothing flying is the one stepped thing left. Free particles (splinters, loose embers) and a guest's replica of the arrows (rebuilt each frame) stay stepped.

### 2026-10-18 — Movement feel: the draw is blended between steps (#161; the builder's reading, Michael's to overrule)

Measured first (`check("feel")`): diagonals are already normalised (3.00 tiles a second along the screen), input is instant velocity (the arcade feel; the first step already moves), the camera trails a walking player by 0.30 tiles (19 px, a time constant of a tenth of a second), and the drawn x of the player is within half a pixel of steady. **The fault was the missing blend:** the loop steps at a fixed 60 a second and the draw used the last step as it stood, so on a 144, 120, or 75 Hz display some frames scrolled nothing and the next three pixels (a spread of 1.0 to 1.2 px against 0.2 to 0.4 with the blend), and one late frame at 60 Hz was a hitch (spread 0.76 against 0.43 with 2 ms of jitter). **Fix:** the characters and the camera are drawn `loop.alpha()` of the way from where the previous step left them to where the last one did (`interp.js`), a draw **up to one step (16.7 ms) behind, fixed for a session at 60 Hz** by where the steps fall against the frames (the reviewer measured 2 to 16 ms; a tap's first motion at 15.8 ms in the slow case against 8.5 ms unblended, and about 8 ms averaged over sessions), which is the price of the evenness and is weighed against "snappy"; the camera's whole-pixel origin and the sprites' whole-pixel positions are kept (pixel-perfect). **Left alone:** the camera's trail (0.3 tiles) and the instant-velocity input, which are feel, Michael's to say whether he wants either changed.
### 2026-10-18 — Hiding a worn item's art: an eye on each worn slot (#159; Primary's defaults for Michael's belt wish, Michael's to overrule)

Michael (2026-10-17): a belt display setting, "if you think belt looks dumb on you and want the benefit without it covering part of your chest". Built as **the wearer's choice, seen by everyone** (WoW's show-helm), not a Display setting: an **eye on each worn slot whose item is drawn on the figure** (head, chest, hands, legs, feet, waist, back), kept as the character's `hidden` list in the save and the player's record; hidden, the item's art is left out of the figure's sheet (so its `covers` rule no longer applies either) and **the item keeps every use** (the belt's slots, a bag's size). Only a slot with something worn can be hidden. The eye is drawn in CSS, so it needs no art.

### 2026-10-18 — A hurt villager heals at the player's rate (Michael, in chat, #236)

On a villager the dead have hurt: **"slow refill like players, and they keep any dialogue that is programmed."** Built as `villager.regenPerMinute` (1, the player's `healthRegenPerMinute`: villagers have no needs, so they are always fed and watered) in the world's step on the authority, **never above their maximum and not for `villager.regenPause` (10) seconds after a hit** (so health does not climb while the dead are still biting); a dead one does not heal. The pause is the builder's reading, Michael's to overrule.

### 2026-10-18 — The map panel: Space stays the shove (#204; the builder's reading, Michael's to overrule)

`docs/33` named `Space` as the way back to your marker while the map is open. **Space is the shove** (`docs/21`), and the map does not stop the world (Michael, 2026-10-15: with a panel open the player can still fight), so **Space is not bound in the map**: **the Centre button and a double-click on the map follow you again**, and zooming or panning off-centre stops following until one of them. A place's name is drawn once any tile of its footprint's box is charted and a name that would sit on another's is left out, so the smallest zoom shows the larger places first.

### 2026-10-18 — A wounded villager still talks and barks (Michael, in chat, #230)

On "a villager the dead have hurt can never be talked to again": **"Yes and yes, slow refill like players, and they keep any dialogue that is programmed."** So **only a dead villager is refused**: Talk is offered and starts for a wounded one, and the ambient bark and the poke's bark are not withheld for low health (`startTalk`, `ambientBark`, the menu's entry); a villager struck (staggered by a blow) still ends a conversation they are in, as one turned or dead does. The health refill is #236.

### 2026-10-18 — The chart: a wall is charted from either side, and a change within reach looks again (#234's review; the reviewer's design, Michael's to overrule)

`docs/33` said a cell holds the two walls its own tile owns. **A cell may now hold walls its tile has not been seen for**: a wall is charted when a tile on either side of it has been seen, so a building seen only from outside has all four faces on the map (its north and west faces belong to tiles inside it), and a cell can be wall-only (surface 0). **A player looks again when anything changes within `ceil(chartRadius / 16)` chunks of them**, not only when they change tile, so a door opened in front of someone standing still shows what is behind it. The first reveal in uncharted land is 1.4 to 3.8 ms against the spec's bracketed 1 ms, accepted without a cache; whole chunks on the wire cost about 14 KB/s to every guest from one walking player, with changed cells only left as a follow-up.

### 2026-10-18 — The mohawk revised: (a) soft (Michael's pick; an art fix)

Michael on the mohawk: "The front 3/4 and back 3/4 views look a little wonky in terms of how much mohawk you can see and the side on view makes the hair a little pointier than I wanted. Front on and back on are perfect." Of the art agent's two revisions he picked the first: "a soft is great!" The three-quarter facings now carry the fin along the head's centreline with its far end out of sight behind the skull, the side profile is slightly rounded, and the front and back are pixel for pixel as they were. The generator (`hair_mohawk()` in `tools/art-gen/character/heads.py`) is the source and reproduces the layer.

### 2026-10-18 — The Onion-head Hat's art: the brow; a dyed hood is dyed like hair, and the dead wear it too (Michael's pick; Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule)

Michael picked the art agent's candidate **(b) brow** of three: "b brow is perfect!" (the opening cut round where the game draws the eyes in every facing, the fold raised above the brow; `data/art/character/layers/head_onion_b_brow.png`, its generator `tools/art-gen/character/onion2.py`). It is the painted head layer `onion` (`data/layers/head/onion.json`), worn by the item `hood` (`art.painted`, colours `#5f8a3a #46682a`). *Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule:* **a dyed hood is dyed like hair**: a painted head item worn with an entry's dye (the villagers' three hoods, `#6b7a3a`, `#b08a3e`, `#8a6a3a`; the dead's two) takes the dye's colour for the light tone and its shade for the dark, by the hair's own rule (`SHADE_DYE`), the outline kept; worn in its own colours (the player's) it keeps the painted greens; a layer the item does not name (a try-on, the dead's pool) is never dyed, nor a layer of more than two tones (the outline aside), so a delivered straw hat or great helm keeps its paint (the review of #229). **The dead wear what they wear**: an item's painted layer draws on one of the dead as on anyone, in that one's dye, and wins over the dead's painted pool for its slot. The hat's bag icon and its 38 by 38 portrait are still owed (#182).
### 2026-10-18 — Talk, part 2: what E talks to, and who is held (#202; the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

**`E` talks to the nearest villager within reach** (their tile against a door's or a window's, the nearest wins; the prompt names which), and the menu's Talk to is the same simple action. ~~A villager hurt cannot be talked to~~ (overturned the same day by Michael's ruling below: a wounded villager still talks) **and one with the dead within five tiles barks instead**; **a conversation ends when the villager is hurt, turned, or dead, or the talker walks out of reach and a tile or loses the clear line** (the host's step drops them, so a lost message cannot hold a villager). **A villager stands for a fixed second after the last talker leaves**, not a rolled idle time, so a conversation never shifts the sim's generator (`check("talk")` proves the generator and the world's text are the same after every path of the guide's file).

### 2026-10-18 — One reach test for every action (#208; Primary's ruling from #206, the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

Over a fence or through an open window is in reach; a wall or a shut door or shutter is not, which keeps a sealed room a cell (#147): `src/reach.js`, distance and a clear line to the nearest tile, asked by the menu's actions (the timed action's start, its continuing, and the auto-walk's arrival), a craft's station **and the chests that feed it**, the panels' closing, a poke, the bed, the fire, the chop, and the farming verbs. *The builder's readings:* an action behind a wall **walks round** to a place with a clear line (as one out of range walks over), rather than refusing; a chest in the next room is no ingredient source; **building and removing a wall keep their distance-only reach** (they act on edges, which are the walls themselves). **Michael's ruling on that (2026-10-18, in chat, asked by #227's review): "Oh yes, removing wall and placing door are fine."** A player may stamp a door into a wall, or Remove a wall they built, from inside or outside a sealed room: the sealed-room law is about the dead (nothing digs, no wall breaks), not about trapping the player. **Primary's ruling (Michael's to overrule): Remove on a made prop and putting down a carried prop need a clear line too** (`placement.removeReachProblem`, `putDownReach`), so a chest or a station cannot be taken down, nor a carried one set down, through a wall. The review of #227 also found the walk-round was only claimed: `approachTile` now skips a tile with no clear line to the target, a bracket on its far side (`side` -1) is reached from its own side, and four valley containers boxed in by a hearth, a table, and two walls (`valley.py`) were moved, with `node tools/check.js` now asserting every container, station, and bed in every map has a tile to stand on.
### 2026-10-18 — Talk, part 1: the guide's name and place, and what the first bark is (#198; the spec's, with the builder's defaults, Michael's to overrule)

Built as #198 from `docs/34` steps 1 and 2. *The builder's and Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule:* **the guide is Wyn**, a neighbour from the farm lane who came to your yard when the bell stopped and has not gone home (a placeholder name and reason, as every villager's), standing **just outside your cottage's door**, in view on stepping out and under three tiles from the spawn, **in a wardrobe of their own**; the file's id is `guide`, not the name, so the name can change without touching it. **The first thing the guide says is a welcome, not a warning:** the lane dead who stood within the 8-tile `deadNear` of the spawn and the guide were moved down the lane (to (86, 212), beyond a wandering guide's reach too), so a first poke is the pointer bark. **The staging map has a guide too**, though its header said no villagers: `34` says every map the generator makes, and `write` (and the validator, for a hand-edited map) refuses one without exactly one within three tiles of its spawn; the hand-written proving ground has none. **A villager's talk id is the map's `talk`, else the name lowercased**, so the seven need no field in the map. **A bark takes its turn from a counter for its group** (`barkTurns`, host bookkeeping a guest never reads; the delta record does not carry it). **A speech line ages in the world's step on the authority** (#154, folded in: a server world never runs `ui/speech.js`), so a bark ends on its own on every host. A talk file may carry a `note`. The guide's topics are the systems on `main` (mining, the bucket, and cloth now); the farms' seed and the millstones follow #219.

### 2026-10-18 — The tool rule built: the defaults of #215 (Primary's, Michael's to overrule)

Built as #215 from the rule below. *Primary's defaults and the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule:* **a hammer** for building a door or a window, boarding up, pulling a board (which now **gives the plank back**) and Remove; **a hatchet** for a log wall and for splitting a log; **the workbench's rows ask by verb** (`uses`): a hammer for furniture, the wheelbarrow, the shield, the hoe and the shovel, a knife for the bow, the arrows and the rod; **harvest by tool**: rye the scythe, potatoes and garlic the shovel, cabbage and pumpkin the knife, apples by hand. **The builder's reading:** *sticks from a plank* ask for the hatchet, not the knife the issue named, because splitting wood is hatchet work (and the anvil's knife needs a stick, so a stick that needed the knife would circle until the weaver's knife; the hatchet is in your cottage's chest from the start). Michael may want the knife back. **Free building waives the build tools** (the hammer for a door, a window, and Remove; the hatchet for a log wall) as it waives the cost, **but not boarding up or pulling a board**, which Michael's 2026-10-11 ruling left outside it ("planks for boards are untouched"); the setting's help line says so. **A tool is found by its verb** (`carriedTool`), so a new tool needs only its data. The joiner's chest gains a hammer (the first hoe needs one), and **your cottage's chest a shovel** (the first potatoes dig on day one; the cabbages wait for a knife, a reason to go out, not a dead end); a waterskin on the belt is filled at a well; the tinderbox may ride the belt.

### 2026-10-18 — An action that needs a tool needs the tool with you (Michael)

"Any action that should realistically require a tool should require you to have the tool in your inventory, if not also equipped, tbd. That is the OSRS inventory puzzle part of the game which Zomboid does not have, because it uses weight to balance inventory size. You shouldn't be able to carry every tool for every activity AND all of your combat gear, except maybe in the dev bag." **A general rule:** an action that would realistically need a tool needs that tool in the inventory (*Primary's reading:* the bag, the belt, or a hand); whether it must also be in a hand is open (open question 32). The bag's size, not weight, is what makes the choice: what you carry for a trip is a puzzle, and the Developer Bag may be the exception. *Primary's reading:* the stricter rules already built stand until the hand question is ruled (chopping needs the hatchet in a hand, #195; fishing the rod, #190; tilling the hoe, #194); the audit of every action against this rule is its own issue.

The rulings #201 forced, *Primary's and the builder's defaults, Michael's to overrule* (the build itself is the pull request and `docs/29-world/05`): **the farms are compact, because the reserves are smaller than the plans** (Hollow Farm fills its 64 by 26 reserve; the plan's weir, millpond, croft, byre, pigsty and the longer crop list are left for their own passes); **flour is two grain to one** at the millstones, which burn nothing; **a pumpkin and flour bake a pumpkin pie** at the oven (the pumpkin was the one crop no recipe used, so `validate-data.py` now fails a dead-end crop); **the granaries hold seed of every crop**, fixed and never refilled (a harvest gives seed back, so the fields are the renewable source); **the great barn's tool rack holds a hoe** (the hoe is wooden and craftable, so this is a way in on day one, not a gate; the scythe stays the smithy's, #200); **the miller is alive and not immune** (he teaches nothing yet), so the dead may take him.

Built as #212 from `docs/30` sections 3, 4 and 6. **Flax is a sixth crop** (rye's days, three and three; its seed and its fibre) and **the loom makes cloth** (two flax a cloth, `data/recipes.json`), then bandages (two from a cloth) and clothes (a cap from one cloth, breeches from two, a tunic from three). **Rags are torn from a garment at the workbench** (a rag is never torn again) and stand in for cloth wherever a recipe asks the `cloth` tag (a torch, the fishing rod). *Primary's default, from Michael's tool rule ("any action that should realistically require a tool should require you to have the tool in your inventory, if not also equipped", PR #214):* tearing wants **a knife on you**, a recipe field `carry` (in a hand, on the belt, or in the bag; `tool` stays "in hand"). *The builder's default, Michael's to overrule:* **a way in on day one**: the weaver's chest holds six flax seed and a knife (a fixed loot table the placed chest names, as the hatchet's did), so the first cloth needs no other source; the farms (#201) add the rest of the seed sources. Dev boxes stand in for the crop's three stages (the art ask is `29-world/09`'s).

Eating from the belt ("being able to eat from your belt perhaps?") is already ruled (2026-10-10, late: food and drink on a belt slot, its key eats or drinks one); the build makes sure it works.   *Built as #179, the builder's numbers (Primary's defaults, Michael's to tune; `heal` in `data/items.json`):* raw 1 to 2 (potato 1, cabbage 1, garlic 1, apple 2, pumpkin 2), cooked 4 to 6 (roast potato 4, cheese 4, bread 5, porridge 6), stew 10; a food on the belt is eaten from its slot menu or a double-click, a guest's through the same menu command.
