- **~~Magic and potions are~~ Magic is learned from a villager, the wizard:** (narrowed 2026-10-19, The valley has no cure; the wizard lives; the witch teaches Potions; gates stop a harvest; every loot table provisional; two old limits struck: the witch teaches Potions) "your stereotypical friendly wizard, big bushy white beard (a chance to bring facial hair that back perhaps) and blue wizard robes and hat. Or similar. It pushes against the vibe clash between valley and regions." His "perhaps" leaves the beard open against 2026-10-13's "facial hair after 1.0" (noted where it sits). Learning ~~magic and potions~~ magic from him (narrowed 2026-10-19, The valley has no cure; the wizard lives; the witch teaches Potions; gates stop a harvest; every loot table provisional; two old limits struck: the witch teaches Potions) is how recipes now work in general (below). He may give the Staff of Fire, the Book of Fire, or one of them (open).
- **The dead strike what they break** ("we might w[a]nt to"; built as #174 and kept by Michael on #185 after he saw it): damage to a gate, door, or shutter becomes "an actual attack, with a damage number, and an animation, just like when they attack you... on a short cooldown", so a crowd is more strikes rather than a vibration that bursts; and the wall's shake "simmer[ed] down". **Michael's ruling on #185, having kept the model: "Keep it: one zombie alone can break a fence section slowly, like a door; a crowd is faster."**
- **Recipes are learned and earned, not all known from the start:** "We definitely do need to overrule finally the recipes from the start thing. Else it eliminates a whole category of rewards from regions." A recipe can come from a villager who teaches it (the wizard's magic ~~and potions~~ among them (narrowed 2026-10-19, The valley has no cure; the wizard lives; the witch teaches Potions; gates stop a harvest; every loot table provisional; two old limits struck: the witch's Potions)) or as a region's reward; *Primary's addition:* or from a skill. Whether a learned recipe may be power, not flavour, is open (rewards are mostly sideways, #123). Teaching sits beside "talk never gates" (2026-10-13, noted where it sits) as its own act at the teacher, *Primary's reading*. It overturns 2026-10-12's "Recipes all known" and 2026-10-13's "every recipe you need to play effectively from the start" (struck where they sit).
- **The skill feel test** is part of the valley's: "do I feel helpless on low skills, and super overpowered in terms of production on high skills?"
- **Bag art first:** the items still on placeholder art ("apple" and "log") get flat bag icons, the cheap win. *Primary's reading:* the pair rule (2026-10-12: an item's world picture and icon from one design) stands, so each ask carries both, the icon first.
- **Farming's dryness lives on the crop** (#194, the builder's reading, Michael's to overrule; `docs/30` section 8 said "each soil tile has `dry`"): a crop carries the time its soil stays wet until, watered soil is wet two game days, a dry crop pauses and never dies; empty soil is not tracked. **Every farming verb needs the tile in reach with no wall between** (Primary's ruling on the review of #194: a closed wall, door, or shutter stops it, a fence and an open window do not; the apple pick the same; containers and wells are their own issue), so a sealed room stays a cell. It keeps the save and the sync hash to the prop (no per-tile state on the floors) and loses nothing a player can see, since only a crop's growth depends on water.
- **Apple trees and fishing join valley 1.0** (#190): "Do Apples have a source? They should come from certain trees and be pickable in some fashion and this makes it part of production, which needs to be upkept. Also I think there's a fishing issue in the backlog let's wrap that into this and make it part of valley 1.0?" Apples now come from trees; fishing, ruled "yes, later" on 2026-10-12 (noted where it sits), moves out of `docs/30` step 9 into the valley's first step. *Built as #190, apple trees, the builder's numbers (Michael's to tune; `pick` in `data/sprites/tree_apple.json`):* a pick gives 3 to 5 apples after 2 s and leaves the tree bare; it bears again 3 clock days later (a day is 720 s; sleep runs the clock fast, so a night is a day's share); chopping is any tree's (2 logs, the fruit lost); the orchard at Hollow Farm is 20 trees in staggered rows at (38, 232), and a tree stands in each of three cottage gardens; dev art, the art ask is #182. **Trees off removes every tree, an orchard's included, so there are no apples from trees in such a world** (Primary's reading, Michael's to overrule: Trees off is a testing mode that already removes all wood; the setting's help line says so). *Built as #190, fishing, the builder's readings (Michael's to tune; every number is in `data/fishing.json`):* a fishing rod (a tool, a stick and a cloth at the workbench, Primary's default until thread exists); Cast the line on a water tile within reach with the rod in a hand, a wait shown as the bar (walking away ends it), then a roll on the table of that water, a miss allowed; the river, the village pond, and the mere (for when it is built) each have a table; three fish, each grilled at a fire for about twice the hunger and a small heal (#179's scheme); a full bag drops the catch at the player's feet. **Fishing has no skill yet** (the skills are open, `docs/30`). The map names its waters, so the tables follow the water and not a guess; art for the rod and the fish is owed as a pair each (#182).
- **The starting kit's readings are his, not defaults** (his word on #183, "Agree on all builder defaults"): a world *adds* to `data/kit.json` and does not replace it; the dev bag is worn in place of the sack, not carried as an extra; the pause menu's World rules screen does not list the kit. Built as #136 (2026-10-17's entry above).

### 2026-10-18 — Mining, the quarry, the iron workings, and charcoal (#196, #197; Primary's defaults and the builder's readings, Michael's to overrule)

**Mining, the quarry, and the iron workings** (#196; `docs/30` section 10, `docs/29-world/06`; Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule): a pickaxe in a hand mines a rock for three stone and the rock goes for good; ~~**an ore rock gives two iron ore and stands spent for three clock days, then comes back (the same mechanism as a picked apple tree), so iron never runs out** while common rocks may go for good, the forest being large.~~ (Overturned 2026-10-25, Natural resources ruled: a mined rock or ore rock is gone, and a new one rises elsewhere in its area, the count kept.) **No chicken and egg:** the iron workings' miners' hut holds a pickaxe and a crate of ore, the quarryman's a pickaxe and a hammer, **the smithy's own chest the first iron (three bars and a sack of charcoal for its fire, #197)**. The two places are in the east woods and the north-east; their cliffs are `wall_stone` (a `fence` feature may now run on some sides only), the mine mouth, spoil heaps, winch, crane, cut stone, ore cart, and ore rocks dev boxes until drawn; ten of the dead are about them. Counts, seconds, and days are data in the rock definitions (`mine`).

**Charcoal: the clamp, the burners, and a clamp in the smithy's yard** (#197; Primary's defaults, Michael's to overrule): the charcoal clamp is a 2 by 2 station with a slow fire of its own (one log a game day, almost no light) and one recipe, **four logs to three charcoal over half a game day** with the fire lit (a log of fuel, a flame carried, #169's rule), so it is fed, lit, and left: the semi-idle rhythm. **One stands in the smithy's yard** (the clamp as a placed prop rather than a recipe), so the smith's loop needs no walk to the east woods; the burners' clearing south of the iron workings has three, sacks of charcoal, a hut with a chest of them, and three of the dead. No smoke is drawn on a clamp yet (`fire.js`'s smoke is the burning dead's).

### 2026-10-17 — A belt torch sees a bit further than none (Michael, #158)

On #158: "a belt torch should see a bit further than no torch; tune its radius above the dark radius." A belt torch's radius was `world.beltLight` 0.5 of a torch's 3, 1.5 tiles, exactly `sight.darkRadius`, so it added nothing to how far you see the dead. **Ruled and built:** `beltLight` **0.6** (1.8 tiles), one number, Primary's default, for Michael to overrule. It also lifts the belt torch's light and how far the dead see it a little, so 2026-10-10's "half its light, seen from half as far" reads "a little over half" from today (noted where it sits). The other choice, a sight-only factor that leaves that ruling untouched, was set aside for one number; say so if the dead seeing the belt torch from farther is unwanted. `check("sight")` has the row.

### 2026-10-17 — The starting kit as data and a world choice (Michael, 2026-10-16 on #123)

Michael's ruling stands as made ("editable in the before-you-enter world settings? For testing? It can start with the default, whatever is there now plus tinderbox, and then for testing you can add more"; "a good place to put a 'start with dev bag' question"). Built (#136): the kit is `data/kit.json`; a world *adds* to it (a list of `item_id count` lines, placed by first fit), it does not replace it; **the dev bag is worn in place of the sack** (so the added items have room), not carried as an extra; both are rules chosen once, read by the host when a new character is made; the dev chest was already the Sandbox step's switch and is left as it is; the pause menu's World rules screen does not list the kit (nothing about it can change after the world is made). *Those three readings are Michael's rulings, not defaults: see 2026-10-18.*

### 2026-10-17 — The roadmap, and sound (Michael)

**The order of the work**, Michael's, with Primary's changes: two he adopted (strangers play before any money; the Steam page as soon as there is real art), one he clarified (step 1's finish line, below), and his own "4.5" made the plan, which he took with "write the roadmap": (1) the valley and skilling; (2) zombies and combat, until every weapon to be had in the valley feels good against the standard dead; (3) the demo; (4) playing together and telemetry, before the outside playtest (his evening with his brother over a server world first: "I need to prove that client to server to client works with another person, and better yet he has sketchier internet than me"); (5) the first outside playtest, on dev art; (6) Midnight Mass in dev art, the vertical slice, and a second playtest; (7) the name, before money ("I must stop here and pick a final name before I throw money at assets"); (8) art and sound, the paid phase ("I am happy to rule out directional sound now, but the zombies should make some noise, plus interface, fire, actions, walking, et al."); (9) the Steam page and wishlists; (10) the regions and release. `TODO.md` holds it, and a GitHub milestone per step. *Primary's readings, Michael's to overrule:* the demo built at step 3 is a dev-art build for playtests, and the public demo keeps 2026-10-13's final art (step 9's Next Fest); an invited playtest is not "public" in 2026-10-14's sense, so the playtests may come before the name.

- **Step 1's finish line** is a feel, not a feature list: "I can load a session and play skillscape for a good while vibing with production and stocking up and making things neat and building... The experience of doing this part should FEEL fulfilling to do and no big hiccups or resource stoppages, or dead ends. It doesn't need to have final dev art or be perfect or perfectly balanced it just needs to feel like playing a game, not testing a game." *Primary's test of it:* an hour of play with no dead end; what breaks the hour goes on a list.
- **Free placeholder sounds come in step 2,** for feel and immersion; paid sound is step 8. (Narrowed 2026-10-18, The Valley's final scope is the first priority; finished means sounds: placeholder sounds are part of the Valley's finish, and when they come is set by its scope, #271.)
- **Sound and sight:** a sound that carries information may have a visual twin where it makes sense, but "I don't want to require that the player can always see or hear it in every case. There SHOULD be a penalty for playing with sound off." Michael: "when I play WoW or OSRS I am very often watching a youtube or a show or a movie and I play with the sound turned fully off... So feel free to push back on me on sound in the future. Be the sound on advocate between us."
- **Every sound on the wiki:** "anything in the game that makes a sound should have the sound as a playable file on that wiki page... So long as it does not break any license rules." A sound whose license does not let the wiki host it stays off the wiki, not out of the game.

### 2026-10-17 — Torches burn down; the tinderbox; the village's lights become lanterns (Michael)

From the same talk: "burning a torch now needs to deplete it, but it should be far less than using it as a weapon, but it drains the whole time it's lit. If it doesn't deplete though then you never need to make more and never need to bring a tinderbox to relight or light new ones. The cold zone can also play with 'puts out or dampens your torch' effects." On the village's own torches, which the clock law keeps from burning out by themselves: "Why don't we swap the village torches to 'lanterns' or 'lamps' and I think that will both explain why they don't go out, and also create a cool new light item that can someday do more stuff possibly. So lanterns, placed on map for now, do not go out, emit light in the light engine. More than a torch? Torch: can still be placed, but goes out. Perhaps placed goes out even more slowly than carried. Right now putting a torch on your belt frees your hand but lowers the light, let's also give the benefit of it getting the 'wall' rate of decay, not the faster held rate." He took Primary's shape as laid out ("take the torch and lantern shape as you laid it out"):

- **A torch burns down while it is lit:** fastest in a hand; at the slower "wall" rate on the belt, in a bracket, or on a post; a kill costs a much larger bite, as a weapon's durability does now. Spent, it is gone. It overturns 2026-10-09's "torches never burn out" (struck where it sits).
- **A torch is doused and lit again with a tinderbox** (T lights, with a tinderbox carried, and douses), and **a new torch starts unlit**. **So does the starting torch:** "Start unlit: the starting torch begins unlit and you light it with your tinderbox when you want it" (Michael, 2026-10-17, on #157). **A torch that leaves a hand, the belt, or a holder is doused** (the builder's reading of "it drains the whole time it's lit", Primary's default on #157, for Michael to overrule): no free relight from the bag.
- **Belt and hand never stack:** a player gives off one carried light, the held torch winning (two in hand at the stronger light, both burning); a lit torch on the belt while another is held burns for nothing. (*Primary's reading* (2026-10-19, The belt restated and added to; a book in the off hand costs the shield; the Holy Symbol is Faith's staff, within the proposal): a carried lantern counts as a carried light too.)
- **The village's torches become lanterns:** placed on the map for now, ~~never going out~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: a lantern can be put out and lit again), a little brighter than a torch and steady where a torch flickers, a light item that may do more someday.
- **The cold region may dampen or put out torches** (an idea for the regions' draft, #123).

### 2026-10-17 — Only characters burn; fire lights what is made to be lit; the fireball as a flare (Michael)

From his chat with Primary: "a fireball lights the way a bit as it travels. We could up that a bit and give it a second life as a utility flare for lighting areas up quickly. Especially if it lands on something it can set on fire. That feels awesome." He then warned against "unexplainable one-off rules", since only characters burn. Primary's synthesis, which he took ("Your light vs burn synthesis is perfect. Now it can do both without spillover."):

- **Only bodies burn** (as ruled with burning, #14). **Anything with fire lights what is made to be lit:** a torch, a tinderbox, and a fireball all turn a campfire or a torch lit (the Light interaction; a tinderbox and an unlit torch from the entry above), never burning or destroying it. Nothing spreads (spreading fire is in `05`, if ever).
  **Lighting a fire from its own menu needs a flame carried too** (Michael, 2026-10-17, on #168, found by #165's review): "lighting a campfire, fireplace, oven, or forge from its menu needs a tinderbox or a lit torch." Built with the light verb's flame rule (`inventory.flameOf`); fuel is still needed; feeding one that burns needs no flame. It settles the reading below: a fire now needs a flame to start, so the tinderbox stays needed.
  *Built as the `light` verb (#156 step 3), the readings for Michael to overrule:* a tinderbox or a lit torch is the flame; it lights an unlit campfire with fuel, a torch on a post or in a bracket that is out, or an unlit torch in a hand or on the belt (not one in the bag, which burns only where it is used); **only "a campfire or a torch", ~~not a hearth, an oven, or a forge,~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: a hearth, a fireplace, and a lantern are lit and put out as switches; the oven and the forge are lit as before) and a fireball's landing asks the same rule**; it needs sight of the target within reach, so it never lights through a wall; it burns nothing else; the right-click menu on a mounted torch that is out offers Light the torch. **A burning fire is a flame (Primary's reading of "anything with fire lights what is made to be lit"): an unlit torch in a hand or on the belt used on a burning fire is lit from it.** ~~The menu's Light (with fuel) on a fire still needs no flame.~~ Overturned by Michael on #168 (above): it needs a flame too. **A lantern is a flame too** (Michael, 2026-10-17, on #175: "Sure why not."): an unlit torch held to a lit lantern, bracket or post, is lit from it, in sight and reach, never through a wall; the lantern ~~never goes out and is never itself lit or doused~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: it is put out and lit again).
- **The fireball as a flare:** more light in flight, and a short-lived light where it lands, light only, no fire. It keeps damage: "it should still do SOME damage, not just the side effects, just not the powerhouse it is now. We can also tune with mana." The numbers with armour and difficulty (#133).
  *The builder's numbers, for Michael to overrule (#156, built):* a lantern lights 3.4 tiles (a torch 3), steady; the fireball throws 6 tiles of light in flight (it was 4), leaves a flare of 7 tiles that fades over 3 s, and its damage goes from 70 to 45 (to be tuned with #133); it lights a campfire with fuel or a mounted torch that is out within a tile of where it lands. A lit torch inside a dropped belt or on a fallen body is doused as a torch leaving a hand is (#162). All are data.

### 2026-10-17 — Every world is an expendable test (Michael)

On #149, about worlds saved with Trees off before the mushroom fix: "We are still at a point where every single session everywhere is still considered expendable tests. I will let you know if I get to a point where I want a safe world for real." **Every world is an expendable test until Michael says otherwise:** a change may break an old save, in the world's files, the save's shape, or a rule's meaning, and **needs no save marker and no migration** (the save's `VERSION` and `save.upgrade` stay for the day he wants a safe world). A change that breaks old saves says so in its pull request's Risks, in one line.

### 2026-10-17 — The siege: a sealed room is a cell, the dead wait for dawn, a crowd bursts a fence (Michael)

From the talk on what keeps a house from being a fortress without making the dead architects (7 Days to Die's diggers were the warning: "They just massively overcorrected"). Primary proposed memory and patience instead of new powers; Michael ruled:

- **A sealed room is safe, and it is a cell** (a law; "This is law worthy, it's the right shape"): "If you can stock your base with enough food and water you should be allowed to hold up in there forever. Destroy the door and wall yourself completely in but with windows. You just can't leave." Nothing digs and no wall breaks (2026-10-10 stands); the cost of a sealed room is that you cannot leave it.
- **The siege lasts until the next dawn, for now:** the dead that know you are inside stay through the night. "I like until the next dawn for now. They stay at night. This also gives you extra reason to light up the area around your house, straight out of zomboid and minecraft. If this feels like a cop out we can extend it. This also makes night inherently more dangerous which we wanted to do without just straight up buffing zombies. They can see in but you struggle to see out if they keep busting open your shutters." (It keeps the clock law: no more dead come; the siege is the ones that saw you, and it ends.)
- **A siege stops sleep:** "you cannot sleep while the dead that know you are inside are still about; the siege bites at night." It narrows 2026-10-12's sleep and 2026-10-10's "sealed gates sleep" (noted where they sit).
- **A crowd breaks a fence, and the fence bursts into splinters:** taking Primary's crowd pressure with a new look, "explode into a cloud of splinters with a tiny animation? Slightly comical but I do not want to animate or draw a pushed over fence. And it makes it visually dead clear that it's gone and you need to remake it." Walls still never break. ~~One of the dead alone cannot break it; a crowd pushing can.~~ **Overturned by Michael, 2026-10-18, on #185: one of the dead alone now breaks a fence, slowly** (the dead strike what they break, `2026-10-18`, #174). A fence has hit points and each of the dead at it strikes, so one alone wears it down slowly and a crowd, more strikers, faster (the real seconds are in `09`).
- **A fence is a problem to solve, not a death sentence, and the dead bite over it:** "make sure that a few zombies don't have a fence section down in just a few seconds. It shouldn't be an I'm about to die but a problem to urgently solve. Like by running away. Or finding a weapon." And on biting over: "Oh 100% they should be allowed to bite over fences, otherwise exploitable." (Michael, 2026-10-17, on #152.) ~~Built as a fence section taking three of the dead 45 s, five 23 s, eight 13 s (`fenceHp` 90, `fenceDps` 1 for each pressing beyond the first, `fenceRecover` 0.5), a gate half of that;~~ **superseded 2026-10-18 (#174, Michael's model): the dead strike it on the attack animation and the bite's cooldown, each strike taking hit points, one small jolt a strike, no crowd threshold; the real seconds with real bodies are in `09` (one alone about 140 s, two 71, three 48, eight 37; a gate half).** The bite already passed a fence (it is see-through), and `check("fence")` proves it.
- **A dead one pressing a fence bites over it, and an open window is a place to be bitten from (the builder's readings for Michael to overrule, #167):** Michael's "they should be allowed to bite over fences, otherwise exploitable" holds for the pressing state too (it had no bite). By the window law (a window is never a safe spot and never a way in) a player at an open window may be bitten from outside, and one a step back may not: decided as the code does, not a new rule.
- **Shutters are buildable:** "shutters need to be buildable, they are currently the property of a wall type I think." On doors and windows he left the way to the build: "We may at some point prefer to initially separate doors and shutters from the wall objects so they are rebuildable more easily... In our case it might even be easier to make all walls solid as built, and then you can build a window or door directly over top and it stamps it into place and creates the opening. Whatever makes more sense technically I suppose, I shouldn't be suggesting tech solutions in case I bias you towards a mistake." "Everything spent, nothing repaired" stands: what is built again is new.
- **Players may build unbreakable stone walls, under two rules:** "1. costs shitload of stone, 2. every entrance that you can use MUST be usable by zombies, and if something gates it it must be breakable by zombies. This stops a fortress. If you want to stay inside your gated walls you can and this would even be a fun challenge, building a fully self sustaining keep, but the second you leave the dead (and other things!) notice you and they come for your doors."
  *The builder's reading, for Michael to overrule (#155):* rule 2 is kept by construction. A door and a window always have hit points, whatever they are made of, so no build can make an entrance the dead cannot break; a plain wall stays unbreakable, and a room with no door is a cell. Nothing is refused at the building. The stone wall itself is #104's.
- **New monsters from the regions press on doors:** "new monster types showing up in the valley from exploring other regions need to add pressure to doors, else the endgame becomes some insanely carefully build walled garden with a million random baddies bumbling around outside. More pressure, more ways to defend both come from exploring ideally." (The regions themselves are draft 2 on #123.)
- *Primary's defaults in #147, Michael's to overrule:* the dead go to the opening they saw you use (a door they break, a window they batter to see in), which narrows 2026-10-09's ten-second search and 2026-10-10's nearest opening (noted where they sit); ~~a fence shakes as a crowd presses, and a gate goes first;~~ (built 2026-10-18 as a small jolt for each strike, #174; a gate still goes first, having half the hit points); a sleep already begun is not broken by a siege that starts during it (2026-10-12's "never interrupted" stands for that); building a door or a window over a wall edge sets its kind, the edges being state with a kind already (Primary's lean, the builder's to weigh). The build: #147.

### 2026-10-17 — Play alone or play with friends; telemetry from every world (Michael)

Michael: letting friends join "should be clarified and not a thing you can do from the in-game pause menu, which is part of what sparked this". **Ruled:** "From the main menu, we make it clear. World is local or server and open, if you don't want other players in the server version then don't invite anyone with a code. So there is no more 'open to friends' button." He took Primary's framing, the choice named by what the player wants: **Play alone** (a world in the browser, offline, never joinable) and **Play with friends** (a world on our server, joinable by its code; started or joined from the same screen). **The choice is made once:** "moving games is for when you have 80 hours you don't want to lose and we are pre-pre-alpha", so Move to the server and Bring it home are hidden from players until beta. Hosting from a browser tab retires for players, and the switch of 2026-10-13 with it (struck where it sits). **Quotas and a measure of a running world's cost** come with it (Michael's yes to them). *Primary's defaults in #143, Michael's to overrule:* two server worlds per browser; the quota replacing the creator key for making a world with friends, which overturns item 5's "a stranger who finds the page can neither join a world nor make one" (2026-10-14, struck there); the measure taken before the demo goes public; the browser host kept as the two-tab test path; Play alone the default, one click to playing. **Telemetry:** "All versions send telemetry, legal stuff TBD when I am closing in on some kind of a real beta" (#144). *Primary's default there:* built anonymous from the start (no id, no IP kept), so the beta's legal answer is a small change.

### 2026-10-16 — The aim guide is off by default (Michael, after a play)

~~**The aim guide is off by default.**~~ (overturned 2026-10-24, The first real melee test: on by default, off in Settings for the challenge.)

After playing the staff and the Book of Fire with the guide on: "I am also playing at the moment with no trees, with infinite mana, and with the ranged guide (bow, thrown, magic) turned ON, which I do not think should be the default or it makes it a b[i]t too easy. You should have to learn by trial and error how long to hold down left click." **The aim guide (the landing marker and its line, for the bow, a throw, and the cast alike) is off by default;** it stays a setting of each player's own. A browser that saved its settings keeps its own choice, as with every default changed before. It replaces the default on of 2026-10-12 (Arrows that hit stay in the body; the bow's guide is a setting). **Built with it:** the setting had done nothing since 2026-10-13, because the simulation drew the marker whenever a player's snapshot carried an aim, which it always does; the setting now gates only what each player's own screen draws, so it is truly each player's own.

### 2026-10-16 — Nobody climbs through a window; region is the word (Michael, in the shape conversation on #123)

Two rulings Michael made outright in the conversation on the game's final shape; the rest of that conversation is a draft on #123, not ruled.

- **Nobody climbs through a window**, neither a player nor the dead: "Let's make this a law, we are going to remove this mechanic [and] everyone's ability to trigger it. It's not fun, and this is not zomboid, you are not clearing entire new blocks every day." A window is for sight and light. **A window is never a safe spot:** "We need to make sure that a window looking out onto a zombie isn't a safespot and they can actually hassle your door and burst in." It overturns the passage through windows of 2026-10-09 (the living and the dead both; hold E to climb) and the dead's climb of 2026-10-10, struck where they sit, and narrows 2026-10-10's "the dead attack the room's nearest opening": a window is never their way in, so the dead that want you go for a door. **Michael ruled on battering windows** (on #140, via Primary): "The dead may batter a window's shutters or boards open to see in, never to pass." The dead batter a window still shut or boarded open for sight, and still go for a door to get in; a dead one with no door it can reach batters the window and then searches (#134, #140; the dead that know you are inside then wait until dawn, 2026-10-17, The siege: a sealed room is a cell, the dead wait for dawn, a crowd bursts a fence). *Primary's default in #134, which the builder settled:* which door they go for is the nearest they can walk to, boarded or not, and when every door of a house opens from another shut room (a hall) they break that room's door first. *The builder's default, for Michael to overrule (#140):* a door's two tiles stay clear, since a chair standing where a door is stood at hides it from the dead and the window becomes a safe spot again: nothing is put down beside a door (a carried prop, a built door), and the generator keeps the valley's furniture off them and keeps both tiles of every door reachable on foot (`check.js` proves both for every map: the second by flooding from the spawn with every door and gate open), and what a station makes lands off them. *Unruled, in draft 2 on #123:* a region's vampires bursting through unboarded windows at night, as the one exception. Built in #140.
- **"Region" is the word** for a place beyond the valley (bosses gate regions, 2026-10-13): "Let's also make sure we scrub the word 'biome' as it's not really what these are and keep region as that word." The ground's own per-area look, which the code called a biome, is now a **zone** (`data/ground.json`'s `zones`, `ground.js`'s `zoneOf`, `map.zoneAt`; "terrain" was taken by the ground's blended kinds): the scrub, #135.

### 2026-10-16 — The needs-michael queue: the guide, talk's panel, a player cap, the art agent's leftovers, sheds, darkness, the combat pass, the engine (Michael)

Michael went through the `needs-michael` issues the sweep filed, one at a time, with Primary. His words are quoted on each issue.

- **The guide (#90):** "a Guide beside the spawn on every map, given a reason to be there and written like anyone else." A villager who stands beside the player's spawn on every map, as a rule (the map generator places them), with a reason to be there and a name and voice like any villager; immune to the dead as ruled 2026-10-13 (`34`).
- **Talk's panel (#91):** "entirely click based for now": the villager's default message, topics to explore each with a wiki link (opened in a new tab, or copied), Continue… when they have more to say, replies as buttons, and "That's all for now" as well as the X; no key binds inside the panel, so the world's keys never go dead mid-conversation. The voice: "warm and slightly cheeky - the OSRS formula", and welcoming, since it may be a new player's first conversation. **Starting one:** "E: talk to villager, as well as right click as a backup option." (`34`.)
- **A cap on the players in a world (#93):** "4 players in a browser-hosted world, 8 in a server world, both in data"; a join over it refused with a clear word ("This world is full."). Separate from the sixteen browsers a world remembers.
  - *The builder's defaults, for Michael to overrule (#93):* the cap counts the places held at once: the characters standing in the world, a connected guest who has stepped away, and a browser world's host always; a character resting in the save whose player left takes a place when it comes back, as a new one does, and is told the world is full if there is none (`31/03`).
- **The design agent's open items (#95):** the Book of Fire and the Staff of Fire never stack; the rule, quoted: "The stackable are usually throwables or consumables: throwing knives and axes, arrows, perhaps potions, perhaps foods, etc. We can make case by case rulings but one-ofs like these but book and staff, definitely no stack." The throwing knife's and axe's icons stay. The weeping willow is cut ("If I want another tree model I'll bring it up at that point"). The art generators are to be kept as an archive under `tools/art-gen/`, never run without Michael, once he names the folder their zips are in (#95 stays open for that copy); a "spiff up the tools" pass is for much later (#106).
- **Sheds (#92, question 5):** "A shed, hut, or forest cabin is one room; the two-rooms rule is for houses." (`30` section 15.) The map's other four questions (the names, which crops first, how many villagers, the gloom's door) are **tabled** to the "finish the valley" session, on the game's final shape, now its own open question (#123, blocked on Michael), with the game's name and the business decisions (#94).
- **Darkness and sight (#88, question 30):** "darkness shortens sight, the dead fading to hidden past a soft edge unless they stand in light; a torch gives sight back; numbers in data." ~~Daytime unchanged~~ (superseded 2026-10-17, on #150: a room shuttered by day is dark too, and open shutters light their room fully, below); each player sees by its own light. (`07`.) Built in #88. **Michael ruled on dark rooms by day** (2026-10-17, on #150): "I think a fully shuttered room should be dark inside. It's a downside, you have to keep it lit with depletable resources if you want true full protection of being inside and not spotted. I also think opening windows fully (not sure how to balance with buildable windows) should basically light the same room fully, so you never need to burn wood during the day if you keep the windows open." **A room shuttered by day is dark, and the dead in it are hidden from a player beyond the short radius; open shutters light their room fully by day.** *Primary's default, Michael's to overrule:* one open window or door lights `world.daylightTiles` (30) tiles of a room fully, so one window lights a small room and a large one needs more in proportion to its size, the number in `data/tuning.json` (`light.openness`). **Michael ruled on targeting** (2026-10-17, on #150): "1. Does melee target work on enemies you can't see? My thought: no. 2. Can you blind fire into the dark with skill shots? My thought: yes." **The dark hides information, never physics:** a dead one hidden by the dark has no swing-target outline, no floating health bar, no hover hand, and cannot be clicked or right-clicked, each client by its own sight; a melee swing still hits whatever is in its wedge, and an arrow, a throw, or a fireball still hits what it lands on. *Primary's default, Michael's to overrule:* a hidden dead one's speech bubble is not drawn, and its groan goes to the log with no position ("Something groans in the dark.").
- **The combat pass (#89):** "keep combat paused until I've played a proper session; rule the three feel pieces then" (the spin-click cap, knockdown 0.3, a push per hit), and fold infection's effects and cures and health potions into the game's shape (#123). A new label, `needs-playtest`, marks work that waits on Michael's play rather than a decision (`github.md` 6).
- **The engine's second helping (#100):** "B8 now as a ready chore, D1 after the menus steps land, close D2 and D4": the pathfinder's buffers (#124) now; `main.js` back to wiring after #109 to #112; the `actions/` module and the `map/` folder closed until something hurts.

### 2026-10-16 — The menus and the site: Michael's answers to `38`'s questions (#16; Michael)

Michael went through `38-the-menus-and-the-site.md`'s nine questions with the menus' Secondary, then showed two references for the look (terraria.org and Old School RuneScape's front page). The rulings as they stand, by question. Where a line says **by default**, Michael did not rule that point: it is the scope's recommendation, and his to overrule.

**The site (Q2, Q9).** Out of a world is **the game's website**: art infused, but a functional, usable website first (Michael: "I want it to be a full page website for sure, the opaque box that is a sort of permanent modal convention goes away"). There are **two websites, which may share a look**: the game's (a splash page, information about the game, and a very clear way to play it in the browser), which for now is all one with the game, so the part outside a world *is* that site; and **the wiki** (`cmzgame.github.io/cmz-wiki`), placed prominently on it. **Later, three things** (Michael): a promotional site, the wiki, and a game client, split post-demo and pre-launch when the game is wrapped in Chromium for Steam (`35`). So the build keeps that split cheap: the site's pages never reach into a world, and the crossing (PLAY in, Exit game out) is one seam to cut along. Where the site is hosted, and under what name and domain, stays with the private half of infrastructure day (2026-10-15), off the repository. This overturns, for the screens out of a world, the dark translucent look the stone ruling of 2026-10-12 kept for the screens (struck there).

**The look (Q2), from the two references.** From terraria.org: **a prominent wordmark made of art that could be the game's** (Terraria's letters are dirt and stone with foliage behind; ours by default stone and timber, the autumn foliage behind); **a prominent, textured menu with PLAY as its big callout**, where Terraria has BUY, and **WIKI** on it, those two the bare minimum; **the background slightly blurred**; **content boxes styled from the game** rather than a generic dark theme (Terraria's are the ground seen from the side; ours plausibly the game's stone). From Old School RuneScape: **a Play button that could not be clearer** (its Try Free), a lot of information in little space, themed side rails, and characters round the title card. **Not taken:** the layout's complexity (no news, merch, or update boxes), anything more Terraria's than ours (no side-on dirt and grass), and **the thin strip of website on a black page** (Michael hates it: the site uses the window's width).

**The background (Q1, with the look).** Q1 ruled **a still placeholder picture** made at boot from the game's art (a cottage, trees, a fire, the goobers, the fireball's glow), generated through the same interface as real sprite sheets (a sprite definition with a `placeholder.kind`), so a drawn picture replaces it with no code change. The references then added **blurred screenshots of the game itself** (Michael: "I think our game is pretty enough to generate actual screenshots to live in the back if we want"), perhaps from **a map made only to stage them** with the best props (an isolated cabin, a well, many trees, leaves, and flowers; the autumn trees "put in a lot of work"). **Michael then ruled the real screenshots in place of the placeholder** (2026-10-16, in session with Primary, quoted): "The screenshots are fine for now! Especially because it's not super clearly in focus." So the staging map's real shots (#118) stand behind the site, and the generated placeholder picture was not made; his painting still replaces them when it comes. This is `31/09`'s "still picture, not a world" (2026-10-13); still no world is created, stepped, or drawn behind the site.

**The line between the site and a world (Q2, Q3, Q4).** **Worlds, Identities, character creation, and Settings are the site's pages.** PLAY opens the Worlds page, so `31/09`'s reason for the title having no Play (a world is always chosen by name) still holds. A world chosen, **the loading screen**, then the world; **"full screen" means the game fills the window**, with no site round it, and a Fullscreen choice in Settings for the browser's own (PLAY never forces it: the browser keeps Esc for leaving fullscreen, and Esc is the pause). **Exit game** saves and goes back to the site; that crossing is the line between in a game and not, which Zomboid does well. **The loading screen** is the boot shell's (`index.html` `#loading`, `boot.js`), shown while a world is read, restored, and its first ring baked, its stages named, closed only when the first frame is under budget; "Saving …" on the way out until the save is written; for a guest, "Joining …" with Cancel, covering the stand-in world until the welcome (the stand-in stays underneath, so `main.js` is not restructured). By default it is up at least half a second, so it never flickers. **In a world (Q3), by default:** the game's own screens stay over the world drawn (pause, the dead screen as `05`'s spectating wants, the character, the game's Settings for what a player changes while playing), and Leave world becomes Exit game on the pause menu and the dead screen. By default the site paints at once and the game's art loads behind it, so PLAY's loading screen shows only what is left.

**Settings (Q5, Q6, Q7).** **Settings is tabs generated from a registry** in `settings.js` (key, tab, label, a line of explanation, the answers; `10`'s pin). **Each setting is a switch or a dropdown, never a row of buttons** (Michael: "I am partial to switches for booleans and dropdowns for multi choice, which more or less what Zomboid does"), so the chosen answer is the only one shown; this ends the hard-to-read selected choice (ruled to be fixed here, 2026-10-15). By default the identity editor's hair styles follow the same rule, and the colour swatches keep their gold outline. **The testing settings are Settings' last tab, Testing**, shown to everyone while the game is in development and hidden in the Steam build. **The world's rules move into each world now** (Michael chose this over leaving them in Settings for a later ruling): zombie aggro, zombies attack villagers, survival, free building, and multi-hit are chosen in a Sandbox step of New world (Zomboid's way), changed by the host from the pause menu, kept in the world's save, and read by the simulation from there, as a server world's already are (`rules.setSource`; the owner's screen sets them). Today a browser's world reads them from the browser's settings at every step (`rules.refresh`), so a rule set in one world follows you into the next; that ends. By default the dev chest, read when a world is made, joins the Sandbox step. **Combat testing and the square's surface retired** (Michael, 2026-10-16, #130: "Let's nix this setting and everything that relies on it, along with the old 'paved or dirt' for the square"; both were invented on the first two-building map). They are replaced by per-world rules, **Trees** (on or off) and **Starting population** (None, Low, Standard, High), under this principle: **a world rule is general to any map (trees or none, how many of the dead, whom they hunt), never a change to one map's layout in one way.** **Michael adopted the lock** (#130, in session with Primary, 2026-10-16): both are chosen **only at New world** and shown locked afterwards (World rules shows them locked, for the host as for a guest; a server world's owner cannot change them), since both shape what is placed and a change would reach only the chunks not yet grown. His addition, quoted: "The start game process needs to clearly identify what is a setting that gets chosen once at world creation, but it's a smart pattern and this is more or less what zomboid does as I recall - some settings are tweakable on the fly, some are baked into the world." **The pattern: a world rule is either baked into the world at creation or tweakable on the fly, and the screens say which.** The Sandbox sets the baked ones (Trees, Starting population, the dev chest) apart under "Chosen once, when the world is made", the rest under "How it plays", changeable later from the pause menu. Off, Trees makes no tree anywhere (rocks, the ground, and the forage stay: the mushrooms are placed against where the trees would have stood, #142). Starting population scales the dead the map places by 0, 0.4, 1, or 2.5 (None, Low, Standard, High; data, `tuning.json`), both its places' authored dead and each wild chunk's, as they are made; it never rises with time. **None** (Michael, 2026-10-16, on #123, via Primary on #131) is a valley with no dead at the start, for playtesting production chains ("I do not want to randomly die of an infection while counting something") and as the cozy test: no dead placed at creation and none grown with the wild chunks, for host and guest alike. The save already carries a world's rules (version 2, since 2026-10-14); whether its version moves is the build's to find, and if it does the pull request is `needs-michael`, as any change to the save is (`github.md` 8.6). A guest sees the host's rules, shown and locked, as now.

**The screens explained (Q8), by default:** the Worlds rows as cards (the name, the character and whether they live, the day and hour, when last played, the code, an "on the server" mark; Play big, Delete small), one plain sentence per screen (the relay's address to the Testing tab), and Controls grouped, all as the site's pages in the new look.

### 2026-10-16 — Auto-drinking, from any waterskin carried (#96; Michael)

*Narrowed (2026-10-19, Unsafe water: a sickness that may come and passes; the skin sips only clean water; crops take any water): the sip by itself takes only clean water.*

Open question 22 since 2026-10-10. Primary proposed the belt as the opt-in (a skin on the belt sips by itself, the bag's by hand); **Michael ruled the wider way: any waterskin the player carries, belt or bag.** At the thirst moodle's second stage ("Thirsty", `autoDrinkStage` in `data/tuning.json`), a sip from the emptiest skin that has water, so one is finished before the next, with the word "You drink from your waterskin." Never mid-fight: not during a swing, a draw or a cast, a guard, a climb, sleep, or another drink. The thirst moodle comes to mean "out of water", as the cozy parent's did. It runs in the world's step on the authority, never in a guest's prediction, so a guest's sip is taken once.

### 2026-10-16 — The four lines of `00-vision.md`, ruled; and no more laws about what the game is not (Michael)

The sweep redrafted the four lines of `00` that read single-player or against the generated map (flagged 2026-10-13; the drafts in #83). Michael's rulings on them, in his words where it matters:

1. **"Not Zomboid with cute art" stands.** "It is in fact not Zomboid with cute art, and that is intentional." He wants a place on the wiki for the Zomboid things the game swears off, to add to as he plays ("thank god this is not a thing in my game"): a delineator, not a rule here.
2. **"Not an RPG" goes.** "The more laws we write about what the game is and isn't, the more boxed in we get." He plans RPG-lite features, so the line was wrong as well as boxing. **The meta-ruling:** `00`'s "What it is not" gains no new lines; a limit the game needs is a ruling here with its reason, never a law of identity.
3. **"Not a random world" becomes a direction:** a hand-authored world with procedural filling of the spaces it leaves empty (forests and the like). "This is another thing I do not want to get boxed in on": the infinite grown wilderness is cool today, and where the game lands between authored and grown stays open.
4. **Real time, as drafted:** a solo world pauses only from the pause menu; a world with friends never pauses.

Waves 0 to 3 are merged (#72, #73, #74, #82 to #86, cmz-wiki#7 and #8); `docs/README.md` is the index. Three rulings the trims surfaced, recorded here so no doc has to carry them twice:

2. **Michael's call 4 of the sweep applies to this file too:** the entry of 2026-10-14 on the name and the project's own accounts lost the name of his other project and the two personal details it carried; the studio's email is named nowhere in the repository. The vague stays.

They stay as asks in `29/09` and places in `29/03` and `29/06`.

### 2026-10-16 — The sweep: a new Primary, and what a doc is (Michael)

Michael named a new Primary for the first time since the role existed and asked it to sweep the whole place: `CLAUDE.md`, the docs, what belongs in the wiki or in GitHub issues. Seven readers read every doc in both repositories in full against the code at `b256132`. The finding: the docs are right about what was decided and wrong about what was done (a doc saying a thing is owed when the code has it, in a dozen places); twenty-eight overturned rulings sit unmarked in this file; only 37 percent of this file is rulings; every rule has three to five homes; the amend-in-place habit was prepending status bullets, not striking. The strikethrough counts in `29-world/` were the river and the moat in ASCII maps. GitHub and the wiki, the last things built, are in good shape. The plan is five waves (the misleading lines; the files every agent loads; this file; the specs in parallel sessions; the issues) and Michael said go on its shape and order. His six calls:

1. **A doc is one of three things: a rule, a reference, or a record.** Rules live in `CLAUDE.md` (the laws) and here (the rulings, one entry each, an overturned one struck where it sits). A reference describes the game as it is and is rewritten in place, never appended; its status block at the top is replaced, not stacked. A record goes to `docs/history/` whole and is never edited again. A build gets an entry here only if it forced a ruling, and then the ruling alone; the record of a build is its pull request and the spec's status block. Open work is a GitHub issue, never a doc section. Each rule has one home; everything else points. **History is archived under `docs/history/`, not deleted** (git has it either way; the folder keeps every cross-reference resolvable and says "record" by its path), and `docs/README.md`, the one index, says of every doc whether it is a rule, a reference, a spec not yet built, or a record, so it stays clear which docs carry live work. The code map moves into nested `src/CLAUDE.md`, `server/CLAUDE.md`, and `tools/CLAUDE.md`, loaded only when an agent touches those folders.
2. **The order:** waves 0 and 1 first, then this file, then the specs in parallel Secondary sessions, then the issues, filed slowly.
3. **`00-vision.md`'s four single-player lines** (53, 54, 56, 71, flagged 2026-10-13): Primary drafts the rewordings in the pull request and Michael edits them.
4. **`35` stays in the public `docs/`, trimmed:** the vague stays (the plan, the costs in round numbers), nothing detailed enough to be a privacy issue goes (an account name, an email, where a token is kept).
5. **The three open design questions** (darkness and the player's sight, question 30; the spin-click turn cap, 29b; the guide's who and where and the talk panel's keys, `34`) become `needs-michael` issues, answered when a related build comes up.
6. **`05-someday.md`'s ideas go to GitHub as one tracking issue**, not one each: most are maybe-someday-maybe-never, and that issue is where the stretch goals and moonshots live. `05` itself stays, one line per idea.

**Infrastructure day is split in two** (Michael, the same conversation): the sweep is its public half and goes on GitHub under the milestone "Process and infrastructure"; the private half (the name, a domain and business email, the site, the relay's Fly app moved to the studio's account, the repositories going private, and, found by the sweep, an off-Fly copy of the server worlds' saves and a backed-up home for the art generators and PNGs that exist only in Michael's download zips) stays a dated checklist off the repository until the repositories are private, by his ruling of 2026-10-15.

### 2026-10-15 — Equip, Unequip, and Durability: no more "wear" (Michael)

Found through the wiki: "wear" meant two things, putting a garment on (the menu's **Wear**) and how used up a weapon or tool is (durability by kills), and two of the wiki's pages each claimed the word. **Ruling:** the word goes from everything a player reads. **Putting anything on is Equip, taking it off is Unequip**, for garments and bags as for hand items (the garment menu said Wear and Take off; it now says Equip, "Equip (6×4)" for a bag, and Unequip; the pick-up refusal and its message say "equip it"; the controls screen says "Equip or unequip a thing"). **The mechanic is Durability** (it never had a word on screen: the bar on the icon, and "Your stick is spent."). The code's own names stay (`wear` on an item definition, `entry.wear`, the `worn` slots): `entry.wear` is in every save, and a rename would be a migration for nothing a player sees. The same day the wiki's chat page became **Chat log** (Michael), so "Log" is the item alone; the game's docs say chat log too.

### 2026-10-15 — Only the pause menu stops the game (Michael)

Michael, casting with the staff and book while his bag was open: nothing happened on a left click or E. An open panel was dropping the world's left click, the E, B, and T keys, and the zoom wheel, and ending a bow's draw. **Ruling:** "bag should definitely be allowed to be open and you can still fight, if your bag is small enough some players may just leave it open," and the same for other bags, things to loot, work stations, and the map: **the only thing that stops you playing and makes you safe is the pause menu.** It finishes what the guard's fix began on 2026-10-12 (`21`: the shield holds with the bag open, "the world goes on while you look in it").

Built: with any panel open, a left click on the world swings, casts, draws, or places (a click on a panel never reaches the world: the canvas alone takes the mouse), the Use mode's click on the world lands (a Use begun from the bag no longer needs the bag shut), E, B, and T work, and the wheel zooms except while an item is being dragged (the wheel turns it). Kept: a right-click menu still takes the click that closes it and ends a draw (a menu is a choice in hand, not a screen); the build palette and the bag still take turns on the screen (N and X say "Close your bag to build." while the bag is up, and opening the bag closes the palette), which is layout, not safety, and Michael's to change.

### 2026-10-15 — Burning made real (#14; Michael's rules, the build's rulings)

Michael said go on #14 with its defaults (0.8 a second for 10 seconds, a torch's blow one in four, the whole stack of arrows dipped at once) and asked that the test toggle go: **the character menu's Set on fire and Put out are gone**, for the dead, the villagers, and the player alike (`__game.burn.ignite(z)` is the tests' way in). The build's own rulings, Michael's to overrule:

- **The torch's chance is on its weapon block** (`"ignite": 0.25` in `data/items.json`), not a `burn.torchChance` in `data/combat.json` as the issue had it: any weapon can carry an `ignite` chance (a flaming sword, a brand) with no code, and the fireball's spell and the fire arrow's shot carry `ignite` the same way. `burn` in `data/combat.json` holds the rest: `dps`, `duration`, and `cap`.
- **The cap counts across refreshes:** one burn, from the first spark until it goes out, takes no more than `cap` (10) however often a new source refreshes its timer, which is Michael's "never more than 10 over the whole burn" taken literally. A body that goes out and is lit again starts a new burn.
- **The spark comes before the blow's damage:** a killing blow that also sets alight leaves a corpse that burns on (the issue's corpse rule, reached from the blow as well as from the burn).
- **A burn is not a blow:** it takes health and nothing else (no push, no stagger, the dead one's path kept; `burnZombie`), so a burning dead one keeps walking at you.
- **`fire` in `data/interactions.json` names any prop whose fire burns** (a campfire, a fireplace, a hearth, the forge, the oven), so one row covers every fire; the `dip` verb refuses an unlit one ("The fire is out."). A row with a verb now says its `message` when the verb succeeds ("You dip the arrows in the fire.").
- **A fire arrow is a projectile of its own** (`fire_arrow`: the arrow with a burning head, a small moving light like the fireball's), so a guest sees it burn in flight from the frame's projectile name. It lands or lodges as a plain arrow, hit or miss.

### 2026-10-15 — Primary's pull requests are reviewed by agents it starts (Michael)

Asked whether the reviewing agents Primary started tonight were a one-night allowance: **a standing permission.** Primary may start a Secondary agent to review its own pull request (a fresh agent with no part in the change, a signed review, Primary answering it in new commits); for a change he is particularly worried about, Michael opens a Secondary session himself to keep direct tabs. Michael: with GitHub he should not have to carry messages back and forth as reviews. Written into `github.md` section 8, step 6, which outlives the bot's flag; the box's rule 4 points there.

### 2026-10-15 — The process while the bot is flagged (Michael)

GitHub's spam protection flagged `cmzgame-bot` after it filed 38 issues in a few minutes (#7's planning), hiding everything it wrote, its approvals included; a reinstatement ticket is filed and may take a week. Michael asked for a stopgap rather than a week without work, and took Primary's proposal over his own of merging locally with no pull requests: **pull requests and CI stay; the required approval drops to 0; every session works as `michaeldippold`, told apart by its signature; a review is a signed comment opening with Approved or Changes requested; only Primary merges, on such a comment from a session with no part in the change and green checks, the reverts and hotfixes of `github.md` 8.8 excepted; nothing is created in bursts.** Kept because the pull request is the record and CI is the gate a local merge would lose. ### 2026-10-15 — Primary and Secondary, a bot account, and `github.md` in force once set up (Michael)

Michael's answers to `github.md`'s questions, and one addition at its very top. **Roles:** every session that works on the game or the wiki asks Michael at its start "Primary or Secondary, and what is my focus?" and changes nothing until he answers. **Primary**, exactly one at a time, is the technical lead: it reviews, approves, and merges into `main`, watches the deploy, and codes too; the buck stops there before it stops at Michael. **Secondary**, any number, each with a focus that is not a fence (bugs, the wiki, infrastructure, features, art), works on branches and never merges into `main`. The question is the first lines of `CLAUDE.md` (which Claude Code loads in every session, so nothing has to be remembered), the wiki's `CLAUDE.md`, and a short `START-HERE.md` for any tool that does not load `CLAUDE.md`. **Accounts:** a new GitHub account for this game's agents alone, used by every Secondary; Michael's own account for Michael and for Primary; so every pull request can be approved by the other side, and branch protection can require one approving review. **`writeplaintext` is never used for this game** (the businesses may part; neither belongs in the other's history). Pushes stay plain `git`. **`docs/bugs.md`** becomes the holding place for unverified bugs, gone through now and then; a reproduced bug becomes a GitHub issue. **The wiki Secondary's routine syncs** push to the wiki's `main` directly; larger wiki changes go by pull request. **Cleaned up:** every "before the merge" and "Michael's to do" item now sits in one TODO section, "Owed by Michael" (the done ones recorded there: the deploy token, Pages from Actions, the first deploy, the wiki rule). `docs/27`'s art comes in by pull request; "Implementing from a spec" opens a pull request instead of pushing to `main`. ### 2026-10-15 — The wiki's rule: synced in the same session as the push (Michael)

The wiki rule as first written (no push to `main` before the wiki is synced) could not be kept literally: `sync.js` reads `origin/main`, so the wiki can only follow a push, and a push to `main` deploys the game at once. **Ruled:** a feature, a fix, or a change to a number is not called done until the wiki is synced for it **in the same session as its push**; a batch of pushes in one session (the census's bugs, say) is synced once, at the batch's end, and nothing in the batch is reported done before that sync is pushed. Written in `CLAUDE.md` (start-here item 7, the rule, and step 6 of implementing from a spec) and in the wiki repository's `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md`.

### 2026-10-15 — The wiki is part of shipping, written into `CLAUDE.md` (Michael)

Step 7 of `37`, at Michael's go: `CLAUDE.md` gains start-here item 7 and a rule beside the multiplayer one. No feature, fix, or change to a number goes to `main` until the wiki is synced for it (snapshot, census, the touched pages corrected or written by its `WRITING.md`, `build --check` clean, an Updates entry, a push); whoever pushes the change owns its wiki update, or names it as owed to the wiki agent in the commit message when the change has no player-visible effect; a bug fixed from `docs/bugs.md` moves to Fixed and its wiki pages are corrected at the same sync. The method lives in the wiki repository's own `CLAUDE.md`. "Implementing from a spec" step 6 now syncs the wiki before the push. Docs: CLAUDE.md, 02, 37.

### 2026-10-14 — The bow held upright, both hands on it (Michael)

(the build's record is `docs/history/02-build-log.md`, same heading)

Michael: the bow held at rest "looks awful"; the draw is fine but for two things: the angle toward the camera, and the off hand hanging by the side. Reproduced on a contact sheet of the player's frames with the held things composed as the renderer does. **The cause of the angle:** the bow was a picture turned with the forearm (`held.js` rotates every held shape into sixteen angles), so at rest it lay flat across the hip, and facing the camera, with the arm pointing at the viewer, it lay sideways. **The ruling:** a bow's limbs are upright in the world, so they are upright on screen whatever way it points; only the belly turns, toward the side the archer faces, and seen end on it is a near-straight stave. Built as a held shape drawn by the facing, not turned (`HELD_BY_FACING` in `held.js`: sixteen fresh drawings, the sheet marked `byFacing`; the joints carry the facing's frame as `angFace`, `draw.js`; the renderer takes it for such a sheet, `render/entities.js`), and a little taller (26 px) so a tip shows past the shoulder when the body is in front of it. **The off hand:** an item's `held.pose` names a pose in `data/poses.json`, which now holds the off arm too when it has `offPitch` (`rig.js` `heldPoseArms`; the book's `present` is unchanged); the bow's is `bow_rest`, both hands low in front of the hips and the other at the string (`pitch 35, out -2, offPitch 35, offOut -5`). The draw's frames moved the string hand in toward the chest's middle instead of out to the side (`offOut` -6 to -7 where it was 2 to 6) and the bow hand to the middle (`out -3`); the release flicks it out.

### 2026-10-14 — The fireball bigger and slower (Michael)

(the build's record is `docs/history/02-build-log.md`, same heading)

Michael played the fireball: it feels great, but it reads as "a fire baseball" when he wanted a fire bowling ball, and it "absolutely ZIPS" to its target, too strong for what it does. **Bigger:** the fireball's sheet carries `scale: 2` (`archery.fireballSheet`) and the projectile's draw in `render/entities.js` honours a whole-number scale, so the ball is drawn at twice its painted size. A whole number keeps its pixels square, but they are twice the world's pixel size, so it is chunkier than everything round it. The clean fix, if that shows, is the design agent repainting the sheet at the size wanted (cells of 128) and the scale set back to 1. **Slower:** ~~the spell's speed is 5 tiles a second, not 12 (`book.spell.speed` in `data/items.json`), so a ball across its full range of 18 tiles flies about 3.6 s and one at six tiles about 1.2 s.~~ (superseded 2026-10-15 by #12, which No friendly fire; the fireball's burst only ahead names: `book.spell.speed` is 5.25 in `data/items.json`) A hit is decided where it lands, as an arrow's is, so a slow ball at a moving target can now miss: that is the balance. Nothing else changes: the damage, the draw, and the flat flight are as they were.

### 2026-10-14 — A dead player lies as a body you can search (Michael's playtest)

(the build's record is `docs/history/02-build-log.md`, same heading)

Michael died while away, came back as a new character in the same world, and found his old self standing, not to be searched. **Ruled (Michael):** a player who dies falls and lies as a body, lootable like the dead's, so the next character can get their old things back. **Built:** at the death the world stops whatever the player was doing (a sleep, an action, a draw, a climb), sets down a carried prop beside them where there is room, and plays the fall; once it is over the body is baked like the dead's (`dressCorpse`) and joins the fallen (`S.corpses`), searched with E or the menu like any body (the sword, the clothes, the belt, the bag with what was in it), saved with the corpses, and sent to guests as the dead's bodies are. The client whose player it was keeps it as its own for the dead screen; playing a world whose character of mine has fallen shows the dead screen over my body, never someone else's character (the old fallback to the first player is not reached); the Worlds row reads a body as dead; a rejoin by that browser finds the body and asks for a new character. **Not in this:** turning into one of the dead (`31/03`, not built).

### 2026-10-14 — Item 5's two defaults agreed: the owner's character follows the owner key, and a removed player stays out (Michael)

Two choices the server plan left as defaults, agreed by Michael before the plan's review: **the owner's character is filed under the owner key**, not a browser id, so the owner plays the same character from any device where the key is entered (other players stay keyed by their browser id); and **removing a player bars that browser from rejoining** until the owner lifts it. The plan goes to the coder agent for review before any code.

### 2026-10-14 — Item 5 ruled: worlds on a server, for availability (Michael)

**The goal is availability:** a friend can play a shared world while its owner is offline. A server world is **on when anyone is in it** and held when empty, as a host's tab is (the clock law: nothing moves while nobody plays, and it costs nothing asleep). Everyone in a server world is a guest, the owner included, which Michael likes for its parity. **Who runs it:** Fly, beside the relay. **Who pays:** Michael, as playtesting for his own benefit. **Where the saves live:** on the server, for a server world. **When:** now, on the current Fly account (moved to the game's own accounts on the infrastructure day). **Deploys:** automatic from GitHub on every push to `main`, since the protocol refuses a mismatch. **Ownership and access:** the server owns the world; the owner authenticates as owner from any device and controls who gets in; a stranger who finds the page can neither join a world ~~nor make one~~ (superseded 2026-10-17: Play alone or play with friends; telemetry from every world: a quota per browser, Primary's default, #143) (details left to the build, reviewed here). **Single player stays local** (Claude's recommendation, Michael asked): the demo is single player on a static site and a Steam game is expected to play offline, so the in-browser path stays regardless, and the sim is the same `game.js` on both; ~~any world, a solo one included, may be moved to the server by its owner, which gives the joiner's view for testing when wanted~~ (superseded 2026-10-17: Play alone or play with friends; telemetry from every world: chosen once, the moves hidden until beta, #143). Planned before it is built: the multiplayer branch writes the plan, reviewed here, as for items 1 and 3.

### 2026-10-14 — The demo sells the game: a demo, not a tutorial (Michael)

Zomboid's tutorial can end in a death with nothing after it because the finished game is a click away; this demo must leave the player wanting the open valley. Michael agreed five rules, written into `36`: (1) **the first run reaches the first night**, where the game looks best (a new player sees nightfall, a careful one dawn); (2) **preparation buys time, never escape** (Michael's correction: the demo still kills everyone, since one you could survive forever is a free game; early deaths read as the player's mistake, each escalation stage answerable for a while, the end certain); (3) **the edge promises the world instead of hiding it**: a bramble thicket the player cannot cross and the dead push through, roads out blocked by a fallen tree and a wrecked cart, signposts naming the valley's places, no invisible wall a player can walk into (the outer boundary only the dead reach); (4) **the end card sells the full game**: the run and the best run, a few lines on what the full game is (pictures once there is final art of it), and **a Wishlist on Steam button**, the card's largest, to the store page (Michael: or the art money may be wasted), also on the demo's title; (5) **the depth shows along the way**: the set laid out so a first day touches boarding, the bench, the hearth, the well, the bag, and wear. Docs: 36.

### 2026-10-14 — The demo re-ruled, the name and the project's own accounts first, and the two open notes of the long arc (Michael)

On the review of `33` to `36` (the docs merged as 62160f0). **The long arc's two open notes, ruled:** (a) the quiet first day touching the clock law is fine: it is a tutorial, an arc that happens once and briefly at the start, and could later be skipped through dialogue. (b) One player's choice raising the danger for an absent friend is fine: the clock did not force it, the friend did; it is theirs to boast of or apologise for, and the game washes its hands of it.

**Before anything public:** the game needs **its name** first, ahead of any marketing or business step. Then **an infrastructure day**, as Michael has done for another project of his: the game's own business email, its own GitHub account with **the repository private** (today it is public and the Pages site serves the whole game, found in the review), its own hosting for a secure site with the demo and the information about the game, with a design. `35`, `TODO.md`. (The details of the studio's accounts were taken out of this entry on 2026-10-16 by Michael's call 4 of the sweep; the private half of infrastructure day is kept off the repository.)

**The demo (`36`), re-ruled:** (1) the dead spawn **off screen and walk straight to the middle of the set** (they are coming for you; nobody goes to find them), in place of the out-of-sight rule, which trees could not satisfy since trees do not block sight; the set no bigger than it must be (you are not meant to explore), wrapped, with a little room to move, in dense forest the dead keep walking out of: a player's boundary the dead pass and an outer one nobody passes. (2) **The goal is to kill the player:** a slow start, then steady escalation, Zomboid's unwinnable tutorial horde with the real game playable for as long as you can hack it; when one way of killing you reaches its limit the next begins. (3) **"Cosy by day, siege by night" is withdrawn**, for the demo and as a phrase for the game: the vision's frame replaces it (the spooky part is exploration of new regions; small night modifiers at most, in the game, never the demo); the demo escalates day and night alike, and surviving the first day to prepare for the night is the replay's discovery. (4) **No co-op in the demo.** (6) The guide's place is open (one option: a villager called the Guide beside the spawn on every map); `zombiesPerChunk` (a grown wild chunk's dead, a `[min, max]` range) is `[0, 0]` in the demo's map, the spawner being the only source. Every time in the demo's schedule is in seconds of simulation, never clock minutes (an in-game minute is half a real second). **Talk (`34`):** `E` near a villager talks, and the nearest thing wins with the prompt naming it, a rule agreed. **`CLAUDE.md`:** the guide is the one villager the dead never harm. Docs: 34, 35, 36, CLAUDE.md, TODO.

### 2026-10-13 — A sleeping player never steps away (Michael, on item 3's plan)

Item 3's plan steps a player away from a shared world after five minutes of real time with no input (the multiplayer branch's rulings). A player in bed sends no input, and waiting in bed for friends who are still up can pass five minutes; stepped away, they would leave the bed and the count of sleepers, and come back to a world whose night had moved on without them. **Ruled:** a sleeping player is never stepped away. Lying down is a choice already made, and a bite in bed is the danger the design keeps (`30` section 3.3). Also asked of item 3 in review: a guest whose tab hides mid-walk must stop on the host (today the host reuses its last input), and the host's own player out of the world while its client stays open must leave the client's "me" its own (today `removePlayer` hands it to the next player in the list).

### 2026-10-13 — When the world stops, and when a player steps away (Michael, for item 3: the host's world while its tab is not in front)

**Why the host's world stops today:** the loop steps the world from the browser's drawing frames, and every browser stops drawing a hidden tab, so a host who switches tabs or minimises freezes the world for everyone (measured in the pane's background tab: no drawing frames, while a Worker's timer ran at 62 a second). The plan for item 3 drives the host's loop from a Worker's timer while its tab is hidden; Michael asked why the game should ever stop while the tab is still open, and whether a generous idle guard would do instead. **Ruled:**

1. **A solo world stops while its tab is hidden**, as single player always has: switching away is a pause, and nobody is waiting on you.
2. **A world with friends in it never stops while its host's tab is open**, hidden, minimised, or covered (the Worker's timer; tabs a browser freezes outright are the one exception, which the probe of step 0 measures).
3. **In a world with friends in it, a player with no input for five minutes of real time steps away**: the character leaves the world, safe, as a guest's does after its sixty seconds disconnected, and comes back where it stood on the next key, click, or mouse movement. The guard is on the character, not the world: stopping the world after an idle spell would strand friends still playing, and a body standing still for two hours is a gift to the dead (Claude's point, Michael agreeing).
4. **Never in a solo world**: Michael often stands still in his own games for half an hour while doing something else, and wants to stay in.

**Claude's assumption, for Michael to overrule:** rule 3 covers guests as well as the host. A guest whose tab is hidden sends nothing but stays connected, so today it stands in the world for as long as it likes, which is the same gift; the same five minutes apply to everyone in a shared world. The idle time is the player's own client's to measure (its keys and mouse, real time, since it is about the person and not the world), and stepping away and back are commands the host performs, so the world's step stays the only thing that changes the world.
