### 2026-10-11 — One poke for everything you can touch: swing and sway (Michael)

Michael wants the "touch to get" economy to start as one default response, an in-world to-do list of what needs work later. **Rulings:**
- **Trees are clickable too.** They were always the same kind of game object as the rocks and mushrooms (props on tiles); they are now marked `interactable` like them. "Chop down" stays on the right-click menu, and right-clicking anywhere on a tree, its crown included, offers it.
- **A left click swings as usual** (the attack, whatever it hits), and the rock, mushroom, or tree under the cursor sways. Right-click sways it and opens its actions, if any.
- **The sway replaces the jitter:** the base stays put and the top leans one way, then the other, dying away over 0.56 s (a skewed quad, `skew` in `gl.quad`); tall things lean further, from a pixel and a half for a mushroom to five for a giant tree.
- **Authoring:** rocks, mushrooms, and trees can be placed on a building's lawn the same way the trial rocks were, as `props` in the map file; the floor stamp, when it exists, gets them as palette entries.

### 2026-10-11 — Sparser again, and big rocks in the wild (Michael, art direction)

An art-direction pass, not a performance one. Measured over the same test area, before and after: trees 983 to 750 (density a quarter lower: the scatter 0.018 to 0.117, the wild 0.0405 to 0.1305), mushrooms 270 to 135 (forage chance 0.021), leaf decals 407 to 224 (`perReach` 0.3, `extraChance` 0.22, `openChance` 0.05). **Big rocks join the wild's generator** (`wild.rocks` in `square.json`): each 40-by-40-tile patch has a 60% chance of one rock at a spot rolled from the patch, in place of whatever tree stood there; at Michael's monitor size (about 2,000 tiles a screen) 144 sampled screens showed 0, 1, or 2 rocks (74, 58, 12) and never more. The village keeps its three placed rocks. Performance gains are small but real: fewer sprites to sort and draw each frame, and slightly cheaper bakes (7.5 ms median, from about 8.7).

### 2026-10-11 — Mushrooms and rocks become game objects that wiggle (Michael)

Michael's redrawn art (bigger mushrooms outlined all the way round; rocks with a curved, bedded base and top faces seen from above) and a ruling: mushrooms and rocks are clickable game objects now, with no stats or actions; a click makes one wiggle on a short cooldown, "I recognize you clicked me". **Built:**
- **Mushrooms are props, not decals.** A `forage` rule in `square.json` places them near trees (a tree within two tiles, 0.042 per tile, five kinds), inside the bounds at load and in the wild as chunks grow, from pure rolls (`treeAt` answers where trees are without making chunks, so the same mushrooms come back whatever order chunks load in). Each sits a few pixels off its tile's centre (a prop `offset`) so they do not line up. Walkable; not carriable.
- **Rocks** take the new art; anchors sit midway between the base's lowest point and its ends.
- **The wiggle:** a click on an interactable's visible pixels (`interactable: true` on its sprite definition) shakes it side to side, about two pixels, dying away over 0.42 s; it will not wiggle again for 0.6 s. Right-click pokes it (and opens no "Walk here" menu); **left-click pokes it instead of swinging**, since clicking on a thing means the thing. The test is per pixel, so a click beside a mushroom still swings.

### 2026-10-11 — Fewer mushrooms, sticks under trees, fewer tufts, a thinner forest (Michael)

- **Mushrooms** down by two thirds (0.042 per tile near a tree).
- **Sticks belong to trees:** each tree drops 0, 1, or 2 (equally likely), at random within 1.2 tiles of its trunk, rolled from the tree's own tile (`"kind": "perTree"` in `data/ground.json`), instead of a chance on every tile near a tree.
- **Tufts** down by a third (0.117 per tile).
- **The generic forest is 40% thinner:** the scatter inside the map (density 0.024 to 0.156) and the wild beyond it (0.054 to 0.174), both in `square.json`. Dense forest stays possible for a later zone; not for the first game.
- **Open question 27 raised:** what is decoration and what is an interactable (below).

### 2026-10-11 — Half the debris (Michael)

Michael on the new decals: the art is amazing, but there is far too much of it. **Ruling:** every decal except flowers comes down by about half. In `data/ground.json`: twigs 0.1, pebbles 0.03, tufts 0.175, mushrooms 0.125 per tile; leaves `perReach` 0.4 with `extraChance` 0.25 and `openChance` 0.06 (the per-tile count rounds down, so the leaf knobs were set by measuring: 469 leaf decals over four test chunks against 886 before). Flowers unchanged at first; then halved too (0.05 per open tile), the same as the rest (Michael).

### 2026-10-11 — The road under the shed stays (Michael)

The east road in `square.json` was authored before the shed and runs under it, so a two-tile dirt stub sits beside the shed and the shed's cobble path hides behind its far (west) wall. Michael: leave it; the map is meant to become customisable, and that is when it gets fixed. Floors already change through one function (`map.setFloor`) that re-bakes the ground; the tool to paint them (laying floors from the build palette, or the editor of question 14) does not exist yet.

### 2026-10-11 — Authored surfaces cover their tiles; edges reach outward (Michael)

Michael on the paths: the shed path looked like dirt until he opened the door (it was cobble shrunk to a hairline), and the cottage path let too much grass spill over it; that it looked right beside a house is not a rule, and roads and paths are hand-authored. **Ruling:** an authored surface (dirt, cobble, water) covers its own tiles and its ragged edge reaches only outward onto the grass; a laid floor beside an earth tile counts as that earth in the blend, so a path runs to the door. The pond stays as it is: blue ground you can walk on (Michael). Numbers and checks in `15-ground.md`.

### 2026-10-11 — Step 4 reviewed: round cobbles, a surface toggle, a tidier farm, a trial pond (Michael)

Michael on step 4: "amazing", "even less artificial"; the dirt edges and the foot-carved path to the cottage are right as they are. **Rulings:** the cobble is round stones packed at random, not rows (the rows read as a stone wall seen through a portal in the ground); a setting, Square and paths, cobble or dirt, switches the square and its two short paths together for comparison, for now (the east road stays dirt); the farm's edges are tidier, since a plot is kept by the player (threshold 0.40 + 0.12·noise); a trial 2×2 pond with a dirt bank is in the map, likely to be removed once seen. Details in `15-ground.md`.

### 2026-10-11 — Leaves, mushrooms, and new art asked for (Michael)

Michael on step 3: he loves the debris idea, but it looks "okayish": the 1×2-pixel leaves read like the spray-paint tool in old MS Paint, and everything is too small except perhaps the twigs. **Rulings:** "litter" is renamed **leaves** everywhere current (the decal rule, its files `leaves_<colour>_<n>.png`, the trees' `leaves` field, the code, the spec; the kit's brief in `15a` stays verbatim); mushrooms come up more often now (chance 0.10 to 0.25, one or two per pick: 92 to 351 over the same four chunks) and get bigger art; the step pauses while Michael asks Claude Design for two things, written up in `15b-art-requests.md`: the 36 trees redrawn natively at 1.5 times their old size (84×104 to 132×213), so the game stops enlarging them and they stop being blurry, and every decal redrawn bigger and readable, with sizes, perspective, palettes matched to the trees, and hard pixels. Step 4 (the earth blends) does not wait on either.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground is scoped: milestone 9 (Michael, scope by Claude)

Michael's four points (kill the grid and texture and blend the ground; the kit's decals as optional layers whose art may grow; it must work in the infinite forest; write the cobble rule now because cobblestone roads are coming) are answered in `15-ground.md`, scoped for implementation by another model. **Rulings made by the scope, all overridable in `data/ground.json` or the map file:** the ground is baked per chunk on the CPU in Canvas 2D and drawn as one quad through the existing shader (the brief's GPU dirt pass is the same maths run once at bake time; one code path, and the picture can be saved and looked at); the checker is gone everywhere and a faint grid shows only in build and remove mode; the kit's darker grass green is the grass now; **cobblestone is allowed** (re-deciding the vision's "no cobbles"), muted and warm, never concrete grey, and **always inside a dirt verge, never touching grass**, with the village square cobbled by default and the roads left dirt; the earth (grass, dirt, farm, cobble) blends with ragged pixel-crisp edges and laid floors (wood, stone) stay hard diamonds; trees cast a baked stepped shadow sized by the sprite; all decals bake flat for now; canopy reach and litter counts are per tree (2, 3, 4 tiles for the three tiers); felling a tree takes its shadow and its litter with it; the kit's missing textures (dirt, cobble, edge noise) are generated at boot behind the usual sprite definitions. The brief's flip-shares-a-bit-with-the-variant flaw is fixed with an independent roll.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground art conversation is open (Michael)

With the trees drawn, the flat checkered ground is what clashes. Michael made a ground kit with Claude Design (a brief, 27 files: six grass tiles and the decals, plus its generator script) and asked Claude to read it and say what it thought. **Ruling: the conversation is officially open; nothing is built and no spec is final.** The notes are in `15-ground.md`, with the brief kept verbatim in `15a-ground-brief-source.md`. Claude's read: the brief fits the renderer (light, ambient and fog are already sampled per pixel by world position, so baked unlit ground works), but four files it names are missing from the kit (the dirt texture, the edge noise, the before and after mockups), the brief's flip-on-low-bit rule is tied to its variant choice, the mockup's tree shadows are not in the text, and its tree-distance numbers assume the old, smaller trees. Michael and Claude talk it through before an official spec is written.

### 2026-10-11 — The trees' tiers are remapped and drawn bigger (Michael)

Michael, seeing the drawn trees in the forest: the smallest was only about as tall as the player ("way too short"), and the old generated giants read as a normal large tree, not as giants. **Ruling:** the small tier is dropped; the sheet's S is the game's `m`, M is `l`, L is the giant (`old`); all three are drawn **1.5 times bigger** (a `scale` in the sprite definition, applied once at load); giants are drawn art and **one tree in five** (weights in `square.json`, one number to tune). The generated giants are gone from the map (`generateTree` stays for the placeholder kinds). Trunk and wood stats follow the tier's new look: `m` 0.18 and 3, `l` 0.22 and 4, giant 0.3 and 6. The viewport's tall-sprite allowance rose from 110 to 230 px so the giants do not pop in at the bottom edge. Higher-resolution sprites would be crisper than the resample; that is Michael's call to make later.

### 2026-10-11 — Melee accepted for now (Michael)

After the mouse pass: melee feels good, the swing-target outline is great ("super duper legible"), multi-hit on and off both work as advertised. Called good. **Left to do, in order:** step 5 (painted held items, the way weapons get drawn); more one-handed weapons with art and stats; an answer on dual wielding (question 23); a usable shield (24); the bow and arrow (25), a careful design of its own; and finishing the trees, whose drawn sheet Michael is making with Claude Design (`06-art.md`). Status in `14-combat.md`.

### 2026-10-11 — Swing targets, and a pin for a sandbox screen (Michael)

- **A green outline on what the next swing would hit.** Deterministic (it is the swing's own test, so green means the swing lands), blind to the swing's cooldown, for testing and for players, a setting that is off by default. ~~Multi-hit stays on by default.~~ (superseded 2026-10-12: Multi-hit is the easy button, off by default) Design in `14-combat.md`, "Swing targets".
- **Pinned: a compact "sandbox settings" screen** to replace the growing list of single rows (design notes in `10-interface.md`). Until it exists the settings screen scrolls.

### 2026-10-11 — Melee tunables, the shove, and multi-hit (Michael)

- **Every number on a weapon is tunable per item**, now including `knockbackChance` and `stagger` beside `damage`, `knockback`, `knockdown`, `range`, `arc`, `swing`, and `stamina`. Fists and the shove are the same fields in `data/combat.json`. Table in `14-combat.md`.
- **The shove's push is cut to a third (1.6 to 0.53)**, as the weapons' knockbacks were. It had been left alone on the argument that a strong push is the move's point; in play it was far too far.
- **Multi-hit is a setting, on by default** *[superseded 2026-10-12: off by default, it is the easy button; the setting stays in the menu]*, with a per-weapon `multiHit: false` override. Off means a blow, and the shove, reach only the nearest zombie in the wedge.

### 2026-10-11 — No laser vision through the forest (Michael)

The soft see-through hole in a canopy is the player's alone. It was punched for every figure, so each zombie behind a tree showed through its own window, which gave away far too much. Now only the player gets it; a zombie behind a tree is hidden by the tree, by depth order, like anything else, and the floating health bar over a damaged zombie hides with it (`computeOcclusion` sets `behindProp` on every figure; the bar reads it), except within 3.5 tiles of the player, since in a dense forest nearly everything has some canopy in front and the zombie being fought must keep its bar, the finisher's tell. Sight (the fog set) is unchanged: trees still do not block line of sight, so a zombie in the open past a tree is still seen; only what the canopy covers is hidden.

### 2026-10-11, build — Removal and longer reach (Michael)

- **A removal tool** (X, or Remove on the build palette): highlight in green what was placed or built, click to destroy it. Props that can be carried and walls the player built are removable; authored walls, trees, the well, fires, and posts are not and never highlight. Containers spill their contents; nothing is refunded. Made to undo "built myself into a little wall box".
- **Reach is a full tile longer**: placing, building, and removing all reach 2.6 tiles instead of 1.6. `canCarry` and the prop hit rectangle moved into `placement.js` so interact and placement still import one way.

### 2026-10-11, build — Placement: carry rule, log wall, free building (Michael)

- **What can be carried is a rule**: anything not rooted (trees, the well), not built in (hearth, torch posts), and not a burning fire (the campfire). The jack-o'-lantern now lifts; it goes dark while carried and lights when set down. An explicit `carry` flag on a sprite overrides the rule. ~~The campfire is fixed until Michael says otherwise.~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: a campfire out and empty is picked up as an item)
- **A log wall, paid in logs.** Michael: build a log wall that consumes logs. Costs are `{ item: count }` in `data/build.json`: a log wall one log, a door three planks, a window two planks. One tree is three logs, so about three wall edges. The wall has its own sheet, `wall_log`, with a log pattern.
- **A Free building setting**, off by default, erases every cost so structures can be tested without gathering; build time still applies. Planks for boards (`B`) are untouched.
- **Building** rules the build forced: the palette on `N`, a `Step back` refusal when the player stands on the edge, and Escape closing the palette before leaving build mode.

### 2026-10-10, night — Goobers, and five pins (Michael, before bed)

- **The figures are goobers, not humans.** Stripped down they are the same small body, deliberately neutral in every way: no sex to the body, undies optional. Gender is only ever inferred from dress, hair, and facial hair, which the editors supply, and the game's own text uses they and them for every figure. The dead are goobers too. Michael: "if you strip em down, goobers." Ruled; written into `00-vision.md` tone rules and `12-figure.md`.
- **Pinned for the next conversations, in his order:**
  1. The older dev art: trees (the variety is right, the models are flat), campfire, hearth, bed, crate, well. The walls, openings, and figures are fine. A pass with the same care the figure got.
  2. The belt as its own paintable item, one design per belt item, instead of one shape with hide or show.
  3. Placement: one tool for building (walls, windows, doors) and placing (chest, hearth, chair) with a hover preview, R to rotate, a validity check, replacing "press E and hope" for carried furniture.
  4. Combat animation and item art before combat itself: what he can paint, how it plays, and a good one-handed swing first, since it serves sword, axe, mace, stick, and torch alike.
  5. The character creation screen: look, hair, facial hair, the painted layers, on the settings screen's pattern.

### 2026-10-10 — The figure (Michael)

- **The figure is a paper doll on a small rig, generated.** A naked body in undies, every garment its own sheet on the same frame grid drawn by the same rig code, stacked at draw time in a per-facing order; items held at hand joints; the dead on the same rig. Michael's question was how far dev art can go before commissioning: the answer is the rig, and the commission waits until the rig's numbers are frozen by play. Design in `12-figure.md`.
- **Eight facings drawn, none mirrored.** Michael: yes if the cost is small; for a generator it is nothing, and the main hand stays on its side. The mirror table remains for an artist who delivers five.
- **Chunky proportion, from the references.** Michael shared two image-model figures as vibe, not to copy: the head about a third of the height, shoulders no wider than the head, limbs fused to the body with only an outline between, one-pixel outline, two or three tones, accents doing the storytelling. "Habbo for a chonkier figure." The generator aims for those rules, not their detail.
- **Clothes do nothing yet** still holds; this milestone is how they look.
- **The editors to come (Michael, 2026-10-10, after painting a hair):** arms with shoulders, gloves, feet, and the helm may be drawn as irregular shapes per facing; chest and legs are pixel substitution on the standard silhouette; a helm is a hair; chest items get a length option and a hide-belt option in the editor; tunics are shirt length by default; a seam where boot meets leg. Design in `12-figure.md`, "The next editors".
- **The figure's drawing rules, from Michael's first art critique (2026-10-10):** outline per body part, never per garment, so no rolls down a limb; Habbo side shading, flat with one darker tone on the right share of each part, no edge rims; hair is a helmet over a rounded-square head and any hat replaces it; the face is drawn front-on with whites in the eyes and a small grim mouth; the tunic is eggshell so skin, shirt, and sack are three colours; hands hang beside the hips. The references: Habbo's figures and Stardew's overworld sprites, which both cheat the face toward the camera. The rig, the poses, and the joints did not change.
- **A torch in each hand gives more light and is seen from further** (Michael, 2026-10-10, on finding the rig let him hold two): about a third more radius than one, the mirror of the belt torch's half. The ladder is belt a half, one hand full, two hands a third more, and every rung trades hands for light and light for being seen. *(The belt rung is a little over half since 2026-10-17, `beltLight` 0.6: "A belt torch sees a bit further than none".)*
- **Built 2026-10-10, with one change found in the building:** outfits bake into one sheet per figure instead of stacking garment sheets at draw time, because a near arm must draw over the tunic and a far arm under it, which whole-figure layers cannot do. The rig's depth order decides, per part, and the result is one quad per figure as before. The player's sheet rebuilds in about thirty milliseconds on any change of look or clothes; the dead get ten baked outfits at boot; held items are overlays at the hand joints. The head came out at two fifths of the height after the first look read too small. The atlas grew to 4096.

### 2026-10-10, late — Containers and carrying (Michael)

- **One container rule: no container goes inside another unless it is empty.** A container is anything with a grid or belt slots: bags, belts, and any small world container that can be picked up later. An empty one rides in your bag like any item; once it holds something you wear it or drop it. Moving things between containers is already there to fix it. This closes question 20 and replaces the "no bag in a bag" rule of docs/11. In the interface: a non-empty container cannot be dragged into a grid, taken from a chest, stored in one, or picked up off the floor into your bag; the labels say "empty it first, or wear it". Clothes and belts on the floor offer Wear as bags do.
- **Big furniture can be carried.** Michael remembered this from the parent demo; neither parent had it, so it is new here. A prop whose sprite says `carry` (bed, table, chest, barrel for now) can be picked up from the right-click menu with both hands empty. While carrying you can only walk: no sprint, no climbing, no actions, no bag or character screen, no belt keys; it is drawn over your head. Put it down on clear tiles in reach from the menu, or with E on the tile you face. A chest or barrel comes with what is in it.

### 2026-10-10, late — The belt as utility slots, a belt torch, the zoom (Michael)

- **The belt is utility slots.** Michael withdrew his question about the belt's usefulness and answered it: tools are used from a hand, so the belt is for consumables and carry items. Food and drink can go on a belt slot, ~~as a stack~~ (narrowed 2026-10-19, Debuffs apart from moodles; only pemmican stacks; hearty food carries buffs; the belt knife is the Dirk: only pemmican stacks); its key eats or drinks one. Hand items still swap with the hand. "Health potions, etc." when there are such things. A confirmation on eating can come if it is ever eaten by accident. (Restated and added to (2026-10-19, The belt restated and added to; a book in the off hand costs the shield; the Holy Symbol is Faith's staff, within the proposal): a lantern carried, a left click as the key, the halberd never on the belt.)
- **A lit torch on the belt gives half its light, and the dead see it from half as far.** Michael: ignore that you would be on fire; it is a trade-off, free hands and a smaller glow against less sight, and perhaps an upgrade for stealth. It is not drawn on the body; only the glow. A torch in hand always wins over one on the belt. *(A little over half since 2026-10-17, `beltLight` 0.6: "A belt torch sees a bit further than none".)* No objection from me beyond the note that the light-at-player rule means the dead's plain sight of you also drops with the dimmer glow, which is the stealth he means.
- **The zoom snaps between three levels: wide 1.1×, middle 1.8×, close 2.7×.** Z cycles them; the wheel steps one way or the other, one step per 300 ms; the settings screen picks one; the level is kept in the browser. A smooth, eased zoom was built first and withdrawn within the hour: as the world slid under the cursor the player spun to follow the mouse and the view jerked, "definitely worse" (Michael). The three levels are about ten percent off either end of 1× to 3×, as he asked. Integer display scaling is withdrawn for the zoom: the GPU scales the world's pixels with nearest sampling, so at these levels some pixels are one screen pixel wider than their neighbours; Michael judges the look. Tiles, sprites, and the dev art are still drawn at whole pixels in world space.
- ~~**The spawn area follows the narrower view:** chunk growth back to three ahead.~~ (superseded 2026-10-13: Reading never grows a chunk: four chunks)
- **Survival is a setting, ~~off by default~~ (superseded 2026-10-13: Step 2 of `30` built: on by default), beside zombie aggro.** Off, nothing drains: no hunger, no thirst, and no cold or other need that comes later; the needs sit full and the moodles stay away. On, the body works as docs/10 says. With both off the game is a workbench where the only way to die is the console.

### 2026-10-10, night, later still — Double-click, zoom (Michael)

- **Double-click uses a thing.** In your bag it equips what can be held and wears what can be worn; on the belt it draws; on a hand it unequips; on a worn slot it takes off. Anything else, or anything in a container, moves across as before.
- **Every slot's menu has Drop**, which puts the thing at your feet without passing through the sack. A belt or a bag dropped this way is just a container on the floor, with what it holds still in it.
- ~~**Zoom is the wheel stepping the world's integer scale, 1× to 3×.**~~ Replaced the same night by the continuous zoom above. Was: Down zooms out. It writes the world-scale setting, so it sticks. Smooth zoom is foreclosed by the pixel art: anything but a whole multiple shimmers, and that is the Habbo look we are keeping. Nothing the dead do depends on the view; aggro, sight, and the active region are all in tiles. The range is deliberately small, in Michael's words "a bit in or a bit out, not Zomboid's whole-field zoom": at 1× on a 1080p window you see what Zomboid shows at 100 percent; 2× is the old 960×540 framing; 3× is a close-up.

### 2026-10-10, night, later — Michael's notes on the screens

- **The bag stays top-right; ~~the character screen is dead centre, on its own~~.** (superseded 2026-10-12: The bag and the character screen are two panels: at the left edge; then The theme is kept: panels move and remember their place) Opening one never moves the other. Michael did not like the bag shooting across the screen when C followed Tab.
- **Zombie aggro is a setting, ~~off by default for now~~ (superseded 2026-10-15: The wiki census's surprises ruled: on by default, still a setting).** Off, the dead spawn and wander forever and never notice the player or a flame; they are there to show spawn distribution while things are tested. On, they hunt as before. When the game is a game the default flips; until then this is the workbench.
- **The dead kill.** The player can die. Death stops the game on a dead screen with the day and hour; the one way out is back to the title, which is a fresh load of the page. A load is fine (the law above). Starvation and thirst kill too, which with aggro off is the only way to die.
- **Save game** sits on the pause menu, disabled, so it is not forgotten. Saving comes later.

### 2026-10-10, night — The view, the screens, and a law (Michael)

- **A law: if the game is good, people will sit for a loading screen.** Michael, in his words: never let that squash good ideas, "in the same way I keep letting what I assume the browser can't do (like dynamic lighting) stop me from actually doing things the way I truly want." Written into `docs/00-vision.md` under Laws, where it outranks the pillars.
- **The world fills the window.** Michael: the inability to have the whole screen be the game "somehow makes it feel less real to me, like keeping you at a distance." The 960×540 viewport (question 2) was my assumption, never his; it is withdrawn. ~~The world is drawn at the largest integer pixel scale at which 960×540 still fits the window, and the canvas is the whole window at that scale.~~ (superseded 2026-10-10, late: The belt as utility slots, a belt torch, the zoom: three fractional zoom levels the GPU applies, the canvas still the whole window) On a 1920×910 window that is 1× and about 2,800 tiles in view, which is what Zomboid shows at 100 percent on 1080p, so the ruling's reason survives. ~~The pixel art stays integer-scaled;~~ (superseded 2026-10-10, late: The belt as utility slots, a belt torch, the zoom) `view.js` owns the sizes. Nothing in the game may be laid out against 960×540 again.
- **The interface has its own scale, like a web page.** The UI layer is DOM over the whole window at a fractional scale of its own, chosen for legibility (auto: about 85 percent of what the probe size would scale to, in quarter steps), and tunable. World-anchored UI (the prompt, the message, the right-click menu) converts through `view.toUi`.
- **Screens.** A title screen at boot over the drawn village (Play, Settings, Controls); the pause menu on Escape with nothing open (Resume, Settings, Controls, Title screen); a settings screen; a controls screen. Any screen up means the game is paused. Escape steps back one screen. Michael: having these now makes tunable settings cheap when he asks for them, which is soon.
- **Settings live in the browser**, not in a save: interface size, world pixel scale, the debug corner. More as they are asked for.
- **The light and fog textures follow the view**, sized to cover it from any camera position; ~~chunk growth widens from three to four chunks ahead so nothing materialises in a big window's view.~~ (superseded 2026-10-13: Reading never grows a chunk: a ring of four chunks round each player, grown by the simulation alone)

### 2026-10-10, after play — The character, Michael's notes

- **The hands are slots like any other.** Drag an item onto a hand and it goes there, whatever was held swapping out to the bag or belt; drag out of a hand to the bag, the belt, or the floor. Michael wants parity between hand items and clothes. This replaces the ruling below that the hands were drawn and never dropped on; keys 1 to 4 and the item menu stay as the quick ways.
- **A torch goes in either hand.** An item's `hands` may now be `either`: it takes a free hand, the main one first, or the hand it is dropped on.
- **The starter kit is poor and says so.** A rope belt and a burlap sack, so better ones found in chests read as better. No hood to start: the head slot begins empty.
- **~~The character screen sits in the centre of the view~~ (superseded 2026-10-12: The theme is kept: panels move and remember their place), laid out like an RPG doll** from Michael's drawing: the belt top-left, the bag top-right, head, chest, legs, and feet down the middle, a hand either side, gloves under the main hand, the bottom corners free for later. The bag column stands beside it. Tab alone keeps the bag in the top-right corner.
- **~~Only hand items go on the belt;~~ (superseded (2026-10-10, late, The belt as utility slots, a belt torch, the zoom): food and drink go there too) a belt may ride in a bag; ~~a bag never goes in a bag~~.** (superseded 2026-10-10, late: Containers and carrying: one container rule, an empty one may) Stated so it is clear. Whether a bag may go inside a bigger bag is open question 20.

### 2026-10-10, later — The character (Michael)

Milestone 5, before combat: the worn screen and a hotbar, designed in `11-character.md`.

- **Everything is a slot, nothing is a pointer.** An item is in one place: a hand, a worn slot, the belt, a bag, a container, or the floor. A held item leaves the bag; this reverses the interface ruling that it stayed in the bag outlined. Michael, on the two readings of a hotbar: a bookmark leaves the sword eating bag cells, a free four-slot strip is storage outside the grid; both rejected.
- **Carrying capacity is always a worn item.** The belt gives the hotbar its slots (two on a plain belt, four on a good one), and the bag on your back gives the grid. Michael's addition and his reason: if the hotbar ties to wearing a belt, the bag must tie to wearing a bag. Zomboid does the same, but that is not why.
- **The bag is an item.** You start wearing a small sack; the items belong to it, not to you. Drop it and everything goes with it; open it on the floor like a chest; wear a bigger one and move things over. ~~A bag never goes inside a bag;~~ (superseded 2026-10-10, late: Containers and carrying: one container rule, an empty one may ride in a bag) picking one up with one on offers Swap. No bag on your back means no grid, with a message. A Developer Bag of twelve by eight comes from the console, never loot; bags are not craftable yet.
- ~~**The hands are drawn on the character screen, never dropped on.**~~ Overruled the same evening, above: the hands are slots with full drag parity. The item menu and the number keys remain.
- **Worn things do nothing yet** beyond their slot. Hood, tunic, gloves, breeches, boots, belts, bags as plain blocks; the first four are the sprite layers of the next conversation.

### 2026-10-10 — Openings (Michael)

The milestone after the renderer: the dead get in. Design in `09-openings.md`. **Accepted the same day**: "Tested and working as desired." His first night found four things, fixed: boards drawn by count, the search staying on the searcher's side of the walls, a flame unable to retake a zombie for eight seconds after it saw you, and a stuck pair dropping their path.

- **Interface before combat.** Michael, 2026-10-10: before the melee port, the inventory, right-click menus, and click to move. Designed the same day in `10-interface.md`, where the parent's rulings are re-decided: **the grid with no weight** (6×4), **right-click as the complete interface with E as the shortcut for the nearest simple action**, **out-of-range actions auto-walk**, **left-click on the floor walks there** (new), **hands are the two slots** with the item staying in the bag outlined, **containers are bags that close when you walk away**, **logs and planks become items** with Chop down and Split as the minimal wood loop, **hunger and thirst as moodles** with ~~**the well as infinite water** (question 16 closed)~~ (superseded 2026-10-12: The big pass answered: drink and fill from every large water, the well among them), nothing saved yet. After play the same day: **left-click to walk withdrawn** (left click stays free for selecting and placing; Walk here in the menu is plenty); **Escape closes the top thing that can close, then pauses** (menu, bag, pause, resume); **a log is 2×3 and a plank 1×3, a log splits into three planks**, so storing wood as logs is denser, the opposite of Zomboid's log problem.

- **Boards only, no bars.** Michael, on reading the first draft: bars and boards together are too fussy. Planks on either side of a door or a window, two or three a side, and the leaf (the door itself, or the shutters) has health of its own. One kind of thing, one number.
- **An opening is a stack, read from the attacker's side**: that side's boards, then the leaf, then the far side's boards. A zombie outside a door boarded on both sides breaks the outside boards, then the door, then the inside boards, and then it is an opening again. Nothing can be walked or pathed through while a board stands on either side. One rule, no special cases.
- **Everything is spent, nothing is repaired.** Michael: if the dead break your door, you do not have a door anymore; the boards they break are gone; pulling your own board is gone too. No repair; the opening stays an opening. Open question 6 is closed by this: spent. Wood is what you go out for: planks for the walls and logs for the fires.
- **One shambler takes a while.** A bare door should cost one zombie tens of seconds and a fully boarded one more than a minute; a Romero shambler does not kick a door down in a few seconds. Numbers tuned by eye in `09-openings.md`.
- ~~**The dead climb through open windows**, slower than you and unable to bite while they do. Michael was about to ask for this; it is this milestone.~~ (superseded 2026-10-16: Nobody climbs through a window; region is the word, #134)
- **The dead attack the room's nearest opening.** When the way to what they want is shut, they go to the nearest opening of the room it is in on their own side, and hit the stack until it gives (narrowed 2026-10-16, Nobody climbs through a window; region is the word: never a window as their way in; #134). Nothing ever breaks a wall (narrowed 2026-10-17, The siege: a sealed room is a cell, the dead wait for dawn, a crowd bursts a fence: a crowd bursts a fence; and, as Primary's default in #147, the dead go first to the opening they saw you use). Noise stays out (question 18).
- **Sealed is derived** from a room's openings each step: every door closed and whole, every window shuttered or boarded and whole. It gates sleep later and costs nothing now (sleep also waits out a siege, 2026-10-17, The siege: a sealed room is a cell, the dead wait for dawn, a crowd bursts a fence).

### 2026-10-09, night — The renderer goes to WebGL (Michael)

Built the same night, all three phases (`08-renderer.md`). Michael at Phase 2: "MILES better, and I could not produce a single weird polygon bug anymore. Wall shading suddenly feels way more intuitive." At the end of Phase 3: "THIS is the right system. It looks amazing." Accepted; the Canvas renderer is deleted. The light debug view is "like looking at the map with infrared goggles on."

Michael took the lighting problem to an outside agent with only the lighting and scaling parts of the game described, and brought back a spec (`medieval_pixel_world_game_spec.md`, in his downloads) recommending PixiJS on WebGL. Read together on 2026-10-09; most of its architecture is what we already have (authoritative world data, derived light maps per chunk, materials apart from art, emitters as data, rooms found not authored). Rulings:

- **Raw WebGL2, not PixiJS, not Canvas 2D for the world.** Michael: libraries like PixiJS exist to make this easier for humans to write; with me writing it, raw WebGL costs little and we own the pipeline. The reason it is worth doing now: everything he was still dissatisfied with is light per pixel (a pool climbing a wall, his own pool on the wall beside him, a tree lit at the trunk and dark at the crown), which Canvas 2D cannot do and a shader does by sampling a light texture at each pixel. The Canvas 2D ordered pass stays until the WebGL renderer reaches parity, then goes. Canvas 2D keeps generating the placeholder art, which uploads as textures. The design is `08-renderer.md`.
- **Portals are emitters.** An opening is a light source on its darker side with the brighter side's ambient as its intensity, times its transmission. At night a lit room's open window and door cast warm patches outside whether or not the hearth is within its radius of them; by day the same openings pool daylight on the floor inside, which replaces the flat per-room openness number with light by each window. One rule for both directions. Michael: matches what he wants from windows, daylight pooling included.
- **Openings carry a transmission scalar**, not a yes or no: a plain wall and a closed door 0, an open shutter and an open doorway 1, a closed shutter a small leak to tune, and later a glass pane most and a curtain little. Light multiplies through; sight is transmission above a threshold. Same one set of blockers, now with a number.
- **The floor field exists.** Ruled on 2026-10-08 and never added: chunks and lookups take a layer, always 0 for now.
- **A pause on gameplay for this is accepted.** Michael: get this right while we are very early, since openings and combat will be written against the drawable interface this produces. He also ruled that my wall-clock estimates are worthless and I should give scope instead.
- **From the spec, taken:** flicker as emitter data, a light-map sampling toggle (nearest is his per-cell backburner, now one line), a debug view of the light map and blockers. **Ignored, as Michael agreed:** TypeScript, 32-tile chunks, top-down Y sorting, Tiled, building as first-class now (question 14 stays open at zero cost), and its vertical slice, which ours exceeds except for construction and save.

### 2026-10-09, afternoon — Torches, fire, hands, and the clock (Michael)

The conversation that opens Milestone 2. Its design is `07-lighting.md`.

- **Every holdable item has a hands designation: `main`, `off`, or `both`.** A torch and a shield are off-hand; a sword, a hatchet, a knife, and tools are main; a bow and a great axe need both. **[Superseded 2026-10-12: the sword, hatchet, mace, stick, plank, and torch are `either` (dual wielding); the shield is off only; the spear is main only and never beside another weapon; the halberd and bow are both.]** A two-handed item empties both slots and an off-hand item is refused while both are full, so a torch at night costs you the shield and the bow. Michael: assign it to everything now, it preps the character gear screen he may ask for. The spear is tabled until combat. No item system exists yet, so lighting brings a minimal item registry and two hand slots on the player ahead of the inventory port; the torch is item number one.
- ~~**Torches never burn out.** Lit while held or mounted, unlit in a bag. No fuel, no tinder, no lighting from a flame. Michael: torches burning down is the unfun kind of busywork, and the pillar agrees: things run out because you used them, and a torch on a bracket is not being used up, so a timer on it would be the shutoff the pillar forbids.~~ (superseded 2026-10-17: Torches burn down; the tinderbox; the village's lights become lanterns; the village's own lights become lanterns that never go out, and a torch you light burns down by your choice.) The wood economy lives in the hearth and the campfire. The Minecraft trade is the cost instead: **a torch cannot be placed on the ground.** It mounts in a **bracket**, which is state on a wall edge like a shutter, or on a **post**, a prop built from wood outdoors. Lighting the whole grounds costs a post per pool, once. A torch dropped on the ground is an unlit item lying there.
- **At night the dead see fire from far away.** One rule for every flame: a zombie within a long range (twelve tiles to start) with a clear line to a light source walks to it, treating the flame as a sighting, so the memory rule does the rest; a person is noticed at the normal short range, scaled by the light on them as in the cozy parent. A carried torch, a campfire in the yard, a hearth seen through an open shutter, and a jack-o'-lantern all pull the same way; closed shutters are how you hide a fire. Michael's intent: a lit guy in a forest is far more visible than a guy in the pitch dark, and fire should pull from off screen too. No memory-length rule yet; longer sight already makes them lose you less. Off screen, the same number becomes the per-chunk lure in `03-world-model.md` once records exist.
- **Fires are data.** Each fire kind declares its light, whether it burns wood, whether it cooks, and where it may be placed. A **campfire** burns wood, goes out, cooks (a hanging pot, no model needed), lights a medium pool, and is built on any outdoor walkable tile with no prop. A **hearth** ~~burns wood, goes out,~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: a switch, no fuel) cooks, lights its whole room and spills a pool through its openings, sits indoors against a wall, is rare in authored houses, and is buildable later. A mounted or held **torch** lights a small pool, burns nothing, cooks nothing. The **jack-o'-lantern** is a tiny source with no fuel. A **furnace** comes later with the smithy, burns, and forges rather than cooks. Michael: people who truly want the grounds lit can maintain a ring of campfires.
- **A lit hearth lights its whole room.** The cozy parent's room-light rule, with the hearth as the switch. A great hall may want a cap by room size; decide when one exists.
- **The clock comes first.** Lighting has nothing to do without a night, so Milestone 2 starts with the parent's clock: a twelve-minute day, brightness from full to about 0.22 at night, never black, a debug key to skip an hour. The night tint is our dusky purple, not the parent's blue, and whether full day is ever quite full is decided by eye, since Halloween never ends here. Sleep, routines, and the trickle hang off the same clock later.
- **The fog becomes light.** At night an unlit tile in your line of sight is dark, and ~~the dead standing in it are drawn dim, never hidden; the parent found that not drawing them read as spawning~~ (superseded 2026-10-16: The needs-michael queue, darkness shortens sight, #88). The player's night-vision pool is small and weak: going dark is possible but blind.
- **Milestone 2 accepted, "good for now."** Michael, end of 2026-10-09, after four rounds of play and fixes on the night layer: not perfect, "this is why people use game engines", and good enough to build on. ~~Noted for later: switching the near walls back to low stubs (still on the `C` key) removes the translucent-wall mixing that caused most of the day's lighting oddities, since a stub has no face for the room to show through.~~ (superseded 2026-10-11: Final rulings on the plan, and pumpkins join the gatherables: stubs retire as a setting) Nothing more is tuned on the night layer until something else needs it.
- **What takes which light.** Michael, from play on 2026-10-09, after a shed's hidden back wall kept the dark of the room behind it and a canopy over a lit room lit up: exterior walls light like the ground and trees, seen or not; the top of a chunky wall is never shaded by the room; and everything standing on a tile takes the light of its own tile. Michael asked whether per-cell light would fix it; it would not, this is about which thing takes which light, and per-cell stays on the backburner for the look of the pools only. Written up in `07-lighting.md`, "How things are lit".
- **Logs are free until you can cut a tree.** Michael: pretend you always have logs on you; `E` feeds a burner as if from the inventory, and consumption rates are not worth testing until trees fall and logs go in a bag. No woodpile prop. The burn rate still runs so a fire still goes out.
- **The light layer may be smoothed.** Michael liked what the parent was going for: the two-cell-per-tile erase map drawn through the iso transform with image smoothing on. An exception to "never scale in `drawImage`" for that one layer, which is a gradient and not pixel art. Per-cell chunky light stays on the backburner in case the first pass does not please.
- **Campfires go on any outdoor walkable tile with no prop.**
- **Open question 8 is closed**: a carried radius, not a cone or a shuttered lantern. The lantern moves to `05-someday.md` as a toggle upgrade. **Open question 17 is closed**: ~~the hearth burns wood and goes out~~ (overturned 2026-10-25, The dead ends ruled: a home's fire is a switch, no fuel).

### 2026-10-09 — Milestone 1 passed, and the vibe pass (Michael)

- **Lighting is next, and it finishes the vibe pass.** Michael, end of 2026-10-09: the last thing needed to complete the vibe is a real lighting system like the cozy parent's, where candles changed the light of a room rather than glowing; from there every gameplay decision is more fun to test. The parent's art looked good but felt a little sterile; this is a totally different place. Milestone 2 is lighting. Torches, carried and placed, are discussed first (open question 8).
- **Fog lighter again** (0.36) and **visibility samples a tile's corners** when its centre is hidden, which removes most of the pizza-slice slivers behind wall ends. **The player has sleeves** and a worn, yellowed shirt (`#c9b98e`, sleeves `#bfae84`) instead of new linen. This supersedes the off-white shirt below.
- **Sets and features.** Michael's terminology (2026-10-09), adopted: a **set** is any authored thing with a footprint and a padding ring that random spawns never enter, from a wagon to a castle; a **feature** is a road, river, or bridge, linear, reserving only its own tiles. A road joining two sets does not merge them, because a set is defined by its own outline. A set's footprint may be any shape: a rectangle, a union of rectangles, or a text mask. The map file now has `sets` and `features`; scatter and the wild fill the rest. Density lowered by about 28 percent at both ends the same day: everything had read as dense forest.
- **Forest density drifts, buildings get a padding ring, and the wild is forest.** Michael, 2026-10-09, after the trunk fix landed where he wanted it (most things passable, some slow you, few impassable): three tree sizes by four colours in an even mix, density that wanders from thicket to one tree in six or eight tiles, and no trees on or beside buildings. Built as: a smooth noise field that scales each scatter rule's density; a `padding` ring (default one tile) around every set and `reserve` rectangles for yards and fields, which take no scatter; and a `wild` rule for every chunk outside the authored bounds, which grows harvest forest and seeds zero to two of the dead per chunk. ~~The world is grown three chunks ahead of the player so nothing appears in view.~~ (superseded 2026-10-13: Reading never grows a chunk: a ring of four chunks round each player) Zones are a named set of kinds and a noise scale; the village and the wild both use `harvest`, so gloom is always a place you choose. The dead beyond forty tiles stand still until the records milestone.
- **Cutting trees down is not precluded.** A tree is a prop on a tile; felling one is clearing that tile's prop reference and dropping wood. Nothing in the renderer or the map caches a tree anywhere else.
- **Trees block only their trunk.** Whole-tile collision made the wood impenetrable and would get you killed (Michael, 2026-10-09). A sprite definition may declare a collision radius: small, medium, and large trees are 0.14, 0.18, and 0.22 tiles, a giant 0.3, a barrel 0.26, and against round things a body's radius counts as 0.2 instead of 0.3, so two trunks a tile apart leave real room. A step into a trunk deflects along its tangent instead of stopping. Tiles with a solid prop stay unwalkable for pathing, so the dead go around; Michael wants forests to be a place you can lose them, since Zomboid's are too dangerous to be fun. Revisit if it ever feels overpowered.
- **Harvest first, gloom later, by zone.** The blue and purple trees beside the orange looked like Halloween candy. Zones are the answer, so for now the whole map is the harvest register (dusky orange, yellow, red, dark green, plus a few old giants) and the gloomy register's trees stay on file for a zone added later, along with the plague doctor and the rest. Get "harvest zombies farming simulator" fun first, then expand. Zones are in `05-someday.md`.
- **Authored structure, scattered filler.** Michael (2026-10-09): author the roads, the buildings, and any water; randomly place the rest, trees as forests, overturned carts to loot, events. That is Zomboid's and Stardew's split too. Built as a `scatter` rule in the map file: a rectangle, a density, a weighted mix of kinds, a seed. Scatter lands only on the default floor, never on a wall's tile, never inside a set's padding, never on a placed prop, and the same seed grows the same wood. Carts and events are later kinds of the same rule.
- **Gloomwood, two registers.** Michael's vibe statement is in `06-art.md`: an aggressively autumn cosy register and a Tirisfal gloomy register, balanced so farming never feels bad. Trees are big and there is more forest than plains. ~~Trees are in green, dusky blue, purple, and the odd amber.~~ Superseded the same day by "harvest first, gloom later": blue and purple are on file for a zone of their own.
- **Occlusion is a hole, not a fade.** A character behind a tree shows through a soft hole cut in every canopy over them, so overlapping big trees never stack into mud. The silhouette pass is gone.
- ~~**The player wears a dirty off-white shirt and brown breeches.** No denim.~~ Superseded the same day by the yellowed shirt with sleeves, above. Brown breeches and no denim still hold. The dead keep their dusty purple.
- **A jack-o'-lantern**, because it is October: a carved pumpkin with a stem and a flickering fire inside, three on the square. The first animated prop, and a light source once lighting lands.
- **The pumpkin's glow joins the lighting pass.** When dynamic lighting lands, the jack-o'-lantern lights the ground around it the way candles did in the cozy parent; the flicker inside stays as drawn. Noted in the lighting milestone.
- **~~Open shutters and~~ (superseded 2026-10-11: Cottage shutters, and a wall height preview: open shutters fold back about 160 degrees; doors still stand at 90) doors swing out on the outside of the wall**, perpendicular to it at the jambs, as if pushed open from inside. History be damned. The renderer knows which side is outdoors from the rooms.
- **Fittings are one wood everywhere.** Doors and shutters are the same wood whatever the wall, as vertical planks with dark metal strips across them, two on a door and one on a shutter leaf. Michael: "I know this is not a thing but I like how it sounds."
- **Doors, second.** Doors are real now that windows work: tap E opens or closes, closed blocks sight and bodies, the dead cannot open them yet (the openings milestone decides how they get through). Doors start closed.
- **Dark medieval fantasy, not strictly medieval.** Michael: skeletons with sword and shield, plague doctors, scarecrow men would fit; medieval must not be a ceiling that shuts down ideas, and the dark-fantasy vibe suits the RPG leanings. Tone rule updated in `00-vision.md`. Nothing fantastical is scheduled.
- **It is a Place.** Michael, after testing zombie memory: better than either parent, it feels like playing Zomboid, the contrast with the art is noticeable and fun, and the cottage and shed read as grounds he hangs out at rather than a test space. The probe's purpose is served.
- **Corner posts.** Where walls meet at a corner, end, or junction, a post of the wall's thickness fills the notch the thickness leaves. Stone wins over wood where they meet. A straight run gets no posts.
- **Every window has wooden shutters, worked the Zomboid way.** Tap E to open or close, ~~hold E to climb through once open. Checked against Zomboid's own description: a quick press does one thing, a hold does the other~~ (superseded 2026-10-16: Nobody climbs through a window; region is the word, #134). Closed shutters block sight. ~~Climbing takes 0.9 s and cannot be interrupted: a window is a desperate exit, not a shortcut.~~ Shutters start closed. This replaces the probe's glass: there is no pane in this village (`06-art.md`), so the sibling's curtains and broken glass do not carry; boards may.
- **The dead know only where they last saw you.** Michael drew the case: he ran a route around a house with solid walls and the dead cut the corner straight to him, as if they had his coordinates. They did: chase was pathing to the player's live tile. Now a zombie remembers the last place it saw you, walks there, searches around it for ten seconds, and gives up (narrowed 2026-10-17, The siege: a sealed room is a cell, the dead wait for dawn, a crowd bursts a fence: the dead that know you are inside wait until dawn). No biting through walls either: a bite needs line of sight. **Noise is deliberately not a factor yet**; Michael is unsure it fits the cosy idea and with no guns there is nothing loud. Open question 18.
- **The stamina bar is the first UI**, because Michael sprints everywhere. Sprint lasts about six seconds instead of two and a half.
- **The fog is lighter.** It shades what you cannot see clearly but never distracts from what is there. 0.74 alpha was near black; now 0.46 in a dusky purple. (Lowered again to 0.36 later the same day, above.)
- **Dev art gets the game's visual identity now.** Spooky medieval, a bit haunted, never permanently night; Tirisfal Glades, not Stardew (narrowed 2026-10-25, Cheery at home, spooky beyond: the outer regions' register, the valley cheery). Thick walls, planks and bricks, open windows, dirt roads, a farm tile, dull grass. The character placeholders were kept at first; the player's clothes changed later the same day (above) and the dead are unchanged. See `06-art.md`, which also records the genre test for any new colour.
- **One screen is a few huts, a small square, part of a castle, or barely all of a small one. That is plenty.** Michael: all of those are scenarios where one screen would be generous. ~~960×540 stands, no zoom.~~ (superseded 2026-10-10, night: The view, the screens, and a law: the world fills the window; three zoom levels since The belt as utility slots, a belt torch, the zoom) The countryside stays in `05-someday.md`.
- ~~**Players can go through windows.** Michael, on seeing the wedge: "now we can let players go through windows!" Open question 5 is settled: the living and the dead both, with the details (slow, loud, broken or open) decided at the openings milestone.~~ (superseded 2026-10-16: Nobody climbs through a window; region is the word, #134)
- **Near walls draw full height and translucent when you are inside.** Michael likes seeing the full wall. ~~Stubs stay on the `C` key for now and may come back later.~~ (superseded 2026-10-11: Final rulings on the plan, and pumpkins join the gatherables: stubs retire as a setting, the idea kept in `05`) This supersedes the stub plan that `03-world-model.md` carried until the docs pass later on 2026-10-09.
- **All four proofs hold.** Michael's words: the continuous map does not feel more "real" than the dioramas, which were legible, but it feels cohesive and spatially real the way Zomboid does; the window wedge is "spooky how much like Zomboid it feels"; the viewport is plenty. The engine question is answered. The next question is whether the game is fun.

### 2026-10-08 — The project exists (Michael)

- **A new project, not a rewrite of either parent.** Both parents stay playable as they are. This game borrows by copying. Reason: the two things Michael missed, knights and moving fluidly between buildings, belong to one game that is neither parent, and a fork leaves nothing broken behind.
- **New game, new gospel.** Rulings from either parent are things to consider, not things to obey. Each is re-decided here when it comes up. Michael's words.
- **Medieval, low fantasy, no guns.** Michael's reasons, in order: a modern setting invites "worse Zomboid" in every sentence and medieval ends the comparison at once; he wants knights and swords; guns felt too powerful. On the last we agreed the gun's real fault was an illegible cost (alarm elsewhere, minutes later) that was fixable in place, so it is not the reason.
- **One continuous map with line of sight replaces the dollhouse.** Fluid movement from building to building is the thing neither parent gives and the reason this one exists. Accepted costs: the whole-room-at-once identity, the 12×12 node, the two-far-walls convention. The cozy parent's lighting is what made real line of sight look worth having.
- **Cute wins over grim.** The dead are drawn like everyone else. Deaths are still stated plainly.
- **Light feeds sight.** Darkness hides and lamps reveal, as in the cozy parent. The whole point of carrying its lighting is that it is mechanical.
- **Milestone 1 is a probe, and nothing else is built until it passes.** Four proofs in `00-vision.md`. If one fails, stop and talk.
- **Chunks replace nodes.** Michael's proposal, ruled 2026-10-08 with these specifics:
  - **The map is stored block by block, from the start.** Michael: one file cuts off too many opportunities, as long as it does not impose on the browser or multiply the code. It does neither: a map that is a dictionary of 16×16 chunks with one `tileAt` lookup is the same amount of code as a flat array, and loading on demand is one fetch per chunk plus "a missing chunk is empty field". A ring of chunks is fetched ahead of the active region so nothing hitches. This makes authored-versus-procedural a live question (below): a hand-authored village with generated fields between villages is now possible.
  - **Active radius 2 chunks.** The dead become real two screens out and take about 40 s to arrive. Michael's preference. Cost is trivial.
  - **Break-ins happen only slightly off screen.** Inside the active region the dead are real and break in as usual, seen or not. Beyond it they are notes and do nothing to buildings. Michael: the dead breaking into houses that do not contain the player is not a priority. No "note chews on a lured house" rule.
  - **Rooms by flood fill, never authored.** Same reason as block storage: it keeps building and the dev tool possible.
- **Fire and resources are the economy; nothing shuts off on a timer.** Michael, 2026-10-08, on what separates this from Zomboid while the chunked world moves toward it: the setting, and the absence of Zomboid's infrastructure arc (water shutoff, power cut, broadcasts going dead). "Fire is everything, and every natural resource is so important." Things run out because you used them: wood for the hearth, water from the well, seed in the ground. Added to `00-vision.md` as a pillar. My lean on the first consequence: the hearth burns firewood and goes out without it, which is the cozy parent's parked "candle burn time" made central rather than fiddly.
- **Farming is in, and it is load-bearing.** Michael: simpler than Zomboid's, closer to Stardew; big for him and for cozy games in general; "if it doesn't work the game probably won't." Cooking comes with it, in some simple fashion. Both are now in the vision and the milestone sketch. The cozy parent's take (planters with three growth stages that need water; cooking as one step with no recipes) is an input.
- **NPCs are in, killable (narrowed 2026-10-18, The valley first; food heals a little; teachers are safe; the wizard; the dead strike what they break: a villager who teaches a skill is immune, as the guide is), and not crippled for the browser's sake.** Michael, 2026-10-08. They carry no weapons and never fight for the player; that is the compromise and it holds. They **flee** from the dead they see until they cannot, then die and turn, so a single wanderer in your base does not kill a man standing still. The player can tell one to **follow** or to **stay** on a tile, which is how you bring a villager home and keep him. Beyond the active region they are frozen notes like the dead. See `04-npcs.md`.
- **Quests: never say never, but not for a good long while.** The game has to be playable, fun, and content-rich first. RPG-like elements were part of the original medieval wish and are not forgotten. Logged in `05-someday.md`.
- **~~Probe viewport 960×540~~ (superseded 2026-10-10, night: The view, the screens, and a law: the world fills the window), no roofs.** Michael accepted both. Nothing may be written in a way that forecloses roofs; they sit in `05-someday.md`.
- **Single storey first.** Michael agreed on 2026-10-08. Tile data carries a layer index from day one so upper floors can be added without a format change; how they render is decided after the probe.
- **The browser can do this.** Checked before starting: Canvas 2D drew the cozy parent's shadowed light layer at 60 fps with headroom; a scrolling camera draws a few hundred diamonds a frame; visibility is the same ray march as the light map with the player as the source; A\* for fifty dead on a few thousand tiles is trivial. The hard parts are art rules, not browser limits.

## Open questions

Only the open ones live here. The answered ones, as they were written, are in `docs/history/02-build-log.md` under "Open questions, answered", and the six below as they stood before the sweep shortened them are there too. A question answered by a ruling above is struck here and moved there at the next sweep; a question that has become work leaves for a GitHub issue.

10. **Day counter as a score.** Alongside survivors, per survivor, on the death card (lean: yes). The dead screen already shows the day and hour and the Worlds row the day; what is left is whether a best run is kept and shown. A design question nobody has to answer yet: to `05-someday.md`.
18. **Noise.** Whether anything the player does carries to the dead by sound. Michael (2026-10-09) doubts it fits the cosy idea, and with no guns little is loud; a bow is quiet by design. Lean: leave it out until something in the game is loud, then decide whether that thing should carry. To `05-someday.md`.
27. **Gathering's remainder.** The law (the wobble and the outline), the actions and their tools (`30` section 4), crops, pumpkins, mushrooms, and rocks are ruled and built. Still open: whether picked things regrow, and whether rocks block sight. To `05-someday.md`.
~~29b. **The spin-click cap.** Of "fights cost too little" (2026-10-12), (a) stagger by weight and (c) bleed with the infection marker are built; (b), a turn-rate cap while a swing recovers, or a stamina or recovery cost for turning past about 90 degrees between swings, is not, and Michael paused combat changes. To a `needs-michael` issue (wave 4).~~ (closed 2026-10-23, The melee feel pass: no cap; the commitment is a slowdown through the swing)
~~31. **Water and thirst** (Michael, 2026-10-18, with the bucket: "if wells are infinite the thirst mechanic may become pointless"). Today any water tile and any well fills a skin for nothing, and a carried skin sips by itself (2026-10-16), so thirst asks only that a skin be carried. What thirst is for, and whether water should cost something, is open (*Primary's examples:* clean and foul water, boiling at a fire, wells that run low and refill, the last weighed against "never harder on a clock").~~ (answered 2026-10-19, Natural water is unsafe until purified; mana; drain heals never replace food; a region gates its spells: natural water is unsafe until purified)
32. **A tool carried, or in hand?** (Michael, 2026-10-18: "in your inventory, if not also equipped, tbd"). Built so far: the hatchet, the rod, and the hoe must be in a hand. Open: whether every tool action asks for a hand, or only for the tool somewhere on you.

~~33. **Unsafe water** (Michael, 2026-10-19, Natural water is unsafe until purified; mana; drain heals never replace food; a region gates its spells: "all natural water (water tiles, wells) is not safe until purified"). How it is purified (*Primary's reading:* boiling at a fire, or Faith's Sanctify on carried water); what unsafe water does to you; whether the waterskin's sip by itself (2026-10-16) skips it; whether watering crops needs clean water (*Primary's lean:* no); whether the trough and the font count as natural water; how a skin shows clean or unsafe. The build is #279.~~ (answered in part (2026-10-19, Unsafe water: a sickness that may come and passes; the skin sips only clean water; crops take any water); the rest is 34)

34. ~~**Unsafe water's remainder**~~ (answered 2026-10-25, Water ruled; `docs/44`) (from 33, answered in part 2026-10-19, Unsafe water: a sickness that may come and passes; the skin sips only clean water; crops take any water): how water is purified (*Primary's reading:* boiling at a fire, or Faith's Sanctify on carried water); whether the trough and the font count as natural water; how a skin shows clean or unsafe; the sickness's numbers; whether the sickness has a moodle of its own, and its name, since "Sick" is already the infection moodle's third stage (*Primary's reading* (2026-10-19, Debuffs apart from moodles; only pemmican stacks; hearty food carries buffs; the belt knife is the Dirk): a debuff, not a moodle, which would settle the name clash if Michael agrees); whether water's infection chance follows the one dial that sets combat difficulty and infection (2026-10-12). The build is #279.

35. **Potions** (Michael, 2026-10-25, The evening's rulings): which herbs, and how many, go into each potion, and what making one takes (vials, water). A sitting of its own.

36. **Herbalism 6 and 7's replacements** (struck 2026-10-25, The evening's rulings), and what the herb bed (Farming 7) grows without cuttings and seeds.

37. **The belt** (#467; pinned 2026-10-25, The evening's rulings): the hand choice kept per kind of item in a slot, Equip to a hand also drawing, an empty off-hand slot taking the off hand's item, the two Equip spellings. A discussion of its own before the sittings.
